# ClimbMatch — current state

What each area of the app does **now**, where its code lives, and what changed recently.
Architecture, commands and working rules are in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md); this file does not repeat them.

## Area docs

Each doc covers: what the area does today · how it works (data flow) · files and key symbols ·
database tables · connections to other areas · the guards that protect it · known gaps.

| Area | Doc | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| Partners | [docs/features/partners.md](docs/features/partners.md) | Discover tab: partner search (zip + radius), filters, availability, match %, crews/guides modes |
| Conditions | [docs/features/conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md) | Route page "Conditions" tab: crag score, alpine flags + start time, "Your conditions" |
| Route page | [docs/features/route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md) | `RouteDetail.jsx`: loading a route, sub-tabs, stars/ratings, crowd counts, contributions, photos, Planner |
| Maps & area browser | [docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md) | Climbs tab: area tree, area map, place search, filters, search normalisation, route map |
| Profile & settings | [docs/features/profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md) | Profile, résumé, verification, photos, units/date/visibility/notification settings |
| Route quality control | [docs/features/route-quality-control.md](docs/features/route-quality-control.md) | Held edits and owner review, per-field confidence and locks, strikes and contribution standing, flags, notices |
| Logbook, crews & Home | [docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md) | Objectives, logged climbs, crews and their Ready state, messaging, the Home dashboard |
| App shell & layout | [docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md) | The app column, the primary nav (bottom bar on phones, left sidebar on desktop), the boot placeholder |
| Groups | [docs/features/groups.md](docs/features/groups.md) | Groups screen: who may change privacy and host events, Discover search, explore by state |
| Ranks | [docs/features/ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md) | Leaderboards tab: scopes, climbing types, boards, how each metric is scored (`leaderboard()`), the table, your standing |

Not yet documented here (add a doc when you next work in one): moderation/safety,
the catalog import pipeline (see [docs/BACKEND.md](docs/BACKEND.md)).

## Recent changes

- **Full trip time walks the trip plan's gain, as the leg card prints it** (owner 2026-11-23, review of the fix below): the leg card's Gain fell back to the trip plan's day gains while the walk read only `gain_ft`, so 68 routes printed "↑ 4,600 ft" on TRAILHEAD TO SUMMIT and timed the approach flat (Mount Ballard's East Face: 1.5 hr, now 4.5), and the *Lower bounds only* note said "no recorded approach distance" on 111 routes whose trip-plan miles the walk had used. `planTimes()` reads both from the same place now (`walkKm`, `walkGainM`). See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Route text stops naming or hedging on its sources** (owner 2026-11-23, "keep going"): 47 lines naming a source (Climber Kyle, "per Mazamas", WTA, Mountain Project, the AAJ, trip reporters) were rewritten in our own words on 25 routes, so Washington's citation audit reads 23 (all allowed: first-ascent credits, clubs running trips, guidebooks to carry). Sixteen hedges that named nobody ("one report logged 15 hours", "Sources differ", "guidebooks list 8-9") are rewritten as plain statements for 14 rows in five states, dry-run clean, applied by the owner with `prose-hedges-2026-11-23/apply.mjs --apply`. The all-states audit no longer counts Whitney's Mountaineers Route as a source. Records in `scripts/oneoff/prose-citations-2026-11-23/` and `scripts/oneoff/prose-hedges-2026-11-23/`.
- **A wall's walk-in shows its crag's first** (owner 2026-11-23, "fix the gap"): when a wall has its own walk-in and the crag above has one too, the WALK-IN card reads *To Calendar Butte*, the crag's walk, then *Then to Nomad Buttress*, the wall's last leg, instead of the wall's leg alone. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Private reports now feed the rappel answers, the road status and summit counts too, and a corrected sun vote reaches the route** (owner 2026-11-23, "do all" on the log-form audit's open items): `route_private_conditions` returns a private report's rappel count, longest rappel, rope and summit flag (0508; never its road note or other words), and the Plan tab's RAPPELS and the road status line, and the Reports tab's ROAD CONDITIONS, read them anonymously. Editing a log re-files its sun vote when the vote or note changed, and the route keeps each climber's newest. The 6 live logs stored as `alpine` on Class 2-3 mountaineering routes were corrected. The test fixture (`scripts/lib/ui-fixture.mjs`) now deletes a throwaway account's contributions on teardown, as the in-app delete does. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Full trip time times every leg the leg cards time** (owner 2026-11-23: "sometimes it doesn't show the approach time or have the time for other legs when it shows it in other places"): its tiles decided N/A from the raw distance, gain and pitch columns while the hours walk the trip plan's day miles and a pitch count estimated from the length, so 14 Approach and 31 Climbing tiles read N/A, and on 28 routes (Slipstream, the Kain Route) Climbing, Descent and Total all did, beside a timed TRAILHEAD TO SUMMIT card. `planTimes()` now says which legs it timed (`approachTimed`/`climbTimed`/`descentTimed`/`anyTimed`) and Full trip time, `SummitLegsCard` and `DescentLegStrip` all read it; an estimated leg reads "≥", a leg card no longer prints "≥0.0hr". The multi-day summit day now gives its hours (camp to summit, back), not only clocks (467 routes), and a party over two names the time it adds to the Total. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **A wall shows its crag's walk-in and parking** (2026-11-23, from the Review desk flag on Fred, "It needs to be better lol"): a climb filed on a wall with no walk-in or parking of its own now shows the nearest crag's above it, and says whose it is (*The walk in to Limbo Area, shared by every climb there.*). 2,697 climbs on 344 walls had shown no walk-in their crag held. Only a crag passes facts down, never a region; the wall's own facts always win. The flag itself was fixed in the data: Nomad Buttress has its own walk in, Fred its start, and Calendar Butte's parking says a Discover Pass is required. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Partners, Profile, Logbook: no box inside a box** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). A partner card's SHARED OBJECTIVES was a blue-outlined box in the card: it is a section between two hairlines. The Profile's SPEED PROFILE readings lose their filled cells (the rate keeps a green rail). In the Logbook, MY OBJECTIVES was a glowing blue card wrapped around every objective card: the wrapper is gone and each objective is its own card. Every button and count is unchanged.
- **ROPEWORK's confidence chip says how sure its ROWS are** (owner 2026-11-23, "not sure why ropework is locked at high confidence if it's wrong"): the heading wore the rope note's level alone, so a note locked by the gear check read *High · locked* over a Simul-climbing line quoted from an unchecked description. It now wears the weakest level of the fields the rows came from (`ropeworkLevel` in `lib/ropework.js`). The citation audit also learned Climber Kyle and the Mazamas as a source (`audit:prose-citations`, and through it `audit:misplaced-prose`); the 46 Washington rewrites it found are in `scripts/oneoff/prose-citations-2026-11-23/`. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Route page: no box inside a box** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). On the Conditions tab the Temp / Rain / Wind tiles in each day row, the bordered chart cards inside HOUR BY HOUR, the boxed readings above each chart and the filled cells of the alpine Conditions and Day by day cards are now plain columns: a thin rule on the left, a reading's colour rail kept. On Overview the ROUTE FACTS tiles and the ROUTE BREAKDOWN rows lose their boxes (a breakdown row keeps its coloured rail). No value, label or tap target changed.
- **A partner confirmation counts at once** (owner 2026-11-23): the partner who confirms your climb no longer needs an account at least a month old. A friend you tagged, with a confirmed email, is enough (still up to 12 of your climbs a year per partner), and climbs confirmed by newer accounts before this now count too. Migration 0507; the log form and Help say so. See [ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md).
- **Your conditions: alpine and mountaineering are separate, and the panel is in two sections** (owner 2026-11-23): *Alpine* and *Mountaineering & scrambling* each keep their own temperature range (a range saved before, when they were one, stays on both until one is changed). The scope chips are two labelled groups, *Crag climbing · sets the conditions score* and *Mountain climbing · adds one temperature line*; the two chips *Not set* / *My range* became one plain action, *Set a range for …* or *Remove my … range*; wind, after rain and the optional extras sit under a *Crag score: wind, rain and more* heading that says they change only crag routes (`lib/CondPrefs.jsx`). After review: a crag route's score card shows only the crag chips, and the carry-over is a tested pure function (`lib/condPrefsSplit.js`). See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Crew card: no box inside a box** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). The crew card held a blue-outlined next-step panel, amber-outlined join requests inside it, a filled box around each request's note, and seven outlined step rows. They are now sections and rows of the one card under hairlines; a request's note sits beside a quote rule, and the current step is marked by a blue number. Accept, Decline and the step buttons keep their outlines and handlers.
- **Groups: organizers can make a group their own** (owner 2026-11-23, "do all", migration 0506). The group form's new *Make it yours* section sets a colour (tints the group's band, initial, and a strip above its name), an announcement banner under the name, a welcome message members see at the top of Discussion, up to 4 links on About (https only), and what to call moderators on Members (in place of MOD). The new text is screened like the name and About text. The database now refuses an RSVP or invite to an event more than a day past, a day later than the app stops offering them. `src/components/GroupCustomizeFields.tsx`, `lib/groupRules.js`, `lib/db.js`. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **Logging a climb: your own reports load whole again, and four leaks in the loop back to the route page fixed** (owner 2026-11-23, audit of the log form per discipline): since 0501 (#3736) a signed-in climber's own logs loaded with only date, stars and style, so the write-up, conditions, times, camps and beta vanished from Completed, the author's own route page showed none of their conditions (the road read *No one has reported the road yet* after they had), and *Edit* would have saved the blanks back (no live log was edited in the window). Own logs read through `useMyLogs`, another climber's Activity through `useClimberReports`. Also: every log on a route not yet loaded was stored as discipline `alpine` (6 of 14 live logs); a sun vote on a report kept to friends, a crew or Just me showed under the climber's name with their note, and now shows as *A climber* without it; and private reports' times and camps now count on the Plan tab's time estimate and *Slept here* lines, as the form promised. Walked end to end on ten disciplines with a throwaway account (logged, read back as a friend and signed out). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md) and [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Added climbs and areas answer to the same rules as edits** (owner 2026-11-23, "How are we doing quality control on people that add a climb or area/crag?", migration 0505): a restricted or banned contributor can no longer propose a climb or an area, proposals are capped at 5 a day (2 for an account under a week old), and turning one down needs a reason the climber is shown and is a strike unless the owner ticks *Honest mistake*. Each proposal card lists the catalog rows it may duplicate (`catalog_find_route` / `catalog_find_area`), the Review desk has a *Proposed climbs & areas* tab, and the nightly AI checker now judges proposals too. Add a climb and Add an area show the climber's standing. See [route-quality-control.md](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Home: no box inside a box** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). Home had two: on the website each setup suggestion was a bordered tile inside the bordered FINISH SETTING UP card, and in RECENT FRIEND ACTIVITY the climb line was a bordered box inside each friend's card. The tiles lose their own border and fill; the climb line is a plain row of the card under a hairline. Every tap target and label is unchanged.
- **Profile: vouches received and given are buttons that open their own pages** (owner 2026-11-23): the Profile tab's Vouches card has one button for each, with its count; each opens a full page with ← Back listing every vouch (Edit and Revoke on the ones you gave), replacing the inline preview and its See all sheet. `src/components/MeTab.tsx`. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **Desktop Home: the greeting sits on the contour texture** (2026-11-23, owner asked). On a phone Home's top banner already wore the sign-in glow and contour lines; on the website the greeting was plain text. In the wide layout the greeting block in `src/components/TodayTab.tsx` now draws `HOME_GLOW` behind it. The phone is unchanged.
- **Groups: events and members polish, and every state on Discover** (owner 2026-11-23). An event card's *N spots left* badge sits beside *N going* instead of hanging off the card's top edge. From the day after an event's date its sheet says *This event has ended* and offers no RSVP, + Invite or Share. *+ Create event* leads the Events tab, centred and wide. Members shows the 7 rows that fill a phone screen, then *Show N more members* unfolds the rest in place (*Search members* by the heading opens the search page). Discover's state dropdown lists every US state, not just the ones with a group. `src/components/GroupEventsTab.tsx`, `GroupMembersTab.tsx`, `GroupsTab.tsx`. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **ROPEWORK says simul-climbing wherever the route says it** (owner 2026-11-23): the card's *Simul-climbing* row read only the rope note, beta, descent and pitch notes, so 151 routes that said it in their description, climbing route, trip plan, tips or partner requirements read *Not stated* (7 of them under a locked heading — the lock is the rope note's gear check, not the derived row). It now reads all of the route's text, prefers a whole sentence, and ignores simul-rappels and simul-solos: 279 of the 284 routes that mention it show it (was 133). `lib/ropework.js`; see `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **One partner confirms a climb** (owner 2026-11-23): a climb you log counts as partner confirmed on Ranks once ONE partner you tagged confirms it; it used to wait until two different partners had confirmed your climbs. The other conditions stay (a friend, a month-old account with a confirmed email, up to 12 of your climbs a year per partner). Migration 0504; the log form and Help say so. See [ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md).
- **Other grade scales are a setting, off by default** (owner 2026-11-23: "don’t show the French grade unless they have that in their settings"): the route page’s *About 5c French · VI UIAA · 17 Ewbank* line now prints only the scales switched on under Settings › Display › *Also show grades in* (French, UIAA, Ewbank, Font). Stored on the device. `lib/grade-scales-pref.js`, `src/components/TechStats.tsx`, `src/components/SettingsDataSheet.tsx`; see `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Area page: Area facts and Getting there redrawn** (owner 2026-11-23: "make the area facts look nicer. Also update the ui of getting there"): each area fact is a tile with an icon (a long value, like a parking lot described in a sentence, takes the whole row), and Season / Most logged sit in their own inset blocks with the months named on the right. Getting there reads as two legs on a rail, *Drive & park* then *Walk in* (the walk-in time as a chip), with the directions in their own panel. Same facts, same gates, same one drive control. New `car`, `walk`, `clock`, `rock` and `nav` icons in `ActionIcon`. `lib/AreaPageSections.jsx`, `src/components/AreaGettingThere.tsx`; see `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Résumé: no email-verified chip, no Unique count** (owner 2026-11-23, "That should only be on profile not resume"): the résumé header drops the ✓ Email verified / Unverified chip (the profile still shows it), and the stat row drops *Unique*, leaving Sport · Trad · Boulder · Climbs on one row. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **Climb rows show the climb's length** (owner 2026-11-23): searching for climbs, an area's climb list and the route finder now write the length after the pitches (*Trad · 5 pitches · 600 ft*, metres when the climber chose metric); a climb with no stored length shows none. `src/components/RouteRow.tsx`, `lib/db.js` (`length_m` on the thin list row). See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Conditions: Washington's open glacier calls researched again, the legacy ids reviewed, and a misfiled hazard list cleared** (owner 2026-11-23, "keep going"): 7 more Washington routes settled from a page read (glacier not settled now 34). Four thin "no glacier" answers were held at unsure. The four `adams_` / `rainier_` routes are glacier travel. Kagevah Peak's Northeast Couloir (Wyoming) lost a hazard list copied from Colchuck Peak's Northeast Couloir in Washington. Data only. See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Conditions: glacier and snow warnings reviewed everywhere else that has them** (owner 2026-11-23, "do reserach"): every remaining state and province with alpine routes that carry snow warnings now holds the two reviewed facts (393 routes in 24 places). The 16 that hold no glacier at all (New England, New York, Quebec, Ontario, the Maritimes, the upper Midwest, North Carolina, New Jersey, Utah, New Mexico, Arizona) lose every snow-bridge line: 149 routes, all glacier no. In British Columbia, Alberta, the Yukon, the Northwest Territories, Nunavut, Idaho, Nevada and Newfoundland, 116 routes are glacier travel, 77 are not, and 51 stay unsettled with the careful wording. Data only. See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Save offline in a private window says why the map and photos didn't save** (owner 2026-11-23): Safari's Private Browsing refuses to store images, so a save there keeps the page only. It used to keep downloading every map tile and then tell the climber to retry "with a better signal"; it now stops at the first refused write (about a second) and says a private browsing window doesn't store the map or photos. `lib/offlineTiles.js`, `lib/offline.js`, `lib/SaveMapOffline.jsx`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Crews and partners: the last of the review** (2026-11-23, migration 0502). The four partner searches snap a climber’s zip centroid to a 0.1° grid before taking any distance, so searching from several points can no longer read off a zip. Proposing *today* for a crew, and a crew’s share link on the day of the climb, no longer fail from late afternoon US time (the database day is UTC). A profile opened from a route’s Find tab forms the crew on that route. A partner list that stops at its limit (50, or 24 for Everyone) says there may be more. The Crews screen’s *Show N more requests* is a full-height tap target. `lib/PartnerSearch.jsx`, `src/components/FullProfile.tsx`, `lib/CrewCard.jsx`. See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **One selected look, and the last of the old headings** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). Seven selectors filled the action orange when selected (Logbook's discipline filter, the Inbox tabs, the sign-in and route-finder mode switches, and three chip rows); they now take the app's one selected look, a cool fill with a blue outline, so orange is left to the action a screen wants you to take. And 62 section headings that were inline copies of the old style (capitals beside a blue bar) in 30 files now draw in the display face with no bar, like the rest.
- **The app has its own colours: warm stone, an orange brand, a quieter blue** (2026-11-23, owner: the look was too close to other AI-built apps). The palette `C` (`ClimbMatchCore.jsx`) was GitHub's dark theme hex for hex; its backgrounds, borders and three text levels are now warm stone, and the three text levels are visibly different. Solid buttons, section rails and progress bars are a deep orange that keeps white text readable (`C.blueSolid`), the active tab and the wordmark's second half are the brighter brand orange (`C.brand`, new), links, selection outlines and Water pins are a mid blue (`C.blue`), and the selected or secondary fill is a cool tint (`C.blueBg`) so a control selected by fill alone still reads. Bouldering moved from orange to sand so it does not read as the brand. The boot shell, splash, sign-in, manifest, favicons and PNG icons follow. Text inputs and dropdowns now use the app font (they drew in Arial). The token names keep the word blue for now; the shared Button component renames them. `check:blue-on-blue` and `blueOnBlue()` were retuned, because at their old thresholds they could no longer see the new blue or the new tint. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Section headings: one look, in the display face, and no coloured bar by default** (2026-11-23, owner: the new look). The four places that draw a section heading (`SL` in `ClimbMatchCore.jsx`, `CardHead`, `Head`, and Home's own) now draw it in Barlow Condensed capitals. The coloured bar beside every title is gone; `CardHead` draws one only where a card passes `tone` (KNOWN HAZARDS in red), so a bar means something. Titles keep their exact text, the confidence chip, the Flag button and the Edit action.
- **Two named typefaces: names in a display face, numbers in a number face** (2026-11-23, owner: part of the new look). A screen's title and a route's name draw in Barlow Condensed; a grade stamp and the figures in the route's stat strip draw in IBM Plex Mono. Everything else stays in the system font. The stacks are spelled once, in `lib/fonts.js` (`FONT_DISPLAY`, `FONT_MONO`); the six font files are in `public/fonts/` with their licences, declared in `index.html`, and `public/sw.js` precaches them so they work with no signal. The display face is for 20px and above only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **A grade is a neutral stamp, and a discipline's colour is in its icon only** (2026-11-23, owner: the colour budget for the new look). The route header draws the grade and the protection rating as square-cornered stamps (`Stamp`, `src/components/Stamp.tsx`) in the plain text colour; an R or X rating keeps red. The discipline pill beside it and every `DiscBadge` are neutral, with the discipline colour in the icon. The Grade figure in the route's stat strip is plain text colour. Amber now means caution, and orange is the brand's. Charts and map pins keep their discipline and data colours.
- **Partners: four screens that said something wrong** (review 2026-11-23, no migration). *I’m free this weekend* now shows, and sorts first, in every partner list, not only Near me and browse (`usePartnerFreeUntil`). Signed in, the finder no longer prints *0 climbers found* and *No climbers match these filters* under a list that has results. An area page’s *Partners here* rows finish scoring instead of reading *Scoring match…*, and its count no longer adds one for your own listing. A climber scores the same match % on a route’s Find tab as in Partners (the tab now reads their listed areas). `lib/PartnerSearch.jsx`, `src/components/PartnersHereCard.tsx`, `src/components/RoutePartnersTab.tsx`. See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **Contour lines behind the headers, the splash and sign-in** (2026-11-23, owner: "i liked the topo look"). `public/topo.svg` is a small drawing of contour rings, every fifth an index line in the brand orange. It sits in the top-right corner of every screen header (`TOPO` in `lib/ScreenHero.jsx`, layered into `HERO_BG` and `HOME_GLOW`), the Me card, a climber's profile header, the loading splash and the sign-in screen. It is decoration and describes no real place; it is a background layer only, never inside a map or a chart. The service worker precaches it. `scripts/oneoff/gen-topo.mjs` regenerates it.
- **Challenges count this year, per discipline; a partial multipitch climb counts its pitches** (owner 2026-11-23: "If they climb a 5 pitch route, does that count as 5 pitches? It should."). A finished 5-pitch route already counted 5; now an Attempt, Turned around, Fell or Hung log of a 2+ pitch route asks *Pitches climbed* (0500 `climb_logs.pitches_climbed`), so three of five before turning back counts 3, not 0. Logbook › Challenges is now *Your challenges · 2026* (starts again on January 1; badges stay all-time), in groups, each row showing its next tier: climbs, pitches 100–1,000, days out; different climbs and a row per discipline you climb (sport, trad, top rope, problems 50/100/250, ice or mixed, summits, multipitch); a new hardest grade and a grade pyramid per graded discipline; crags or areas, states, partners. New lifetime badge ladders on the same counts (`challengeStats`/`challengeKeys` in core). See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Crews: eight things that did not work as shown** (review 2026-11-23, migration 0499). *Remove limit · stop listing* no longer closes the crew to everyone (a size of 0 is no limit in the database too). *Remove* on a member of a real crew removes them, for the organizer; it had only recorded a vote on your own device. A request to join survives a reload (`useMyCrewRequests`), so *Requested* and its undo come back. A crew its organizer disbanded leaves your screen without a reload. The crew’s note for climbers browsing it is stored (`crews.open_note`) and shown in Join a crew, and a session’s *line for the crew* fills it. A listed crew stays in Join a crew while any proposed day is still ahead. A crew that fails to save takes its card back. One-tap *List me* on area pages no longer blanks a stored listing (`addPartnerArea`). Also: forming a crew with a climber is invite-only by default, and an area page’s *Crews here* stops counting crews whose day has passed. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **A climb saved offline opens its whole page with no service, from Logbook › Saved › Offline** (owner 2026-11-19): the route page's button reads *Save offline* and keeps the page, its map, photos (newest 40, plus those on its trip reports), topos, climbers' tracks, the crag's walk-in line and the names of the climbs beside it; never the forecast. Logbook › Saved has an *Offline* chip (and a card under All) listing each saved climb: tap to open, *Update* to save again, swipe to remove. A save whose map cannot download keeps the page and says the map is not saved, instead of deleting the whole save. With no connection the route page says *No service. This is the copy saved on this device N ago.* and the Conditions tab shows one card saying the forecast needs a connection. A whole-state download still saves no images. `check:offline-claims` §11 (40 assertions) proves each part is written and read back; `scripts/oneoff/probe-offline-saved-climb.mjs` drives it on a 390px phone with the network cut (47 checks). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md), [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md), [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **One set of words for when you are free** (owner 2026-11-23, "make it consistent"): a partner card read *Usually free: Weekends · Weekday PM* beside a profile showing *Weekday evenings* and *Weekend mornings*. The card now lists the same chips the profile lights (`availSlotLabels()`: *Weekday mornings / Weekday evenings / Weekend mornings / Weekend evenings*, all four for a climber free any time), the old labels the *How you match* line used say *Weekday mornings / evenings* instead of *AM / PM*, and the Help answer uses the same words. `ClimbMatchCore.jsx`, `src/components/RealClimberRow.tsx`, `lib/Help.jsx`; `check:match-percent` holds it.
- **Signed out, a trip report no longer says who the climber went with (0501)** (owner 2026-11-23: "dont have names for signed out"): Everyone-visible trip reports still read signed out, but `climb_logs.partners` (the account ids of the people a climb was logged with) is no longer readable with the public key: anon's table-level SELECT is revoked and every other column granted back by name, as 0435 did for profiles. The app never drew partners for anyone but the climber; the résumé's read of another climber's logs (`useUserLogs`) now names its four columns instead of `*`. A migration that ADDS a climb_logs column must grant it to anon, or guests' trip reports fail; `check:profile-privacy` fails when one is missing. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **The elevation profile prints no Gain and Loss for a track that starts away from the trailhead** (2026-11-23): a recorded track starting more than 2 km from the route's trailhead pin is a piece of the route (Rainier's Ingraham Direct stores the top 3.8 km), so the profile says *Track starts away from the trailhead* where the totals would be. The profile itself still draws and scrubs. `check:profile-gain` pins it. Data, same day: two tracks stored summit to trailhead were reversed (Lundin and Cutthroat West Ridge), a 1 km fragment was cleared (Goode Southwest Couloir), and seven tracks that were another, bigger outing over other peaks were cleared (Buck Mountain, Helmet Butte, Dot Mountain, Oval Peak, Sentinel Peak, Hurry-Up Peak, Mount Formidable); those eight routes show their pins alone. Four routes' tracks were moved into the way in they were recorded on (`trip.gpxPts`/`trip.elevPts`, as Mount Logan's Thunder Creek way in already stored one): North Star East Route (Phelps Creek, its default, which used to show no track), Switchback Mountain (Crater Creek), Cannon Mountain (Stuart Lake) and Blackcap Mountain (Monument Creek), whose recordings had been drawn among another way in's pins 4.7-14 km away. Four tracks that stop 520-660 ft below the summit were cleared under the owner's reached-the-summit rule (Mount Watson, Pinnacle Peak in the Tatoosh, Chianti Spire, Warrior Peak; each ended inside the 300 m reach of its pin), and two tracks whose stored elevations were not the terrain had them rebuilt from the USGS ground at every point (Argonaut NE Couloir, which read 3,160 ft under the ground, and Johannesburg's NE Rib). Mount Cameron's distance and gain were researched and locked in the ledger (`research-data/route-ledger-2026-11-23/cameron-batch.json`): `dist_km` 5.3 -> 19.3 (the stored figure was shorter than the straight line to the summit) and `gain_ft` 3,170 -> 5,095, each from a published trailhead-to-summit log agreeing with the route's own track within 6%. A census of the same impossibility (a stored one-way `dist_km` shorter than the straight line from the route's own trailhead pin to its summit pin) found 15 more WA routes; a research batch (`research-data/route-ledger-2026-11-23/distance-batch.json`) locked Mount Fernow (8 -> 12.2 km, gain 8,100 -> 7,124 ft) and Mount Adams North Ridge's gain (7,691 ft as stored). The other 13 found no published trailhead-to-summit figure the route's track or pins agree with; on 12 of them the impossible `dist_km` was cleared (owner, 2026-11-23: "clear them"; `scripts/oneoff/clear-impossible-dist-km.mjs`), so the page shows no distance, or the trip plan's own total where one exists. Big Four's Spindrift Couloir keeps its 1.6 km (the walk to the couloir). Adams North Ridge's 5.6 km, disputed in the ledger (published 16.1 km vs its track's 13.5 km), was cleared the same way on a second run.
- **Conditions: glacier and snow warnings reviewed in six more states** (owner 2026-11-23, "keep going"): Oregon, California, Colorado, Wyoming, Montana and Alaska now carry the two reviewed facts Washington got (`routes.glacier_travel`, `routes.snow_in_season`). Of 775 routes that carried the snow logic or named a glacier: 160 are glacier travel (the *Glacier & snow* card, bridges stated), 488 have no glacier (a *Snow climb* card with no bridge wording, or no snow warnings at all for the 96 reviewed dry in season), and 127 are not settled and keep the careful "any snow bridges" wording. Data only; no code path changed. See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **An approved wall order shows at once in the owner's open app tabs, and the Review desk's first tab has a count** (2026-11-23): approving on the desk used to leave an open ClimbMatch tab on the old order for up to 15 minutes, even after a reload; the desk now tells the browser's other tabs to re-read that list. The *Edits, flags & wall orders* tab shows how many are waiting, as the other two tabs do. Nothing was added to the Menu or its badge. `lib/queryClientRef.js`, `lib/db.js`, `review/ReviewDesk.jsx`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **A partner card's *Usually free* shows the schedule the profile shows** (owner 2026-11-23, after a live check): a test account's card read *Usually free: Weekends* while its profile showed weekday evenings and weekend mornings, because the card and the match % read the stored tags and the profile reads the week grid. `availOf()` now reads the grid when a climber has one (through `availTagsOf()`, as every save does) and the tags only when they do not, so the card, its *Overlaps yours* badge, the match % and the profile agree. `ClimbMatchCore.jsx`, `src/components/RealClimberRow.tsx`; `check:match-percent` holds it. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Route and area listings: fixes from the review of the Find tab** (2026-11-23). *List me for this route* no longer shows while your own listing is still loading, and the tap can no longer blank a stored note and dates (`addRouteListing` inserts without overwriting). A climber at the 30-listing cap can edit a listing again, on routes and areas. A listing note that matches the screening floor is refused at save. A window ending today stays visible to others through the evening. The finder says *listed* where it said *saved*, a guest is told when the crews read failed, your own passed dates read *Your dates ended*, and a crag session no longer shows under an alpine route’s Current crews. Migration 0497; `src/components/ListMeOnRouteBox.tsx`, `lib/PartnerSearch.jsx`. See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **Conditions: the height the snow is assumed to start at is measured from the trailhead** (2026-11-23): with no high camp the card took half the route's stored gain off the high point, and that gain counts every up-and-down of the approach, so Jack Mountain North Ridge read *No overnight freeze at 1,643 ft* under a 9,066 ft summit. With a trailhead pin it is now halfway between the pin and the high point, and the card says so. `snowFloorFt` in `lib/alpineConditions.js`. See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Three crew and partner safety holes closed** (review 2026-11-23, migration 0498). A climber could approve their own request to join a listed crew by rewriting who invited them; a membership row’s `invited_by`, crew and member are now fixed once it exists (`crew_members_lock_identity`). A climber you blocked could still ask to join your crew; the request is refused, and one made before the block no longer reaches Requests or the bell. A climber you blocked no longer shows on an area page’s *Partners here* card or in its count (`partner_area_counts`, `src/components/PartnersHereCard.tsx`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Accessibility reports and Trails & GPS tracks moved off the Menu onto the Review desk** (owner 2026-11-23: accessibility reports shouldn't have their own box on the Menu, more hidden but not removed; trails and tracks with them). Both are now sections of `review.html` beside the edits table (`review.html#access`, `review.html#trails`), each rendered by the same queue component, with its waiting count on the tab. The Menu's admin Review section loses the two tiles, and its badge no longer counts them. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md) and [route-quality-control.md](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Signed out, nobody can list uploaded photos or read a crew's notes and gear (0496)** (owner 2026-11-23: "I don't want peoples data getting leaked … if you aren't signed in"): a probe with the public key found everything private already closed (profiles, messages, crews, zips, emergency contacts), but the `topo-photos` bucket could be LISTED, profile photos included, and a listed crew's `crew_members` rows (note, gear, invited/pending) were public. Images still load by their public link; the storage read rule now answers only the file's owner and admins, and a crew's rows are read only by its organiser and the people on it (Join a crew still lists it through `crew_listings`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Filling a crew: an email invite counts toward the spots left** (2026-11-23): the *Inviting to <crew> · N spots left* bar now subtracts unclaimed email invites, as the crew sheet's friends list does, so it no longer offers a spot the database then refuses. The signed-in probe gained `--send` (one invite, to a declared QA test account only, read back and removed) and `--rock` (grade labels on real cards); both pass. `lib/PartnerSearch.jsx`, `scripts/oneoff/probe-crew-invite-mode-signed-in.mjs`.
- **Partners here: *List me here* is a full-width button on its own line** (owner 2026-11-23): on an area page's Partners here card it left the header's right edge and now leads the card's action section, above *Start a crew here* / *Crews here*, with its Save-vs-list line and *Add dates or climbing types* under it (`src/components/PartnersHereCard.tsx`).
- **Filling a crew, checked signed in** (2026-11-23, a read-only walk on real accounts): invite mode now turns on for an **area** crew (the crew is handed to the finder with its place; the finder could not resolve a real area by itself), and only for the crew's **organiser**, the one person who can invite. The near-the-climb list waits behind the zip box like every other real list; the default distance while filling a crew is Anywhere, so a climber who saved the climb is listed as wanting it wherever they live; a card carries one invite control, not two; a climber you only invited reads *Invited*, not *On crew*; and the example list's *0 climbers found* no longer sits under a climber the near list found. `scripts/oneoff/probe-crew-invite-mode-signed-in.mjs`.
- **Suggested wall orders arrive on the Review desk** (owner 2026-11-23: "I receive it in the admin like what would happen when people suggest edits"): a climber's left-to-right order for a wall is an *Order* row in `review.html`'s table, beside held route edits and flags, with *Use this order* / *Keep current order*. The Menu's *Wall order suggestions* tile and queue are gone. A wall has one order for everyone; approving rewrites it. `review/ReviewDesk.jsx`, `lib/db.js`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **A route's Partners sub-tab is now *Find*: list yourself on any route, see Current crews, start one** (owner 2026-11-23). The label changed (the id is still `partners`). On a route that is not a crag route, a signed-in climber has a *List me for this route* box (`ListMeOnRouteBox`, new table `route_partner_listings`, migration 0489): one tap lists you, then optional dates and a note. Listed climbers lead the list on that page and in Partners › by climb (`partners_by_objective` returns them first with `listed`, `listing_note`, `free_from`, `free_to`, `saved`), under the same discoverable and block rules; a crag route keeps *List me at <crag>*. The tab opens on one **Current crews** section, always drawn: your own crews here, then open crews with *Request join*, then **Start a crew** (a listed crew for the route; on a crag route a session at the crag). Crag rows gain *Plan a session*. Signed in, the tab no longer shows example climbers or example crews. `src/components/RoutePartnersTab.tsx`, `src/components/ListMeOnRouteBox.tsx`, `lib/MyPartnerAreas.jsx` (*Climbs you’re listed on*); see [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md) and [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Conditions tab: glacier warnings only where there is a glacier** (owner 2026-11-23): a mountaineering route no longer reads "bridges are weak" because of its discipline. Two reviewed facts per route decide, `routes.glacier_travel` and `routes.snow_in_season` (migration 0490; yes / no / not settled, editable from the route's edit sheet under *Conditions & character*): reviewed glacier travel keeps the *Glacier & snow* card; a route with snow and no glacier gets a new *Snow climb* card whose freeze warning is worded for snow; a walk-up reviewed dry in season gets no freeze flags; a scramble that crosses a crevassed glacier now gets the glacier card. Washington is reviewed (753 routes; 298 glacier travel, 414 none, 41 not settled); other states keep their warnings, worded "any snow bridges", until `npm run audit:glacier-travel -- --state <slug>` is worked down. `lib/alpineConditions.js`, `lib/terrain.js`, `src/components/ForecastBox.tsx`. See [conditions.md](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **The fit rule reaches Join a crew and the friends picker** (owner 2026-11-23): a route crew's card on Join a crew says how its climb sits against your grade (*At or below the grade you lead*, *Above your lead grade, within what you could follow*, *Well above the grade you list*), and *Best match* puts crews in a discipline you climb ahead of ones you do not. A crew sheet's *Your friends* list runs best fit first and prints the same grade label. Asking to join and inviting a friend are never blocked. `lib/crewFit.js`.
- **Who fits a crew's climb: one rule** (owner 2026-11-23, "it has to make sense based on what they like to climb, distance away from you or the climb, and grade level"): a climber can be invited if they saved the crew's climb **or** list its discipline. While filling a crew, each card says how their grade compares (*Leads this grade* · *Can follow this grade* · *Climbs below this grade* · *No <discipline> grade listed*), the list runs best fit first with climbers below the grade folded under one toggle, and a second list finds climbers of that discipline near the **climb** (100 mi for crags, 250 mi for mountains, ice and mixed) with the distance printed *from the climb*. Find partners › A route shows that second list too. Grade never blocks an invite. `lib/crewFit.js`; see [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **Invite several climbers to a crew while browsing the partner finder** (owner 2026-11-23): a crew sheet's *Browse everyone in the partner finder* opens the finder at the crew's climb or crag with an *Inviting to <crew> · N spots left* bar. Each card gets **Select to invite** (a climber who saved the climb, or lists the crew's discipline); picks stop at the spots left; *Invite N climbers* opens one Invite to crew sheet with one shared note. After sending, the cards read *Invited* and the list stays; **Done** returns to the crew. See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **The policy-update notice no longer covers a popup's buttons** (2026-11-23): while *Our Terms and Privacy Policy have changed* is up, every popup ends at its top edge, so the last rows of a sheet (its Send or Save) stay reachable; on the website the strip beside the notice is dimmed with the popup. Found on the live wall-order sheet, where Send sat under *Read Terms*. Also: a wall-order decision refreshes the bell at once, and the review cards' note box stays inside the card. `index.html`, `ClimbMatch.jsx`, `scripts/check-bottom-panels.mjs`. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **A climber's profile header says how long they have climbed** (owner 2026-11-23): *Nyr exp* after the home area on the profile other climbers open, from the year they started; the résumé, the partner card and your own Profile tab already said it. `src/components/FullProfile.tsx`. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **Inviting to a crew keeps you where you were browsing** (owner 2026-11-23: "it doesn't go straight back to the crew page so you can keep browsing more"): *+ Invite to crew* on a partner card or a friend row opens the Invite to crew sheet (so every invite can carry a note) and stays on that list; the card then reads *Invited to <crew>* with a **View crew** button. *Add to your crew* in the Form-a-crew prompt also stays put, and now says when that crew is full. Starting a NEW crew still opens it. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md) and [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **Top contributors: ties share first, and CI test accounts are off the boards** (owner 2026-11-23): two climbers level on points both get the ★ pill, the gold medal and the profile badge (0495; the account id used to pick one), and the two CI walk accounts no longer appear on Ranks. See [ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md).
- **Join a crew: your areas are one row** (owner 2026-11-23: "one row"): under *My climbs & areas* the card of area chips is now the short row Find partners has, *You’re listed at N places.* beside **Edit areas** (*Add areas* when you have none), which opens the Listed areas sheet. The sentence also counts your saved areas, and a failed read still says so. `lib/CrewFinder.jsx`; see [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **A skill tapped on a climber's profile shows its card** (2026-11-23, found looking at the live site): the card (`SkillGuideSheet`) was drawn at z-index 9700, under the profile popup at 9800, so tapping *Crevasse rescue* on someone's Skills tab opened a card nobody could see. It is now at 9850.
- **Discover groups is searched, filtered and paged in the database** (2026-11-23, 0487): Discover read every public group and its roster into the browser and filtered there; the screen paged, the read did not. Signed in, the search box, the state chip, *Near a place* and all three sorts now run in `discover_groups()`, 20 groups a read, with the total and the chip counts from the database; your own groups are read separately. *Most active* now orders real groups (it only ever ordered the example ones), the *Other* state lists the groups it counts (it listed none), and a failed read says so instead of *No groups yet*. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **Field checklists are gone from the app** (owner 2026-11-23, "Remove field checklist from the app"): the Safety tab's card went on 2026-11-18; now the *Field checklist: …* button inside a skill's card is gone too, and so are the four checklists themselves (partner check, BRAKES rappel check, before lowering, crevasse-rescue order) in `lib/skillGuides.js`. A skill's card keeps what it is, when you need it, what goes wrong, the links out and the courses (`SkillGuideSheet`). See [route page](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **The match is said in one sentence** (owner 2026-11-23: "better match explanations"): a partner card and the profile's COMPATIBILITY WITH YOU panel now lead with what the two of you share, for example *You both climb 5.10 sport, list Index to climb, and are free Saturday and Sunday.* Up to three things, heaviest first: grade with its discipline (or the disciplines), shared mountain objectives, a shared listed area (named when both list that same area), the days you are both free, and a similar hiking pace. It is written by `compatSummary()` from the rows the % is summed from, names only what is shared, and prints nothing when nothing is. **The grade term counts every number grade as a step** (same day): 5.8 and below used to share one, so 5.6 read *Close in grade* against 5.10a; it now reads *Some grade gap* and scores lower. Grades from 5.9 to 5.12 are spaced as before, and 5.13 and up, which also shared one step (5.13a read *Same grade* as 5.14d), now count a step per letter grade too. `ClimbMatchCore.jsx`, `src/components/RealClimberRow.tsx`, `src/components/FullProfile.tsx`; `check:match-percent` holds it. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Edit profile's *How climbers will see you* shows the whole profile** (owner 2026-11-23, an audit of the card): it stopped at the grades, so availability, certifications, belay devices and skills, with the month each was last practiced, were entered above it and previewed nowhere. It now draws all of them, plus years climbing, gender and pronouns, names disciplines by label, and lists what is still empty. `lib/EditProfileScreen.jsx`. See [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **Logbook › Saved: no saved routes, areas on top, and add a place from its box** (owner 2026-11-23): the *Saved routes* card and its *Routes* chip are gone (a saved route is an objective, on the Objectives sub-tab; one line at the foot of Saved leads there), so *Saved areas* is the first box. Each of *Saved areas* and *Saved peaks* ends in *+ Add an area* / *+ Add a peak*: search the catalog and tap a hit to save it, without leaving the tab. `src/components/SavedPlaceAdd.tsx`, `src/components/LogbookTab.tsx`; see [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Forecast: the week scrolls to the right everywhere, and the key-point switch is on the phone** (owner 2026-11-23): on the website the seven day tiles of *Forecast at the crag* / *Forecast at key points* wrapped into a stack; they are one row that scrolls sideways again, as on the phone. The key points (trailhead, camp, summit…) are one line of pills on both, and tapping one shows that point's week; the phone no longer stacks a card per point. `src/components/WeatherPanel.tsx`, `lib/wideLayout.js` (`data-cm-scroll-x`). → [route page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Crew card: member avatars sit side by side** (owner 2026-11-23: "the avatars overlap"): the row beside *N of M in* no longer stacks the avatars over each other; it draws every member with a gap, three to a row (four squeezed the count and names on a phone), wrapping onto the next row. `lib/CrewCard.jsx`.
- **Groups › Discover: the State list offers every state you downloaded** (owner 2026-11-23): it was built only from states that already had a group and hidden when the chips covered them, so a climber whose groups were all at home could pick nothing but home. A downloaded state with no group yet is listed with a 0. `src/components/GroupsTab.tsx`. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **A climber can suggest the left-to-right order of a wall's climbs** (owner 2026-11-19): a wall's climb list has *Order wrong? Suggest a different order* (or *Know the left-to-right order? Add it* where none is stored). The sheet moves climbs a place at a time, by typed place number, or reverses the whole line; climbs with no place yet wait in their own list until placed, and only placed climbs are sent. It is a suggestion only: it waits for the owner's review (on the Review desk since 2026-11-23), *Use this order* there renumbers the wall, and the sender is notified either way. `src/components/WallOrderSuggest.tsx`, migration 0485. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **The profile's Gain and Loss no longer count GPS jitter on steep ground as climbing** (2026-11-23, measured over the 430 profiles the app draws): 23 routes read more than twice their whole relief (Liberty Bell's Beckey Route: 15,393 ft on 2,372 ft). The count now takes whole swings between turning points with a threshold that rises where the recording is jumpy (`lib/elevGain.js`, `check:profile-gain`): Liberty Bell reads 3,905 ft, clean recordings move by about 1%, and none reads below its own net climb. The worst recordings are halved, not cured (Colfax Cosley-Houston 20,427 → 8,715 ft). Two routes whose stored elevations were not elevations (Sherman Peak Squak Glacier, Mount Logan Banded Glacier) had them cleared.
- **Adding a topo photo, audited** (2026-11-23): a refused *Save topo* is said inside the drawing sheet (it was shown behind it, so Save looked dead) and keeps the drawing; a double tap stores one line; a caption typed over a new photo is saved with the line; a photo named with an accent, an odd space or a `#` uploads (the file name is reduced to plain characters, for every photo upload); a GIF is stored as a still; a guest is asked to sign in before choosing a photo; and a photo whose line or row is refused comes back down. Then (owner, same day: "do the rest"): a new photo can be posted with nothing drawn on it (*Post photo without a line*; the log form's *Use photo without a line*); the topo viewer has *Report this photo* and *Report this line* on other climbers' work, and tells you when your own photo or line is held or removed, with *Ask for another look*; a topo's caption is screened (0488) and a line's words — its name, variation captions, marker notes — are screened, holdable, reportable and appealable (0492, kind `topo_line`); the Guidelines name topo photos and lines as reportable. Detail in [docs/features/route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **The elevation profile is the drawn track's, or it is not shown** (2026-11-23, after a test of the profile on live data: 442 routes show it, 432 could be scrubbed): the ten whose stored elevations came from an older line than the track on the map (Mount Adams South Climb printed *Loss 36 ft · 644 samples* under an up-and-back track) showed the map alone until their data was rebuilt the same day: each recording thinned to the usual spacing with one elevation per point (`scripts/oneoff/regenerate-dense-track-profiles.mjs`; the way down checked against the ground, median 26-89 ft), so all ten have a profile again (Mount Adams: *Gain 6,716 ft · Loss 6,716 ft*).
- **Suggested climbs uses your profile grade when your logs have no grade to go on** (found testing a real account on the live site, 2026-11-23): a climber whose only alpine log is Mount Baker's North Ridge, graded on the ice scale, gave the alpine row nothing to measure from, so it listed every alpine climb on Mount Stuart, 5.10d and 5.11- included, though their profile says Alpine 5.6. A row for a discipline you have logged but with no grade on its scale now centres on the grade on your profile.
- **Tracks & trip reports have a page of their own** (owner 2026-11-19: "after many uploads, it will get crowded"): the route page keeps the three latest parties and a *See all* row in place of *Show N more*. The page sorts Latest / Fastest, filters by season, GPS track, outcome and year, loads past the 30-track read with *Load more*, and its bottom bar takes the tracks you switched on to the map. `lib/RouteTracksPage.jsx`, `lib/RouteTracksPanel.jsx`; see [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Crews: pick the meet-up pin on a map** (owner 2026-11-23): under the Meet-up sheet's *Map pin* box, *Or pick it on the map* opens a tap map on the crew's climb or crag; tap, then *Use this spot* saves the point (*Move it on the map* once one is saved). The Privacy Policy version is now 2026-11-23 for the meet-up sentence added with 0484, so existing accounts are asked to accept it once. `lib/CrewCard.jsx`, `lib/policy.js`. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Completed matches Objectives** (owner 2026-11-23, "make both consistent"): no Sections select, five sorts in the Sort select (the website table's headers keep the rest), the same inset numbered cards with no left edge line, and a card is no longer a button holding its Edit / View / Vouch buttons. Help gains *How do I remove an objective?*. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Group events: edit one, extend a series, cancel the rest of one** (owner 2026-11-19, "do all"): the host's event sheet has **Edit** (title, date, time, meeting spot, details, cap; on a series a checkbox applies everything but the date to the later events), and a *This series* card that says how many events are left and when the last is, with **Add N more** and **Cancel this and the N after it**. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **Monthly group events land on the right day** (2026-11-19): a monthly series started on the 29th, 30th or 31st skipped February and slid into the next month (Jan 31, Mar 3, Mar 31, May 1); it now clamps to the month's last day. Dates no longer pass through UTC, and the form says how many events it schedules instead of promising an edit the app does not have. See [groups.md](docs/features/groups.md).
- **An Alpine grade on your profile, and suggestions from your alpine, ice and mixed grades** (owner 2026-11-23: "do the profile grade"): the profile editor and setup now ask an alpine climber for an Alpine grade (lead and follow, 5.0 up), beside the ice and mixed grades they already asked. It shows on your profile and partner cards like the others, and *Find partners* can filter on *Leads* for alpine. *Suggested climbs* now gives a row to each of those three grades, as it did for sport, trad and bouldering, and no longer spends one of its three rows on a discipline the area has no climb of: on Mount Stuart, a climber with rock logs and Alpine 5.6 on their profile gets *West Ridge 5.6*. Stored in `climb_grades`; no database change.
- **Crews: paste a Google or Apple Maps link as the meet-up pin** (owner 2026-11-19): the Meet-up sheet's *Map pin* box reads the point out of a pasted maps link or a coordinate, saves only the coordinate (`crews.meet_lat`/`meet_lng`, 0484), and gives every crew member *Google Maps* and *Apple Maps* buttons to it. Short links are expanded by the new `resolve-map-link` edge function. The old *Exact pin* box saved nothing. `lib/meetPin.js`, `lib/CrewCard.jsx`. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Crews: backup days past the first** (owner 2026-11-19): on a crew's Day sheet each member orders the days they can make — 1st choice, backup, 2nd backup, 3rd backup… — from a *Your pick* menu, the order is saved (`crew_day_acks.rank`, 0483) so crewmates see it, and when several days work for everyone the one the crew ranked best is the agreed day. It was two chips kept on the phone. `lib/CrewCard.jsx`, `ClimbMatchCore.jsx` (`dayRankPos`, `rankAgreedDays`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Latest from this area: see more, sort, filter, and open the report itself** (owner 2026-11-19): past four reports the card gets a *See N more* dropdown (ten at a time), a sort (newest / oldest) and filters for discipline, date climbed and trip reports vs logged climbs, all answered by the database over the whole area (migration 0478, `area_latest_reports`). A row opens the trip report or logged climb over the area page, and has its own *View trip report* / *View logged climb* and *Route page* buttons. `src/components/AreaLatestReports.tsx`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Suggested climbs: no X-rated climbs, and recent logs count for more** (owner 2026-11-23: "do what you recommend" on the audit's open items): a climb rated X or R/X is never suggested (a 5.7 trad climber at Christian Brothers was offered *The Asterisk 5.7 X*), in a personal row or the fallback list; an R climb stays but is ranked down. The grade a row centres on now weights each logged climb by its age (half every two years), so a 5.6 from six years ago no longer pulls a 5.11 climber's suggestions down, and the discipline you climb lately leads. Not done: a profile-grade row for ice, mixed and alpine (the profile stores no such grade: a new feature), and trad-on-a-peak (measured: 4 of 1,110 routes on the 535 busiest peaks).
- **Suggested climbs sits under Classic climbs, and suggests climbs at your grade** (owner 2026-11-19): the dropdown moved up from the foot of the area page to directly under *Classic climbs here*. Audit of what it suggests, on live data: every row was ranked from one pool, the area's first 120 climbs by name, with no limit on grade distance (a 5.7 sport climber at Index was offered 5.10a and 5.10c; a 5.6 alpine climber on Mount Stuart 5.10d and 5.11-); slash grades ("5.10b/c") never scored; and a new account's stated grades ranked nothing. Now each discipline row reads its own pool in the climber's grade window, best-rated first; climbs outside the window are left out; a classic or well-starred climb wins a near tie; and the sport / trad / bouldering grade on your profile earns a row until you have logged that discipline. The Route finder's *Up to your grade* preset, which read the same grades from the wrong object and never fired, works. `check:suggestion-discs` runs the real ranking.
- **Replies stay when the comment above them is deleted** (owner 2026-11-23): a deleted comment still leaves no *Comment deleted* line, but the replies under it are drawn where it was instead of going with it, and the delete sheet now says *The replies under it stay.* `src/components/Comments.tsx`. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **"Wrong place?" is not asked on a state's top-level region** (owner 2026-11-19): Washington's *Northwest Region* and the 834 other regions and ranges filed directly under a state or province cannot be in the wrong place, so their page no longer offers *Tell us where … belongs*; sub-regions and crags keep it. The owner's *Wrong place claims* queue now prints where each area is filed and drops a claim as soon as it is resolved. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Crew itineraries: the whole plan shows, crewmates can edit it, and the crew is told** (owner 2026-11-19, after an audit of who can see and change a plan): a crew card draws every day in full (goal, gain, loss, hours, distance, pack, notes, schedule) instead of day titles; *Change* opens **Edit the crew's plan** on the current plan; sharing, changing or removing it posts a line in the crew chat; a crag session's plan is saved to its builder's account and listed in Saved › Itineraries. The unreachable member-drafts panel is gone. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **A new page opens at the top** (owner 2026-11-19): *Add trip report* on a climb with no write-up opens the log form at its top (it jumped ~20 sections down to the write-up box; *Edit report* on a climb that has one still lands there). Audited everywhere else and fixed: Profile / Activity, the Groups tabs, the safety views and questionnaire steps, onboarding steps, the Inbox tabs, the sign-in screen's modes, the new-list steps, a second climber or group opened in the same sheet, and a chat thread now opens at its newest message. One hook, `lib/usePaneTop.js`; `check:pinned-dismiss` now also fails a popup that opens scrolled. See [app-shell-and-layout.md](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **Join a crew: edit your areas under *My climbs & areas*** (owner 2026-11-19): that mode matches crews against the areas you listed, but showed no way to change them. It now has an *Areas you’re listed at* card: each area is a chip that opens its listing, and **Edit areas** (*Add areas* when you have none) opens the Listed areas sheet; a failed read says so instead of reading as none. After an audit the same day the card is the pane's only way in (the *Looking for partners? … My areas* card above the finder duplicated it and is gone from Join a crew; Find partners keeps its own) and it counts the saved areas that match too. It names three areas at most, then *+N more*, so a long list of listings does not push the crews off the screen. Removing a listing you opened from its chip returns you to the crew list, as Back and Save do. `lib/CrewFinder.jsx`; see [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Objectives: no ✕ on each card, count and Select on one row** (owner 2026-11-19): a climb is removed through Select, which already confirms and handles several at once; *4 climbs to go* moved onto the Select row, so the list starts higher. The token-box injection suite reads the guard's whole output again (a 1 MB capture limit had turned three real catches into misses). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Discussion: a smaller format bar with three more formats, and a deleted comment leaves nothing** (owner 2026-11-19): the format buttons over every comment box and the trip-report box are drawn 30px, down from 44 (`lib/tapSlop.js` still gives a touch 44px), and the bar gains **strikethrough**, **quote** and **link** beside bold, italic and the two lists. Stored text is still markdown (`~~struck~~`, a line starting `> `, `[words](https://…)`), read by `renderMD`. A deleted comment is no longer drawn as *Comment deleted*: it disappears (the replies under it stay, since 2026-11-23). `src/components/RichEditor.tsx`, `src/components/Comments.tsx`. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Top contributors, tested with real accounts** (2026-11-19): every board (27 areas, the route card, Ranks › Contributions overall and By area, the profile badge) matched a score recomputed from the raw rows, as four signed-in accounts and signed out. Fixed: a row on the AREA page now opens that climber’s profile (it was announced as a button and did nothing); a row with no profile to open is plain text, not a button; a contributor whose name cannot be read still shows *N pts*. See [ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md).
- **Trip plan shows its confidence once** (owner 2026-11-19): the spelled-out *Medium confidence* chip in the plan's chip row is gone; the level chip on the *Trip plan* heading stays. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **TOP CONTRIBUTORS honours "Show me on leaderboards" and blocks (0486)** (2026-11-19): a climber who turned the switch off was dropped from Ranks but stayed, by name, on every area and route card and kept the Top Contributor badge; a climber blocked by a contributor saw a nameless *Climber* row. `area_top_contributors` now leaves out the people `leaderboard()` leaves out. See [ranks.md](docs/features/ranks.md).
- **Objectives: one flat list, four sorts, clearer cards** (owner 2026-11-19): the Sections select is gone (the discipline chips and Location already do it), Sort is Newest first / Target day / Name / Grade with logged climbs always last, and each objective is its own inset card with a numbered badge. *Find partners* is white on blue. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Objectives rows are plainer** (owner 2026-11-19): no coloured line down the left of each objective (the status pill says it), no ✕ beside a set target day (clear it from the calendar), and the Status filter is gone. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Objectives audit** (owner 2026-11-19): an objective card is no longer a button with buttons inside it (the name-and-details block opens the climb; `check:axe` failed four rows on it); the *To do* pill is gone (only a logged climb is marked); *Log* is the quiet green button beside a solid *Find partners*; the card's buttons are taller. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Partners: the listing box is one short row, and Listed areas has an area finder** (owner 2026-11-18): the You card on Find partners shows the Join a crew sentence (*Looking for partners? List the crags and peaks you climb — you’re listed at N places.*) beside **My areas**, in place of a heading, a paragraph and a chip for every listed area. On the Listed areas screen the search box is a taller, outlined box under an *Add an area* heading with a clear button; **Browse areas** under it walks country, state, region and crag the way the Climbs tab does, with *+ List* on every row below a country; your own listings sit above it, the first four of a long list with *Show all N*; and *Remove this listing* sits under a rule, 57px clear of *Save changes* (`PartnerSearch`, `MyPartnerAreas`). See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md).
- **The climb picker finds downloaded areas with no signal** (2026-11-18): with the network cut and a state downloaded, the picker's first search said *Couldn't run that search*: its fallback asked the device for a whole country, which a phone never stores. It now falls back to the search over everything downloaded, and says it is showing what is on the device. `src/components/DbClimbPicker.tsx`, `lib/offline.js`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **The climb picker's search inside a country no longer times out either** (2026-11-18): tapping *United States* and then searching still ran `areas_in_subtree` over the whole country. It now uses the catalog search and keeps that country's hits, each naming the two levels above it and its state. `src/components/DbClimbPicker.tsx`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **The climb picker's area search no longer times out** (2026-11-18): in the log form, lists and partner search, the first box searched the whole United States through `areas_in_subtree`, which hit the 3 s limit on common words ("north cascades", "liberty bell", "index") and said *Couldn't run that search — check your connection*. It now asks the whole catalog once (`useCatalogAreaSearch`, 0.1–0.7 s measured as anon), each hit naming the two levels above it and its state; the old per-country search remains as the no-signal fallback. `src/components/DbClimbPicker.tsx`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **The website's Completed table fits a 1280px window** (2026-11-18): from 1280 to about 1390px the page column is narrower (561px at 1280) and the table scrolled sideways, cutting Style mid-word and hiding Stars. There the table now drops its Discipline column and shows the discipline as the icon beside the grade, as the cards do (`useNarrowColumn` in `lib/wideLayout.js`, `MyAscents`); wider windows keep all eight columns. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **A climb search hit says exactly where it is** (owner 2026-11-18): the area page's route search names each hit's wall under at most the two levels above it, then the state (*Index › Lower Town Wall › Main Wall, Washington*, not *Main Wall*), and the climb picker behind the log form, lists and partner search, which showed only a name and a grade, now adds the same line. `areaWhere` in `lib/areaBrowserShared.js`. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Your crews can be sorted, each card is numbered, and Quick view says more about the place** (owner 2026-11-18): with two or more crews the Crews tab has a *Sort* menu above the cards (*Default order*, *Soonest day*, *Ready first*, *Name (A to Z)*); the same order drives the *Quick view* rows, and each card's header carries its number top left, matching its Quick view row. A Quick view row now adds the climb's grade, discipline and pitch count (or *Sport session* for an area crew), the area it is in, and the agreed day or how many days are proposed (`CrewListView`, `CrewCard`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Partners: Experience is under More filters, and More filters is in every search mode** (owner 2026-11-18): the *Any experience* pill left the row and is an EXPERIENCE row of chips (Any / Beginner / Intermediate / Advanced / Expert) at the top of the More filters panel, counted in its badge. More filters used to be hidden under My areas and A peak; it now shows in every mode, so the experience, speed and availability filters reach those lists too. The row itself now wears the Join a crew look (the *Find a crew by* buttons: two across, squared, blue while chosen or set) and each dropdown shows one arrow, not two. See [partners](docs/features/partners.md).
- **Logbook › Objectives sorts, sections and filters the way Completed does** (owner 2026-11-18, "scroll by discipline and filter by that like we do for completed"): under the discipline chips, Sort, Sections, Location and Status are a row of selects that is always on screen (the *Sort & filter* toggle is gone), and *Sections* (no sections by default; state or discipline) groups My Objectives and every custom list. Completed starts with no sections too; the climber picks state, year or discipline after. Section headers are the ones Completed draws, and 3 or fewer sections start open (`lib/ListsManager.jsx`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **A logged climb with conditions filled in is a trip report, write-up or not** (owner 2026-11-18, found on the owner's own log): Completed asked only whether the write-up box had text, so a log with its road, trail, snow and hazard sections filled in read *View log* / *Add trip report*. One test now decides everywhere (`logHasReport`, `lib/logReport.js`: conditions, tags, times, notes, beta, photos, a plan or the write-up; a style, date, rating and party size alone are a tick): such a log reads *View & share* / *Edit report*, its form is *Edit your report* opening at the top, and its delete is *Delete trip report* (`MyAscents`, `LogAscent`, `TripReport`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Area page: Route finder, Objectives and Map are three equal, solid-blue buttons** (owner 2026-11-18, "map button is small compared to route finder and objectives ... make all 3 of those buttons stand out more"): Map was a content-width outline about a third the width of the other two, and only Route finder was filled. Each now takes a third of the row, 48px tall, in the solid blue with a white bold label; the objectives count is a badge on that button's corner so the label keeps its width (`AreaActionRow`). See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Community Consensus says each thing once** (owner 2026-11-18, "very confusing and difficult to understand"): the Reports tab's card is one summary line and one confidence, then HAZARDS, CONDITIONS NOW, MENTIONED MOST, RATING AND SEASON. Fixed with it: a hazard voted gone no longer keeps its warning on Reports while Overview clears it; the keyword scan matches whole words (*photo* read as Hot, *window* as Windy); *recent* means 365 days. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Route page: report and condition rows are named by what they show** (2026-11-18, `check:axe` red on main): the *Recent tracks & trip reports* rows (Plan) and the Reports tab's condition / hazard rows carried names that left out text the row draws (stars, outcome, season, *View*), failing WCAG 2.5.3 on 12 nodes. Each name is now the row's visible text in order. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **More genders, pronouns, and a gender filter you can tick several of** (owner 2026-11-18: "check online for other genders, pronouns … they should also be able to select multiple at a time", "they only want to climb with women and non binary"): a profile's gender now offers Genderqueer, Genderfluid, Agender and Two-Spirit beside Woman, Man and Non-binary (still one, still optional), and the profile editor has **Pronouns**, up to four from a fixed list, shown after the gender on both profile headers (migration 0475: wider `profiles.gender` check, new `profiles.pronouns text[]`, readable signed-in only). In partner search the single *Only men / women / non-binary climbers* pill is gone: **More filters** opens with **Show only**, one chip per gender, any number at once, for every signed-in climber on every mode; its badge counts each picked gender, and a climber with no stated gender is not shown while any is picked. The Privacy Policy says so (`POLICY_VERSION` 2026-11-18, so signed-in accounts see the update notice once). See [partners.md](docs/features/partners.md) and [profile-and-settings.md](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md).
- **Ranks › By area is chosen the way a climb is found** (owner 2026-11-18: "ranks by area on phone needs a better ui like how we would find a climb"): the *Ranking in* card’s **Change** now opens a Choose-an-area PAGE in place of the board, laid out as the Climbs tab is: a header naming the area with its path as links and a **See rankings for <area>** button, the countries and states as two-column cards with climb counts (A to Z), and below a state the Climbs area page’s own sub-area list (`AreaChildrenList`: Sort, discipline line, *View area*). It replaced the inline list between the scope row and the board (8 rows at a time, biggest first, one *Up to* step at a time). Walking ranks nothing; See rankings, a search hit or a *Your areas* chip does. `src/components/RankAreaPicker.tsx`, `lib/Leaderboards.jsx` (`pickId`); see `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **No more swiping sideways to change the page** (owner 2026-11-18: "Remove the swipe to go to next or previous page"): the phone gesture added on 2026-11-14 is gone everywhere it was wired, both the swipe between a page's tabs (the route page, Logbook, Profile, another climber's profile, Partners, Groups, Ranks) and the swipe between the bottom-bar sections. Tabs and sections change by tapping them. `lib/pageSwipe.js` and `check:page-swipe` are deleted; `check:back-button` keeps the phone Back-button checks and fails if the swipe hook is imported again. The browser's own sideways "back a page" drag is off as well (`touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom` on the page in `index.html`): with the swipe code gone, a right drag in Chrome left the app from Home and jumped to Home from every other section. `scripts/oneoff/probe-no-page-swipe.mjs` drags a finger across every section and the route page and reports any swipe that moves the page. Rows still swipe to archive or delete (`SwipeRow`), and the phone's Back button still steps back inside the app (`lib/backButton.js`).
- **Area facts sit directly under the description** (owner 2026-11-18, "area facts should go under description on area pages"): on a crag, peak or wall the AREA FACTS card (rock, faces, elevation, walk-in, parking, season) was below the photos and Getting there; it now follows the description in both page orders (`AreaPage`, `AreaFactsCard`). See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **A crag's Getting there map opens in 3D** (owner 2026-11-18, "only add it if it makes sense"): the map on a crag's Getting there carries the route and area maps' **3D** button, on its parking and crag pins and, where a walk in is on file, on the trail too (none is yet), with a traced line saying so in the key (`WalkInMap`'s `on3d`, `AreaGettingThere`).
