Fixed from a two-account walkthrough: the meet time now saves to `meet_time` (it was device-only, so members' crews could never be Ready); proposing a day no longer writes `agreed_date`; the meeting spot no longer drops keystrokes; members no longer get organiser-only controls; a confirmed partner tag makes a crew past (vouch + climb again) for the partner too; "Climb with them again" is the one re-link place; rail links scroll to the crew.
- **Skills: 8 more rescue/retreat/glacier skills, renames no longer orphan a profile, and a route says which of its required skills are on your profile** (owner 2026-11-14, safety-and-skills audit: "do what you recommend"): `SKILLS` adds tying off a loaded belay, hauling systems, tandem rappel, two-person crevasse rescue, retreating mid-route, stuck-rope recovery, short-roping and rope-team setup (50 skills, a new *Retreat* group). Profiles store labels, so `profileSkills()` / `skillKeyOf()` / `SKILL_ALIASES` in core resolve old spellings and the retired `rappel`. The route Partners panel adds *Your skills* under *Required skills*, matched by `lib/requiredSkills.js` ([route page](docs/features/route-page.md), [profile](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md)).
- **Objectives: no header "Log a climb"; Target day as tall as Find partners and Log** (owner 2026-11-14): the Logbook › Objectives header is just *My Objectives* now (every objective row already has its own *Log*; the picker still opens from Completed › *Log a past climb*, which `check:ui`'s picker flow now taps). Find partners, Log and the Target day box share one 16px line-height, so all three are 28px tall; Target day keeps `nowrap`, so it is as wide as its text on a phone (`lib/ListsManager.jsx`).
- **Logbook: Completed in sections, Objectives grouped by real state** (owner 2026-11-14): Completed (`lib/MyAscents.jsx`) now sections by state by default, the same look as Objectives, with a picker for year, discipline (the chip groups) or no sections; sections fold when there are more than 3, and the sort (#3322's options) and sections are remembered on the device (`cm.ascSort`, `cm.ascGroup`) and show from the first log. Objectives showed "Chilliwack / Picket" and "Monte Cristo" as if they were states: `routeLoc` read a DB area's `region`, which some areas fill with a sub-region. It now takes the state from the area's `path` (`lib/stateSections.js`), shows the sub-region as the row's parent line, and both lists file unresolvable climbs under "Location unknown", last.
- **Add a climb from the menu starts at the area you are on** (owner 2026-11-14): on the Climbs tab, Menu › *Add a climb* now opens with the area page you are looking at already chosen (country, state and trail filled in) and its next level ready to pick, so you can go on down to the exact crag; from any other tab it still starts at the country. The area page's own *Add a climb* already did this.
- **Area charts read a pre-counted rollup** (owner 2026-11-14: "make the climbs here charts load faster … same with grades spread"): migration 0421 adds `area_route_rollup` (each area's own routes counted by discipline × grade system × whole grade, kept current by `trg_routes_area_rollup`), and `area_climb_stats` / `area_children_grade_counts` now sum it instead of scanning every route under the area. Same output. Before: California timed out (HTTP 500 at 3.1 s, so state pages drew no charts), Red River Gorge 693 ms. `useAreaClimbStats` gains the 5-min staleTime its sibling hooks have.
- **Faster first load and route open** (owner 2026-11-14: "anything else we can speed up in the app for users?"): seven non-Today screens (Logbook, Me, Groups, Crew list, Crew requests, Résumé, Settings) and the route page's Suggest-a-fix form (88 KB) load as their own chunks via `lib/lazyScreens.js` (`suspended()` brings its own Suspense; `preloadWhenIdle` fetches them once the browser is idle, so tab switches stay instant). Main chunk 1,700 → 1,555 KB, RouteDetail 733 → 631 KB. The area page starts classics and the wall grade bars with the page on a state/region/range instead of waiting for the children list. `readParentSource` (guard-sources) also drops a moved component's name from a parent's `lazyScreens.js` import, so stitched sources stay valid.
- **Online audit of the dry-in-season snow calls** (owner 2026-11-13: "audit … based on deep online research"): each of the 361 routes PR #3268 took the freezing-level flags off was checked against trip reports and route pages by peak/approach (`research-data/snow-in-season-2026-11-12/audit/`, ~175 searches): 172 confirmed dry, 121 no source found (kept on the text review), 13 snow in season and 55 unclear. Those 68 get their flags back, e.g. the Diamond and Lower East Face routes (Mills Glacier snowfield), Mount Goode North Buttress, Vesper North Face, Taylor Peak (Andrews Glacier descent), the 8 Whitney east-side routes (Mountaineers Route gully). The dry list was then 293 routes (since 0490 it is the `routes.snow_in_season` column).
- **Form a crew from a group; private crews** (owner 2026-11-14: "the ability for people to form a crew from groups they are in", and "why can't we just keep the private crew non public?"): a group's *Members* tab has *Form a crew from this group* for any active member: pick members (`GroupCrewPicker`), then a saved climb or an area, and they get ordinary crew invites. Migration 0426 adds `crews.private`: unlisted under Join a crew, no share link, nobody can ask to join, and the roster is read only by the organiser and the people on it. A crew formed from a private group starts private; the organiser switches *Private / Listed* on the crew card. Checked by `scripts/oneoff/verify-0426-private-crew-pglite.mjs`.
- **Home: pinned states and provinces read A to Z, one per row, with Last updated and Update** (owner 2026-11-14): the pinned card in `src/components/TodayTab.tsx` used to wrap chips in pin order. Each pin is now its own row; the right side says when its climbs were last saved to this device (`dlTimes`) and offers *Update* (`updateState`, the same refresh as Manage areas), or *Download* for a pin not on this device yet.
- **Route page: one log row, at the top** (owner): the *Quick log* / *Full log* pair at the foot of Overview (above *More on*) is gone; the pair at the top of Overview stays. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Messages: Archive, Mute, Mark as unread and Delete a conversation, saved to your account** (owner 2026-11-14, "audit what Facebook does"): swipe right archives (Undo), swipe left deletes your copy (asks first), and a ⋯ on every direct and crew thread adds Mark as unread/read, Mute (1 h / 8 h / 24 h / until turned back on), Archive, Restrict and Block. Muted and archived threads leave every badge; archived ones return on a new message. Migration 0422 (`conversation_state`); the old swipe-delete was React state only and came back on reload. See [logbook-crews-home](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Download GPX sits beside Save map offline** (owner, 2026-11-13): the route’s Download GPX is now in the same row as *⤓ Save map offline*, right under the route map (Plan tab, crag Overview, and the desktop map rail, where Draw approach shares the row too), instead of a full-width button further down the Plan tab below the recent tracks. Same labels (pins only / waypoints only) and the same rule for when it shows; seed routes with no Save map offline still get the button under the map.
- **Crag route Overview loses its CONDITIONS section** (owner 2026-11-14: "remove the CONDITIONS section, we already have a conditions subtab"): on sport, trad, top-rope and bouldering routes the CONDITIONS facts (Aspect, Sun, Season, Wet, Rock quality, with their `+ <Fact>` adds and the crag-wide season fallback) now sit on the Conditions tab, after Aspect & sun and Seasonal guidance and before the forecast. Aid has no Conditions tab, so it keeps the section on Overview. `check:bare` asserts both placements. Its heading is now **ROCK & SEASON** (same day): "CONDITIONS" repeated the name of the tab it sits on.
- **Faster deploys: main's browser walks run every 4 h, not on every merge** (owner 2026-11-13, "takes awhile to deploy"): measured over ~60 runs, CI jobs ran in minutes but waited 25–40 min for one of the free plan's ~20 runners, because each PR push started 20 jobs and each merge 19. Render guards and Zero state now walk main on a 4-hour schedule (plus *Run workflow*) instead of on every push to main (16 jobs that gated nothing, since `deploy.yml` never waits on them), and Build check's three static side guards share one job (4 → 2 jobs per PR push). PR walks are unchanged. `check:ci-cancel` now accepts a schedule as walking main (#795).
- **Another climber's profile: pace on Overview, no Speed tab** (owner 2026-11-14): `FullProfile`'s *SPEED PROFILE* card moved from its own tab to Overview, directly above *SPEED COMPATIBILITY*; the tab strip is Overview · Safety · Vouches · Objectives. A stale `_tab:"speed"` opens Overview.
- **The tick-list read no longer times out when the disk is slow** (2026-11-12 outage follow-up): `useRoutesWithLists` read 2,320 table pages to return 64 rows and was the query that timed out most that day (468 of 2,684 PostgREST statement timeouts); a timed-out PostgREST session is left idle in its transaction, which is what filled the pool. Migration 0419 adds the partial index `routes_named_lists_idx` and the RPC `routes_with_named_lists` (the predicate as a literal, so the index applies), and `lib/db.js` calls it.
- **Crag route facts: missing ones on one line, crag-wide ones on the crag** (owner 2026-11-13, after a census found 94–100% of crag Route facts empty on 303,571 routes): each fact section shows what it has plus ONE *Not on file yet* line of `+ <Fact>` buttons instead of a placeholder row per fact; Season falls back to the crag's / parent area's season; on a crag, wall or peak a missing Season or Aspect reads `+ Season · crag` and opens the area's *Suggest an edit* sheet, so one edit fills every route there (`CragFactRows.tsx`, `TechStats.tsx`, `RouteDetail.jsx`, `lib/cragFactField.js`; `check:bare` section 6).
- **NEXT DOOR is a thin strip right under the route header's facts** (owner 2026-11-12: "right under the header grade and number of reports header in a thin strip so users can easily go to the right or left climb without having to scroll"): *‹ left climb · grade | NEXT DOOR n of N | right climb · grade ›* sits under the Grade / Reports row on wall climbs (not boulder problems, which keep ON THIS BOULDER); the NEXT DOOR card under BASE OF THE CLIMB is gone (`src/components/WallNeighbours.tsx`, `RouteDetail.jsx`).
- **No RACK card on Class 1-3 routes or hikes — a rule** (owner, 2026-11-13: Grays Peak's Class 1 North Slopes printed RACK with the generic scrambling "light rope + slings" line): `isNoRackClass` (lib/rack.js) reads the headline grade (`displayGrade`, a range at its top, so Class 2-3 loses the card and Class 3-4 keeps it) on every discipline, plus any hike. RouteGearCheck drops the card, its generic default and its add-the-rack placeholder; the route's own rack entries join GEAR & ESSENTIALS; the printed sheet heads them "Gear". `check:no-rack-class-1-3` (in build).
- **Area map and sub-areas live in the desktop left panel** (owner 2026-11-11: "this needs to be in the left panel on desktop on climbing areas … then everything else isn't squished in the middle section"): from 1280px the area page portals its map and *Areas (N)* list into a new fixed left panel (`wideLeft`, `#cm-left-panel`, the right rail's mirror) instead of a 196px column inside the page column, so the page column keeps its full 700px. Its rows carry no grade spread (owner 2026-11-13: "remove the grade spread from the left panel"). The panel shows only while the area browser is the page in view; the left ad (`SideAd`) now sits in it, under the list. 1024–1279px (no side panels) gets the phone's sub-area section. `lib/dialogA11y.js` skips `[data-cm-left]` as it does the rail.
- **The API cannot be taken down by a stuck transaction** (2026-11-11 outage, owner present): migration 0416 sets `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 60s` on the `authenticator` role PostgREST logs in as, so Postgres ends a connection left inside an open transaction instead of letting 17 of them fill PostgREST's pool (what timed out every read for three hours). `docs/codebase/supabase-scripts.md` gains a three-step runbook for "every read times out": project up? pool full of stuck transactions? disk I/O budget spent?
- **Search shows more than six results** (owner 2026-11-13: "when i click mt only 6 show up, we should allow more options"): the website header search (and the phone Climbs search, inline) opens at 8 places and 10 climbs, with *Show all N places* and *Show more climbs* (30 more at a time, up to 200) growing the list in place (`WideSearch`, `lib/DesktopShell.jsx`). → [app-shell-and-layout](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
- **No route printing on the desktop website** (owner 2026-11-12: "remove the print feature on routes for desktop view"): the header *⎙ Print* button is gone and the Share sheet hides *Save as PDF* at 1024px+ (`useWideScreen` in `RouteShareSheet`); phones keep *Save as PDF* (`routeSheetHtml`). → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **No text is cut off with "…"** (owner 2026-11-13: profile vouches read "Safety & judgmen…" beside a wide score box): the profile's vouch summary and each vouch card lay their categories out on a grid whose label column fits the longest label (the score box is narrower, its stars 11px), so "Safety & judgment" reads whole down to 320px. Route rows (dense), Classics rows, itinerary tiles, the crew join request, the desktop inbox preview and "waiting on you" list, the route header's social strip, an area's latest reports, the area Route finder / Objectives / Map chips (Map now sizes to its word) and the route header's NEXT DOOR names wrap instead of clipping. Expandable "Read more" previews are unchanged. `measure-clipped-text` finds no clipped text outside the tap-through trip-report cards; check:overflow clean.
- **Route finder: filters in one line, compact rows, Export CSV** (competitor review batch B, item 11, owner 2026-11-04): the results header names the active filters between the count and the sort; on the website a *Compact rows* toggle (remembered) and *Export CSV* of the loaded results. Phone unchanged. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Logging a climb no longer claims a first ascent** (owner, 2026-11-13): the log sheet's *This was a first ascent* and *I developed/bolted this route* checkboxes are gone, with everything they fed: `creditFirstAscent` (filed the logger as the route's FA), the route page's *First Ascent Credit* / *Development* panels, the trip-report pills, and the *First ascent* / *Route developer* badges. The owner was shown as FA of Mt Goode's Northeast Buttress for being its first logger. Who climbed a route first is route data: enter it in *Add a climb* (or a route edit) under *First ascent*. The `climb_logs.fa_ascent` / `developed` columns stay but nothing sets or shows them.
- **check:pinned-dismiss walks Compare at 1100px** (2026-11-12): since the desktop sides became equal (#3259) the column is 592px at a 1280px window, under the half-a-viewport the guard takes for a popup page, so Compare opened and the guard reported that its opener opened nothing; main went red on *Overlays contain their own scroll*. The opener in `scripts/lib/overlay-scaffold.mjs` now uses 1100px, where the column is 700px with no side panels. Compare itself is unchanged.
- **No freezing-level warnings on alpine rock that is dry in season** (owner 2026-11-12: "It doesn’t matter for routes that don’t have a glacier or snow crossings on a normal summit attempt day"): 798 alpine-rock/scramble routes carried the freezing-level and refreeze flags because their text mentions snow anywhere, mostly early-season talk. Each was read twice against its own text (`research-data/snow-in-season-2026-11-12/`, RULE.md: peak season; snow lingering only into early/midsummer does not count; unsure keeps the flags): 361 dry routes (a built-in id list then; `routes.snow_in_season` since 0490) now read `snowInSeason: "no"` and `hasSnowLegs` drops them; glacier and alpine ice are untouched. Also `névé` in `lib/terrain.js` no longer matches inside "never"/"whenever"/"Nineveh".
- **Live site check walks in as a guest** (2026-11-13): the twice-daily `live-site-check` failed every run from 2026-10-23 on a healthy site, reporting "the app never rendered its nav bar": production opens on the sign-in screen and the walk never tapped *View as guest*. `check:ui` now takes that way in whenever the gate is on screen, and reaches Profile by its bar tab when a guest’s Menu has no *Your profile* card (`scripts/lib/menu-screens.mjs`). Walked against production: all 22 screens OK.
- **Area page prompts in one row on the website; labels read apart from their notes** (owner 2026-11-05: "they don't need to be stacked"; "the subheaders look too similar to the copy below"): at 1024px+ *Add a climb*, *Suggest an edit* and *Wrong place* sit side by side as question + button cells; a new palette token `C.label` (soft blue) colours the ~73 small uppercase field labels that were the same grey as the notes under them (`AreaPage`, `AddHereFooter`, `SummitBriefing`, `ClimbMatchCore.jsx` `C`).
- **Climbs to confirm are answered in Notifications; Alerts is called Notifications** (owner 2026-11-11: "Find a better place for climbs to confirm … Are alerts the same as notifications?"): a tag is now a row with *I was there* / *Wasn't me* (`LogTagRow`, `lib/LogTagConfirmations.jsx`) under Requests in the bell's panel, in Home's list and on the desktop rail; the card above the Logbook is gone, and Ranks keeps a one-line pending link. Home's *Alerts* and the rail's *Alerts* card were the bell's list under a second name, so all three say *Notifications*, swipe-left says *clear* everywhere, and they show one count (`_alerts.newN`). On the website the bell keeps its dropdown and *See all notifications* opens the panel as a page in the column (`NotifPanel page`). Weather and agency alerts keep the word. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Compact area page and area photos** (owner 2026-11-11: "the climbs here box is too large on area pages ... you have to scroll too far to look at the areas below"; Mountain Project used as a reference): grade spread first with *Climbs here by type* behind a dropdown, the description clamped, one Route finder · ★ Objectives · Map row, every sub-area (no cap) in a left column on the website and right after that row on a phone, striped classic rows marked ✓ logged / ★ objective, a thin photo strip, Getting there words then map, all in one bordered sheet; the sticky path and the tab title name the area. Migration 0411 (applied and recorded): an area photo is a `topos` row with `area_photo` and no route, so it keeps the bucket, moderation and removal rules; `area_photo_feed` reads the subtree newest first. → [maps and area browser](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md)
- **A crew's size is a database rule; email invites hold a spot** (owner 2026-11-12, follow-up to PR #3220): migration 0415 refuses any invite, accept or email invite that would put a crew past its size, even two at the same moment; an email invite counts toward the size and the finder's open spots; signing up to claim one is never refused. In the app one `crewIsFull` test decides everywhere, and a refusal from the database reads "The crew is full". See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Desktop: equal side panels, centred column** (owner 2026-11-11: "there needs to be equal space on desktop view on the left and right panels… center the middle too"): `wideGeom` (lib/wideLayout.js) centred the column + rail as ONE block, so the right side (rail + margin) was wider than the left. The page column is now centred on the window, with a 324px side (GAP + 300px panel) on BOTH sides at ≥1280px: the rail on the right, the left ad's panel on the left (AD_SIDE_MIN 1440 → 1280). The column narrows where the window cannot hold both sides (1280 → 592px; 700px from ~1390). index.html's boot placeholder repeats the numbers.
- **Partners › By Area: list yourself at the place you picked** (owner 2026-11-11): choosing a state, region or crag in By Area now shows a prominent *Climb at <place>?* box right under the picker with a one-tap *List me at <place>* (then *Remove listing* / *Add dates or climbing types*), so the climber searching a place can be found there too, without first saving the area or opening My areas (`src/components/ListMeInAreaBox.tsx`; [partners](docs/features/partners.md)).
- **84 more mountain trad routes filed alpine** (owner 2026-11-11, "do all of those now"; 0418): a second pass read every trad route for approach and descent signals 0413 never looked at, a camp or bivy, glacier travel, a multi-hour walk, and moved the Bugaboos, the Devil's Thumb, Lotus Flower Tower, the Incredible Hulk and similar backcountry lines. See [route-quality-control](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Menu: Your groups section removed** (owner 2026-11-11: "Remove your groups from menu. We already have a button for groups there"): the Menu no longer lists up to five of your groups above the tiles; the *Groups* tile opens Groups › Your groups. *Your crews* stays. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Climbs reopens on the page you left** (owner 2026-11-11: "go straight back to the page on the climbs that you were just looking at"): leaving Climbs for another tab and tapping Climbs again returns to the same climb page (same sub-tab, its Back still works) or the same area / Map / Route finder, at the same scroll, instead of the region picker. Tapping Climbs while on Climbs still goes to the area browser. Review follow-up: a search or header-search jump still loading when you leave now finishes on return (full breadcrumb; the Route finder for a header search) unless a newer jump is waiting; a climb's Back to another tab forgets it; the scroll is restored once, never while a page view or dialog covers Climbs, and stops on a scrollbar drag. `lib/climbsReturn.js`, `goTab` in ClimbMatch.jsx. See [maps and area browser](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Objectives: state first, for every list, sort and discipline** (owner 2026-11-11): `lib/ListsManager.jsx` grouped climbs under a state header only when a list spanned 2+ states and only for the "To do first" / "By area" orders; a one-state view (e.g. Sport → Utah) dropped the header and printed "Area · Utah" on each row. Now every list shows its states first (collapsed; click one to see its climbs, sorted by the chosen order inside it), rows show only crag · area, and a search opens every state so a match is never hidden.
- **Crag routes: one trail section, and a trail drawer you can shape** (owner 2026-11-05): CLIMBERS' TRACKS on a crag is "Climbers' walk-in recordings" and draws nothing when empty (the crag map's *Add the trail* is the one invitation); the log form attaches a GPX "of the walk in"; the drawer gets draggable points, midpoint handles, Undo/Redo, full screen and *Follow trails* (BRouter); *Submit Trail* is gated at 10 points, not 3 → docs/features/route-page.md
- **Desktop website: a nav strip under the top bar, a centred block** (owner 2026-11-11, PR #3240): the left sidebar's seven tabs moved to a 48px nav strip in the fixed header; the column and the rail are centred as one block; *Your crews* (with their chats) and *Your groups* are rail cards and the rail's Direct chats are gone (the top bar's Messages keeps them); the avatar opens an account menu (☰ kept); the left ad sits in the margin beside the block. See [app shell](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **Crew counts include the organiser everywhere; a pending request is not "in"** (owner 2026-11-11): migration 0408 makes the creator a confirmed `crew_members` row on create and backfills crews missing one, so the crew finder and "crews here" spot counts stop showing a crew one short; the finder no longer counts your own pending request as a member; the demo accept-request "crew full" check uses `crewSize` (it counted pending requesters); the profile's "N of M climbers" uses the crew's own size. 0410: unanswered invites hold a spot in the finder too (`held_count`), invites refuse past a crew's size, and a full crew offers no Invite on a profile. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Route facts: a locked grade no longer locks the empty facts** (owner 2026-11-11): on every crag route the ROUTE FACTS card's Edit pencil opened the Flag sheet, because it fell back to the grade, and a crag grade is always High. Its heading also read *High · locked* over rows saying *Not on file yet*. Now the card pencil and each facts section pencil open the editor at the first empty field (`firstEditable`, `lib/firstEditable.js`), with Grade still locked inside it. The lock chip sits on the grade itself, and `check:facts-pencil-open` (build) holds it. No database change: the edit guard was already per field. → [route quality control](docs/features/route-quality-control.md)
- **No Discussion under the area Map** (owner 2026-11-11: "Remove discussion from area maps"; area pages keep theirs): opening *View map* on an area no longer shows the area's comment thread below the map; nor do the Route finder and Objectives screens. RoutesTab hands the thread to `DbAreaBrowser` as `areaDiscussion`, which draws it right after `<AreaPage>` only; the fetch stays keyed on the area, so toggling the Map refetches nothing. `check:area-surfaces` asserts the placement.
- **Route map: a Slope angle layer** (competitor review item 23, owner picked 2026-11-04; hosting decided 2026-11-11 by drawing it in the browser, so nothing to host): a *Slope* button under Full screen shades ground of 27 degrees and steeper in avalanche-terrain bands (27, 30, 35, 45, 60) over any base layer, from zoom 11 in, with a colour key. Drawn from the public AWS Terrain Tiles; it carries the elevation credit Canada's and Mexico's licences require, only while it is on. Also fixed: the route map showed no tile credit (Esri, OpenStreetMap, OpenTopoMap) on first load, only after a layer button was pressed.
- **Trip plans for the Washington routes moved trad to alpine** (owner 2026-11-11, "do what you recommend"; follows 0413): of the 51 WA rows 0413 re-filed as alpine, 48 had no plan. Researched from open pages only; **8 written** (Dragons of Eden, Dreamer, Mount Olympus via the Blue Glacier, Mount Stuart Direct North Ridge, North Early Winters Spire NW Corner, Prusik Stanley-Burgner, Southeast Mox Beckey Route, Vesper North Face), each with 2+ opened sources and stated figures; 40 held (23 skipped, 17 low confidence or with no stated day figure, incl. two Dragontail plans whose only figure was the lake-trail round trip). Inputs, outputs and the rollback before-file: `research-data/trip-plans-2026-11-11-alpine413/`. Live audit after: 0 prose, 0 malformed.
- **Crag route page: shorter facts card, a Route description section; mountain trad routes are alpine** (owner 2026-11-11, #3242): ROUTE FACTS keeps grade, pitches, length and rock; Pitches link and Simul-climbing moved to GEAR & SAFETY; ROUTE DESCRIPTION is its own section under the facts with a pencil; the I–VI grade on a crag reads *Commitment*; *Location on the wall* takes up to 1,000 characters (0412). Under the owner's rule "crag for trad is just a short approach", 0413 re-filed 306 trad routes that are mountain days (summit or ridge finish, long backcountry approach, or glacier travel) as alpine; Outer Space and other valley walls stay trad. See [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md) and [route-quality-control](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Safety tab: no repeated forecast, no Got beta?** (owner 2026-11-11, #3218): *Forecast at key points* / *at the crag* and *Weather & mountain forecasts* are off Safety wherever the Conditions tab carries them (a route with no Conditions tab keeps them there), and the *Got beta?* box closes Overview and Plan only. `check:axe` now waits for the route page's tab bar, so it really scans the Plan, Conditions, Reports, Safety and Photos tabs (they were NOT REACHED on every run). See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `docs/guards/browser-walks.md`.
- **Partners: a Leads ▾ filter** (competitor review item 20, the filter half; owner 2026-11-05 picked lead vs follow grades): once a roped discipline is picked, *Leads 5.10a or harder* keeps the climbers who lead at least that grade there. Follow-only climbers and climbers with no grade on file drop out; it filters the real lists and the examples alike.
- **Trip plan research retry, WA** (owner 2026-11-11: "retry and keep going"): the 91 WA routes wave 2 skipped or held as low confidence, re-researched from open pages only (no blocked site, no browser workaround). Same apply script, `--dir=research-data/trip-plans-2026-11-11-retry`: **25 new plans** written (2+ sources, medium confidence, every day titled with a figure), re-read 25/25; 18 held as low confidence and 48 still skipped (listed in `out-retry-*.json` with reasons, plus the row-data contradictions each researcher found). Hours appear only where a source states them, as its range ("6-8") rather than a midpoint. The 88 WA stub rows with no approach, gain or waypoints were not retried. Live audit after: 893 plans, 0 prose. The search budget ran out again mid-retry; the remaining skips need a fresh session.
- **Trip plans are day by day everywhere; every prose plan converted; wave 2 of WA research** (owner 2026-11-11: "then do text only plans convert day by day, make that a rule in the app when we do other routes and states"; research "Washington only", mixed only on a peak): one rule, `itineraryShapeErrors` in `lib/itineraryShape.js` — a plan is null or `{ days: [ … ] }` with typed figures, and a new or converted plan also has titled days each with hours, miles or gain. `import-alpine`, `load-state` and `load-wa-rock-safe` abort on a bad plan; `check:itinerary-shape` (build) tests the rule and that every writer calls it; `audit:itinerary-shape` reads the live table; migration 0409 adds the database CHECK. Data (`apply-trip-plans-2026-11-11-wave2.mjs`, rollback file beside its inputs, re-read 336/336): 236 prose plans converted by researchers to titled day cards, 74 split mechanically at their own Day markers with the text kept whole (low-confidence conversions, 2 skips, and the 27 trad/sport rows the owner excluded from research), 26 new WA plans. Live audit after: 868 plans, **0 prose**; non-crag routes with a structured plan 528 → 837. Hours a converter computed from a pace rule were dropped (the planner computes those); road sentences were stripped from `cal`. Research yield was low (26 of 159) because the session's shared web-search budget ran out and SummitPost / The Mountaineers / CascadeClimbers / WTA trip reports refuse fetches; skipped rows are listed in each `out-research-*.json`. Review fixes in the same PR: the chips show on routes with no plan, a rest day needs a stored 0, summary-only plans render. → [enrichment-prose](docs/codebase/enrichment-prose.md)
- **Trip plan redesigned, and 43 Washington plans researched** (owner note 2026-11-11, "make the whole trip plan better ui. Also do full research to fill the missing data on routes"): the Plan tab's Trip plan opens with an at-a-glance card (day count, summary, Gain / Loss / Distance / Moving tiles, trust chips), a *Before you go* box, then one card per day with a DAY N tag, a *Goal* line and a labelled stat grid; a missing figure is a dash, a day with none says so with *+ Add*, a spare day reads *No travel planned this day*, and schedules fold behind *Hour by hour*. Data: of 13,037 non-crag routes only 499 had a structured plan (all WA), 283 a prose one and 12,255 none (`scripts/oneoff/measure-trip-plan-gaps.mjs`). Wave 1 researched 18 WA plans with days lacking time or distance and 75 WA peak routes with no plan, each from two or more sources: 14 gap fills and 29 new plans written (`apply-trip-plans-wa-2026-11-11.mjs`, rollback `before-2026-11-11T17-43-13-634Z.json`, re-read 43/43 by `verify-trip-plans-wa-2026-11-11.mjs`); 18 low-confidence plans held, 32 routes skipped for thin sources. Days with no hours went 39 → 14. Left: 157 WA no-plan routes (`inputs/not-in-wave1.json`), ~5,233 peak routes outside WA, the 283 prose plans, and the row-data disagreements the researchers logged in each `out-*.json` `notes`. → [route page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Lead and follow grades on the profile** (competitor review batch C, item 20, owner 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05): for every roped discipline the profile editor asks two grades, *Lead* and *Follow*, instead of one grade plus a Lead / Follow-only switch; a climber who only follows leaves Lead empty. Stored in the shape every reader already reads — `grade` + `role` — with the follow grade added as `climb_grades[k].follow` only when a leader states one (no migration: the column is a map), so older profiles mean exactly what they did. Profiles, the Profile tab and the editor's preview print it beside the role (*leads · follows 5.11b*, `gradeRoleText`); match % keeps scoring the lead grade (docs/codebase/algorithms.md). A Partners filter on it waits for the results-first Partners screen (#3186).
- **Hiking pace is not labelled self-reported** (owner 2026-11-11, following the résumé change of 2026-11-05): the *SPEED PROFILE* heading no longer adds *(self-reported)* when you typed your own rate, its edit note no longer says the rate is flagged, and a partner pace comparison shows *You* rather than *You (self-reported)*. The flag that only fed that label is gone. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Your conditions offers sign-in; Float plans says device, not phone** (owner 2026-11-05, follow-up to the Menu audit): signed out, Your conditions said "Sign in to keep it on your account" with nothing to tap. From the Menu, the Home checklist and Settings it now reads *Saved on this device.* with a *Sign in to keep it on your account* button that opens sign-in. Float plans said "Saved on this phone" and "Swipe a plan left to delete it" on the desktop website too. It now says *this device* everywhere and leaves the swipe sentence off on the website, where each plan's Delete button is the control. The Menu's Float plans tile also checks where it landed: one tap in three still ended at the top after the first fix, so it now re-scrolls for a few seconds if the page was pushed back, and stops as soon as the climber scrolls.
- **Belay catches are gone** (owner 2026-11-05: "too confusing"; vouches stay): no screen logs, counts, confirms or shows a catch. Removed: the Profile's Belay & Catch Record and Recent catches, *Log a belay catch* on a climber's profile (`LogCatch`, `CatchLedger` deleted), the *Falls caught* tile and stat-line figure, the Ranks Belay board, the *Log 10 belay catches* challenge, the Year in Climbing *Belay catches* tile, the share card's and partner card's catch figures, the log form's *Who caught the fall?* question, the catch alerts, sample catches, a vouch's *Catch verified* line, and every Help, legal, privacy and notification sentence that named them. Migration 0417 (2026-11-12) then dropped the `belay_catches` table, its stored rows and every catch function, and rebuilt `leaderboard()` without the Belay board. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Best day to climb removed** (owner 2026-11-05): the Home tile under Jump back in, the Menu's Plan & log tile and the full-screen overlay are gone, with `lib/BestDayTile.jsx`, the `bestDayOpen` state, the one-shot day handoff into the Conditions card and the per-area discipline-count hook only it read. The crag conditions score stays on every route's Conditions tab and in Compare routes. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Résumé rows swipe to remove; no self-reported marks; every date is a calendar pick** (owner 2026-11-05): in the résumé's Edit mode each Experience row and course is a `SwipeRow` (hide a logged or seed climb, remove a hand-added one or a course; buttons beside the row on the website) — the ✕ at the end of a flex row was squeezed off by a long name or role, and seed rows had no control at all. The *self-reported* badge on courses and the *added by hand* suffix on climbs are gone. Free-text date fields became calendar pickers: the résumé's course date and climb date, the guide dashboard's credential *Valid to*, the guide inquiry's dates (From / To, stored as one formatted line), and the float plan's departure and hard-return times (`datetime-local`, printed through `fpWhen`). See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`, `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Search first** (owner's picks from the competitor review, 2026-11-04, batch A item 1): the phone Climbs tab opens on the catalog search box under its title, then the United States' regions (it opened on two empty selects); on every width each climb hit shows its discipline's glyph with the grade in that colour, and the empty box offers *Near me* (the Climbs map at your position) and your last five searches, kept on this device only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Post an open crew in two fields** (competitor review batch B, item 17, owner 2026-11-04): on the website the Crews tab has a saved climb + a day + *Post* under *+ Create a crew*, making a normal open crew with that day proposed. Phone unchanged. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Help & FAQ page with a contact line** (competitor review batch B, item 19, owner 2026-11-04): the footer's *Help & FAQ* opens the tour + FAQ as a page in the column on the website (← Back), the Menu's *How ClimbMatch works* the same content as a dialog on a phone; both end with *Contact: hello@climbmatch.example* — a PLACEHOLDER on the reserved .example domain (`lib/contact.js`, owner to replace). See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Route page: sticky sub-tabs, jump links in one row, the conditions score in words** (competitor review batch A, item 4, owner 2026-11-04): the Overview · Plan · Conditions … bar stays under the app bar while the page scrolls (phone and website); on the website the section jump pills show six and fold the rest behind *More ▾*; the Conditions score gets one or two plain sentences (label, what holds it down, best day of the week, when the forecast was fetched). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Partners: results first** (competitor review batch B, item 16, owner 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05; phone and website): Find partners opens with a title that states the search ("Climbers who climb where you do · within 50 mi of 98201") and ONE wrapping row of pills — Search by ▾, Distance ▾ and Within ▾, Discipline ▾, Experience ▾, Free this weekend, Only women/men/non-binary climbers, Sort ▾, More filters, My areas — in place of the name box, the mode grid, the Climbers-on-ClimbMatch header, the distance chips and slider, the gender card and the count/sort/Filters row that stacked above the first card. Name search is an icon pill beside the title. Cards stay one per row (`lib/PartnerSearch.jsx`).
- **Crag regular** (competitor review batch C, item 22, owner 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05 — Strava's Local Legend): the climber with the most logged days under an area in the last 90 (two at least) is named on the area page's PARTNERS HERE card and under Ranks › By area, with the day count; signed in only, the same people the boards rank, a row that opens their profile (`crag_regular`, 0404; `useCragRegular`). Nothing is drawn when nobody has two days there yet.
- **Legal documents open at their title, and leave Settings behind** (popup audit, owner 2026-11-05): the four legal pages are the one screen drawn inline rather than as a fixed popup, so from the footer or Settings › Legal & help they opened scrolled to wherever the page behind had been, and on the website the Settings sheets stayed open over them. `App` now scrolls to the top when `legal` is set, and the Legal & help rows call `closeSettings()` first. The audit found every fixed popup already opens at its top, and every per-section Edit lands on its section; the route fix form now also forgets its prefill and pitch focus on close.
- **Footer: Install ClimbMatch** (competitor review batch A, item 10, owner 2026-11-04): the desktop footer ends its ClimbMatch column with an Install link while the browser has offered to install the app (Chrome and Edge on the built site; never on Safari or Firefox, never once installed); a click opens the browser's own install prompt. `lib/installPrompt.js`; see `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Your groups in the desktop sidebar** (owner's picks from the competitor review, 2026-11-04, batch A item 2): under *Your crews*, up to five of your groups with a red dot when one has posts you have not seen (the Groups tab's own count), and "See all N groups" past five. Same data the Menu's Groups tile already holds. Desktop only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Area page, website arrangement** (competitor review batch B, item 15, owner 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05): at 1024px+ CLIMBS HERE and GRADE SPREAD sit side by side, the Route finder's form sits inline under them (type, grade range with its top preset to your own profile grade, sort; *Find climbs* opens the finder with those filters — `RouteFinderInline`), classic climbs are one-line rows, and AREA FACTS gains **Most logged here** beside Season: twelve bars of what climbers logged under the area by month (`area_logged_months`, 0403: every visible log, counts only). Phone page unchanged.
- **Reactions are emoji again, Facebook's way** (owner 2026-11-05): the 21 reactions got their emoji back (`REACTIONS[].e`). On a group post and in every comment thread, resting the mouse on Like, holding it, right-clicking it, or tapping React or the reaction count pops Facebook's floating bar over that button — emoji only, no word under each, your pick on a blue disc, the pointer swells the one under it — and the count line stacks the top three emoji on small discs. The bottom-sheet picker of line icons is gone; `check:no-emoji` allows exactly that one table. See `docs/guards/social-and-crews.md` and `docs/features/groups.md`.
- **Saved searches removed** (owner, 2026-11-05: "Remove saved searches features and everything related to that"): the Route finder's *Save search* and *N saved* list, the Climbs tab's *Save this search* row, Logbook › Saved's *Searches* chip and card, the Menu's *Saved searches · N* tile, the Help tour line, the `useMySavedSearches` reads and the `saved_searches` table (migration 0407 drops it) are all gone. The finder still opens with words and filters handed in by the website header's typed search (`openFinderAt` → `finderJump`). `check:outage`'s Saved-view landmark is *My itineraries* now. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Menu: Offline library and Hire a guide tiles removed** (owner 2026-11-11: "Remove downloads from menu. Also remove hire a guide from there."): the Menu no longer carries the download-icon *Offline library* tile (Saved & lists) or *Hire a guide* (Plan & log). Offline downloads stay under Logbook › Saved › Offline, and guides under Partners › Hire a guide.
- **Menu: Downloads tile removed, Float plans tile reaches the plans, Your conditions audited** (owner 2026-11-05): the Downloads tile only scrolled Home to the pinned-states card, so it is gone (Offline library under Saved & lists remains). The Float plans tile switched to Crews but never scrolled: its one-shot 300 ms timer fired before the lazily loaded Float plans chunk mounted, leaving the plans 1,800 px below the fold. It now polls for the box (up to ~4 s) and scrolls once it exists. Your conditions (Menu tile, Home checklist item and Settings all share `CondPrefsControls`) was driven end to end: a quick-range chip updates the range line and the stored prefs, Default restores them, More conditions expands, and the sync line states where the prefs are kept.
- **Route page: the social strip is one row, and sits in the header too** (owner 2026-11-05): *N viewing now · N page views · N crews eyeing · N climbers logged it · N lists saved* no longer wraps on Overview (it scrolls sideways if it must), and the same stats render as a bordered cell row under the header's Grade · Dist · Gain · Reports strip, above the sub-tabs. One component, `RouteSocialRow` (`src/components/RouteSocialRow.tsx`), draws both; a zero stat is left out as before. → [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **The desktop page column has edges** (owner 2026-11-05: "add a border around all the middle … audit how Strava does it"): at 1024px+ a 1px `C.border` line runs down each side of the 700px page column, from the top bar's border to the footer's, square and unshadowed — the frame X's feed and Strava's activity page draw (Strava's dashboard, Mountain Project and AllTrails frame nothing; theCrag frames sidebar + content + rail as one sheet, which the edge-pinned layout has no block for). A box-shadow, so no width changes; the boot placeholder paints the same lines; a full-screen page placed in the column no longer draws its own pair (the column's lie just outside it). Phone unchanged. `lib/wideLayout.js` `COL_EDGES`; see `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **ROUTE BREAKDOWN's heading loses its caption** (owner 2026-11-05: "Remove text like this Tap a row for detail near where all the headers are. It cuts off the edit button on the phone"): the *Tap a row for detail* caption beside the pencil is gone; the heading row is the title and the pencil (`RouteBreakdown`). No other heading row carried a caption beside its pencil.
- **ACCESS & REGULATIONS says it is a dropdown** (owner 2026-11-05: "isn't clear that it's a dropdown, dropdown button needs to be more clear with context"): the closed row on a route's Plan tab ends in a blue *Show details* pill with the chevron, *Hide* once open, instead of a bare 16px chevron (`AccessFold`). Phone and website alike. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Discipline is locked** (owner 2026-11-05): a route's discipline can no longer be edited by a climber — the edit sheet's Discipline card shows the route's own discipline as High · locked with a *Flag it* button, and the database answers high for `discipline` / `rockStyle` on every route (0406), withdrawing the scrambling / mountaineering / alpine / trad exception; a route with NO discipline on file keeps the chip picker, and the owner’s approval of that first edit locks it. See `docs/features/route-quality-control.md`.
- **Messages: a long message stays inside its bubble, and the quick replies fit the screen** (owner 2026-11-05): a pasted URL or any unbreakable word now wraps inside the blue box instead of pushing it aside and spilling past it, and the canned replies under a thread wrap to the screen's width rather than running off the right edge with the last chip cut in half. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Crag-page Filters button is a real button** (owner, 2026-11-05): the Filters control over a wall's climb list was a small muted chip, the same size and shade as the sort chips under it, and hard to find. It is now a full-width large button in the text colour with a turning chevron, drawn as the Route finder's Filters button is (`src/components/AreaPage.tsx`).
- **Route tags are list membership only** (owner, 2026-11-05: "keep list membership tag and remove all the rest"): the chip row under a route's header draws the lists the route is on (50 Classics, Bulger with its rank, the Washington peak lists, Regional classic) and nothing else. The character chips from `routes.features` (Exposed, Sustained, Slab, Talus…) and the derived Glacier travel / Raptor closure warnings are gone; the column stays (the Style editor went in the follow-up below). `check:route-tags` pins it. See `docs/guards/contributions-and-logbook.md`.
- **Style editor gone, saved-count chip reworded** (follow-up to the lists-only tags, owner 2026-11-05): SuggestFix no longer offers *Style / character* and Add route no longer asks it, since `routes.features` reaches no screen; the proposal and apply map drop the key, `check:add-route-fields` no longer pins it. The social strip's "N lists saved" now reads "N climbers saved it", the same count ROUTE STATS calls *On objectives*.
- **Wall cards carry a mini grade chart on the website** (competitor review batch B, item 13, owner 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05): on an area page at 1024px+, each child's card draws five bars — its graded climbs banded on its main scale (rock ≤5.7 · 5.8–9 · 5.10 · 5.11 · 5.12+; V, ice, mixed, aid and class have their own bands) — so a crag's walls say at a glance which one suits your grade (`WallGradeBars`). One read for the whole list (`area_children_grade_counts`, 0402). Ungraded climbs are in no bar and the chart's name says "N of M graded". Phone cards unchanged.
- **Forecast at key points: a point switch on the website** (competitor review batch B, item 14, owner 2026-11-04): instead of three or four stacked cards of seven tiles each, at 1024px+ a row of pills names the key points and the chosen one's week shows; the phone stacks them as before. The review's *On this wall* rail list is not built: it needs the wall-neighbour read, a database item. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Group event cards show who is going** (competitor review batch A, item 7, owner 2026-11-04): an event row shows up to four faces of the climbers going with *N going*, a capped event an amber *N spots left* corner badge (*Full* at none), and Discover's title names the place picked (*Climbing groups near Index*). See `docs/features/groups.md`.
- **Crews: request rows and Add to calendar** (competitor review batch A, item 6, owner 2026-11-04): on the website a crew's join requests are one-line rows (avatar, name, note, Accept, Decline) instead of three-part cards; once a crew day is agreed, an *Add to calendar* button beside the date downloads a one-day .ics event with the place and meeting point (`lib/ics.js`). Phone cards unchanged. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Home: Finish setting up as tiles on the website** (competitor review batch A, item 3, owner 2026-11-04: "are the sections too wide? IE Finish setting up"): at 1024px+ the checklist's rows are tiles two across (four before *View more*) instead of one-line rows stretched across a 700px column; the phone keeps its list. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Profile stat line, tighter Ranks rows** (competitor review batch A, item 8, owner 2026-11-04): your profile header says *N climbs · N falls caught · N vouches* under your name, a public profile *N climbs logged · N vouches* (real climbers, zeros left out); Ranks rows are ~42px on the website and 56px on a phone. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md` and `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **Ads on desktop route pages, and one scrollbar** (owner 2026-11-04: "i would also like to have ads on route pages. So as you scroll down, ads will be on the left panel and right panels" → "go"): a climb page on the website carries a 160×600 in the sidebar under Your crews and a 300×600 in the rail under the map; both panels slide up with the page and the ads stay in view. The slots are Google AdSense units and stay EMPTY (no label, no box) until the repository variables `VITE_ADSENSE_CLIENT` / `_SLOT_SIDE` / `_SLOT_RAIL` are set — there is no ad account yet; setting them also adds the ads sentence to the Privacy Policy (bump `POLICY_VERSION` then). The rail's widgets follow the page's scroll instead of scrolling on their own. Phone untouched. `lib/Ads.jsx`; see `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Rail chats in two dropdowns** (owner 2026-11-04: "separate it by crew and individual chats with dropdowns"): the desktop rail's Chats card is now *Crews* and *Direct*, each a folding section with its unread count on the header, five newest apiece and "See all N" past that; both open by default. Desktop only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Typed search opens the Route finder** (competitor review item 12, 2026-11-05): on the website, "5.9 trad near Leavenworth", "WI4" or "V4 in Squamish" in the header search offers, as its first row, the Route finder with that grade range and discipline, scoped to the place typed (else the home state, else the biggest state) — `lib/typedSearch.js` (`parseTypedSearch`; grades through `lib/grade.js`'s one parser), `WideSearch` in `lib/DesktopShell.jsx`, App's `openSavedSearch`.
- **Desktop top bar: bare ☰ and a real logo** (owner chose 2026-11-04, built 2026-11-05): on the website the Menu button is the icon alone — no card, no "Menu" label, accessible name unchanged — and the wordmark is the app mark (`BrandMark`) beside a 26px ClimbMatch, in `ClimbMatch.jsx`'s banner. The phone keeps its boxed Menu.
- **Desktop footer spans the whole window** (owner 2026-11-05: "needs to fit the whole bottom screen"): `WideFooter` (`lib/DesktopShell.jsx`) is a full-width band, not a card inside the 700px page column — it pulls left by `--cm-col-left`, is drawn at the window's measured client width (not 100vw, which counts the scrollbar), paints above the fixed sidebar and rail at the page's end, and centres its brand, link columns and state directory in a 1320px container.
- **Smaller ✕ and Back; Groups gets the same header as the other tabs** (owner 2026-11-04: "the back button is way too large on phone and desktop", "the x button is also still too large", "groups has a different header than the other tabs"): `POP_CLOSE` / `POP_BACK` in `lib/popupChrome.js` go from 48px to a 36px circle / pill (14px Back label, 18px ✕), on phone and desktop; Groups' boxed headline becomes the quiet header, with + Create group beside the caption.
- **Desktop Groups: cards three across with a cover** (owner's desktop audit 2026-11-04, PR 5 of 5): Discover, Recommended and For you › Your groups lay groups out as Meetup does — three equal cards across, each under a colour band with the group's initial. Phone unchanged. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop audit fixes** (owner 2026-11-04: "the top right panel coming up, the date needs to be on one line" → "audit and fix all"): the rail's Coming up date no longer breaks across two lines; the route page's Print · Draw approach · Compare · Share · Saved row fits one line beside ← Back at 1280px; the rail map's Street · Sat · Topo · Snow buttons no longer run under its ⤢ Full screen button. A sweep of every desktop tab, a route page and the Menu at 1280 / 1440 / 1920 found nothing else clipped, wrapped or off the edge. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop controls are website-height** (owner 2026-11-04: "are buttons and things too large on the desktop view?" — "do what you recommend"): at 1024px+ the buttons and selects lose the phone's 44px touch minimum — a box-shaped button is 34px, a select 38, the unified buttons 32 — so with the 1.1 zoom they draw at 35–42px, where Mountain Project and Strava draw theirs; the text size and the zoom are unchanged, and so is the phone. One block in `lib/wideLayout.js`. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop footer, Strava-style** (owner 2026-11-04: "make it thinner on the bottom … look how strava does it … more branding"): the two tall stacked lists become one branded card: logo, wordmark and a line on what the app is for on the left; Explore · ClimbMatch · Legal columns on the right; a thin © row with Back to top. The state directory still heads it on Climbs and route/area pages. Desktop only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop Climbs root: one list of states, not two** (owner 2026-11-04): the footer's "Explore climbs by state" directory no longer ends the Climbs tab's own root page, where the state picker above it already lays out every state as a card under the United States / Canada buttons; it still ends route and area pages (`showStates` in `ClimbMatch.jsx` → `WideFooter`, `lib/DesktopShell.jsx`).
- **Desktop website: says "click", a mouse can delete, chip rows wrap** (owner's desktop audit 2026-11-04, PR 3 of 5): ~120 of the app's own "tap…" sentences read "click…" on desktop (`tx()` in `lib/tapWords.js`), swipe-only rows show Archive / Delete buttons on hover or focus, sideways chip rows wrap, the route map's layer buttons no longer hide under ⤢, and the Climbs tab opens on the United States. Phone wording and gestures unchanged. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **One set of button looks** (owner's desktop audit 2026-11-04, PR 2 of 5, phone too): primary (navy), secondary (bordered), link, and green only for Accept / Log (`lib/buttonStyles.js`), on crew join requests, partner cards, Logbook lists and the profile, whose actions sit in one row on desktop. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop website: larger, a centred search, titles on every page** (owner's desktop audit 2026-11-04, PR 1 of 5): at 1024px+ the page column, its pages, the rail, centred windows and the Messages panes are drawn ~10% larger (`ZOOM` in `lib/wideLayout.js`; maps zoomed back to 1), the column is 700px, the top-bar search sits centred in the window, every tab draws its title, the rail's Alerts card opens on the newest three, and the state directory ends only the Climbs tab and its route/area pages. Phone unchanged. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Grey text is brighter** (owner 2026-11-04: "grey and hard to see … Recent posts from your groups", "Communities with their own events and members"): the palette's two greys rise, phone and desktop — `C.textMuted` #8b949e → #a7b0ba (5.1 → 7.2:1 on a card) and `C.textSub` #99a3ad → #b8c0c8 (6.2 → 8.6:1) — with the copies in index.html, AppErrorBoundary, AuthModal and ConfirmSheet. White > secondary > muted still reads as three steps.
- **Errors reach the team** (owner, 2026-11-04: "do what you recommend" — error monitoring in our own database, not a third party): a render crash, an uncaught error and an unhandled promise rejection are filed to `app_errors` (0399) through `file_app_error()` — message, stack, page path without the query string, the build's entry-script name and the browser. Read them in the Supabase Table Editor (`app_errors`, admin only). The Privacy Policy says so under *What we collect*, and `POLICY_VERSION` moved to 2026-11-04. The crash screen still does not claim "we've been notified": delivery is best effort.
- **Route finder: a grade range no longer times out on a region** (owner note: "route finder doesn't appear to work", follow-up to #2571): Sport + Trad 5.6–5.10 in any large region (Central-East Cascades, Joshua Tree, Boulder, Wasatch …) still said *Couldn't search routes* after 3 s, because the app's signed-out role reads under RLS and the database then walked the grade index across the whole catalog once per crag. 0398 keeps it on the per-crag index; those searches now answer in 0.1–0.8 s with the same results. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Crag rock types corrected where the research disagreed** (owner note on crag area pages, 2026-11-04): the 235 stored values the About-text research had doubted were each re-checked. 26 crags now show the right rock, among them the Skaha walls (gneiss), the Gunks' Millbrook and Sky Top (conglomerate), Breaks Interstate Park (sandstone), Queen Creek (tuff) and Rattlesnake Point (limestone); Hound Ears is now placed at Boone. The rest were synonyms, or the sites did not agree, and were left as they are. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **About text for crags with 30–49 climbs, finished** (owner note on crag area pages, 2026-11-04; #3106–#3113 and this PR): the last 110 areas of the tier were researched; 36 got an ABOUT description in our own words (plus parking, walk-in or aspect where two sites agreed), the rest were skipped because no two sites described that sector, or it is closed. 957 of the 1,127 crags, peaks and walls with 30+ climbs now have one. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Boulder problems are called problems, and are never in a wall order** (owner note on routes next door and bouldering, finishing #2543): a boulder problem's page, its log, add and edit forms, and its share sheet now say *problem* wherever they named the climb (*Problem description*, *PROBLEM FACTS*, *Share this problem*, *Problem name*...). A crag list no longer offers *Left → right* because of a problem's stored position, and the Route finder with only Bouldering picked sorts *By boulder* and counts *problems*. A failed-to-load empty crag now says *No climbs in this crag yet*. See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **About text for the busiest crags** (owner note on crag area pages, resumed 2026-10-31; #3103–#3106): 220 more crags with 50+ climbs got a researched ABOUT description in our own words, plus missing parking, walk-in, rock, aspect and season facts where two sources agreed. 268 of the 279 crags with 50+ climbs now have one; five stored rock types the research contradicted were corrected. `scripts/enrich/area-page-apply.mjs` now paces writes 1 s apart and refuses any entry that shares 8+ words with a page in its `review.sources`, which is the rule `enrich:apply` already applies to routes.
- **One trail per crag, and a way to approve it** (owner note: mark the trail from parking to the crag on the map, finishing #2532): a trail a climber traces or records from a sport, trad or bouldering climb is now saved for the whole crag once approved, so every climb there shows it on its CRAG MAP, and the crag's own page shows it under Getting there. Approving one replaces the old line instead of joining the two. Before this nothing in the app could approve a submitted trail; the owner now reviews them in Menu > Review > Trails & GPS tracks. A traced trail needs at least 10 points (one at every bend), because the map draws nothing shorter. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **A route's grade in other scales** (owner's note: what OpenBeta has that we lack): the Composite Grade panel adds "About 6a French · VI+ UIAA · 18 Ewbank" under a YDS grade and "About 6B Font" under a V grade, from Mountain Project's comparison chart, as MP's route pages print it. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Groups near a place** (owner note: "does search by area make sense it seems to be too local. Need more ways to join groups away from home and ways to explore"; follows #2517's browse-by-state): a group can name the catalog crag, peak or area it climbs from (`groups.area_id`, 0396; a trigger sets its point from `area_point()`), Discover has a **Near a place** search over the whole catalog with a 25/50/100/250 mi radius, nearest first, and a crag or region page links to *Climbing groups near <area>*. The three live groups were placed (Wasatch Range, Bellingham and Mt Baker Hwy, Snoqualmie Pass Area). See [groups](docs/features/groups.md).
- **Every US/Canada FastestKnownTime summit track placed against the WHOLE catalog** (owner, 2026-10-30: "add all the tracks then"; walk-ups stay out, owner's choice): the first new-climb pass had looked at 171 of 2,410 US/Canada tracks (a title filter, the snapshot peak list, and seven states skipped). Now each track's HIGHEST point is placed against all 64,218 areas with a coordinate: 1,582 summit tracks (1,376 round trips, 206 one-ways ending at their top). Tracks on a held peak were researched per track against our routes there (pairs earlier waves decided skipped): 18 more routes draw an FKT line, e.g. Denali West Buttress, the Nose, Royal Arches, Mt Hood South Side, Slesse SW Gully, Gannett W Face, Kings Peak, South Sister, Trap Dike. Summits we did not hold were triaged, looked up by full name across the state, and researched: 3 more climbs added (Black Tusk, Golden Hinde, Storelk). A Summit pin is placed only where the catalog's coordinate lies within 150 m of the track; Denali's and Fremont's area coordinates sit 4.3 and 1.7 km off. Held: Landale (its one grade source can no longer be read), Olomana and K1 (no Hawaii). Notes: `research-data/gpx-tracks-fkt-oneway-2026-10-30/held/`.
- **Link-ups on the route page** (owner notes 2026-10-28, "for routes that people often link up, provide information on how to do that", and the note asking for the other peaks climbed on the same trip; one section serves both, beside the climber-suggested PAIRS WELL WITH): alpine, mountaineering and scrambling routes gain a LINK-UPS section (`lib/LinkUps.jsx`, table `route_link_ups`, 0395): each researched link-up the climb belongs to, with the order of summits, what joins each leg, how the descent and logistics change. PAIRS WELL WITH already stays off sport, trad and bouldering (#2397). Data: `research-data/link-ups/` (162 researched, each from two independent pages; 107 written by 2026-10-30 across WA, OR, CA, CO, WY, UT, ID and NY, covering 178 climbs; 54 name only peaks the catalog holds no climb on), written by `scripts/oneoff/link-ups/apply.mjs` after `resolve.mjs` proposes and `set-ids.mjs` records each leg's REVIEWED route id; `also_route_ids` shows a link-up on a peak's standard route without calling it the leg climbed. → [route page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **The approach tile names the climb it includes** (2026-10-28): a linked way in can carry `climbToStartHrs`, and the Shuksan SE Ridge's Fisher Chimneys way in reads "Approach · incl. 5 hr climbing to start". The total is unchanged (guidebooks cost a walk and the climbing on it separately; nothing is added here).
- **How you get there: one way in at a time, its own hazards** (owner's note, 2026-10-25): the card is headed HOW YOU GET THERE with a blue rail and shows only the picked way in; the "Pick yours" intro and the Plan-tab time note are gone. KNOWN HAZARDS follows the pick: each of the 128 routes with more than one way in carries a per-way-in hazard list (`audit:approach-hazards`: 0 missing, 0 identical).
- **Every water source a route names is on its map** (owner note: "Audit water waypoints. Make sure we have all of them per route"): new `npm run audit:water-pins` reads each route's own text (approach, itinerary, camps' water notes, pin notes) for NAMED creeks, lakes and springs and asks whether a pin stands for each. First census, 1,070 routes with waypoints: 917 named sources, 80 pinned as Water, 203 already a camp/crossing pin, 634 with no pin. `scripts/oneoff/add-water-pins.mjs` placed 150 Water pins on 126 routes from OpenStreetMap geometry (stream × recorded track or mapped trail; lake shore nearest the track), each gated by 3DEP height and the route's own pins; see [maps](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **FastestKnownTime ONE-WAY tracks, and 10 new climbs from FKT summit tracks** (owner, 2026-10-30: "the one way track should be fine to add too"; "if they can be added as new climbs for the catalog, then add them"): a one-way track (trailhead -> summit, and it must END at the summit) may now draw a route's line; a stored track is kept only when it is complete or already runs trailhead -> summit. All 7,414 cached FKT tracks re-matched: 53 one-way pairs, 19 screened out (ultras, link-ups, trails over crags), 34 researched, 15 matched, 8 written (Pikes Peak East Slopes, Marcy Van Hoevenberg, Antero, Shavano, Oxford via Belford, Lone Eagle Solo Flight, Jobs Peak East Gully, Mt Washington OR North Ridge); the other matches already drew a trailhead -> summit line. New climbs: 155 FKT summit tracks in states not compared before were looked up in the catalog (name across the whole state, every area within 3 km); most are held or are walk-ups, which are not added. 10 researched climbs added, each with its peak area, the FKT line and Summit/Trailhead pins: Lefroy, Birdwood, Smuts, Cline, Northover (AB); Vaux, Begbie, Brunswick, Ossa (BC); South Pioneer Peak (AK). Mount Olomana is held: the catalog has no Hawaii. No FKT time is stored, no source is named. Notes: `research-data/gpx-tracks-fkt-oneway-2026-10-30/`.
- **Every water source a route names is on its map** (owner note: "Audit water waypoints. Make sure we have all of them per route"): new `npm run audit:water-pins` reads each route's own text (approach, itinerary, camps' water notes, pin notes) for NAMED creeks, lakes and springs and asks whether a pin stands for each. First census, 1,070 routes with waypoints: 917 named sources, 80 pinned as Water, 203 already a camp/crossing pin, 634 with no pin. `scripts/oneoff/add-water-pins.mjs` placed 169 Water pins (and filled 4 coordinate-less ones) from OpenStreetMap geometry; Water pins 199 → 368 on 295 routes, MISSING 634 → 462, each leftover with its reason (stream × recorded track or mapped trail; lake shore nearest the track), each gated by 3DEP height and the route's own pins; see [maps](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Map pins outside Washington checked, and every WA summit pin one by one** (owner note: "Way points markers are still way off for many routes"): every trailhead pin on the 112 non-WA routes with waypoints (83 distinct points) was checked against a published trailhead coordinate, and 14 were moved (Whitney Portal, Happy Isles, Cathedral Lakes, Saddlebag, Bear Lake, Timberline and others). Every flagged summit pin was researched: South Pioneer Peak's pin and peak had been 1.2 km off on a lower bump; Crystal Crag, Pikes, Lone Eagle and Superior moved onto their GNIS summit. The 252 WA summit pins the WA audit had not researched one by one were each checked. 13 moved (Tiffany, Mount Stuart, Hinman, Booker, Bacon, Ferry, Frenzel Spitz, East Wilmans, Spectre), several of which had stood hundreds of feet below the top. 35 rows written, every one re-read. Notes: `docs/guards/waypoints-and-tracks.md`, data `research-data/oos-waypoint-audit-2026-10-30/`.
- **WA alpine waypoints audited for exact location** (owner note): every waypoint audit re-run on Washington's 854 alpine, mountaineering and scramble routes. 55 rows were corrected from published coordinates (USGS GNIS, WTA trailhead coordinates, Mountain Project), each gated by the ground and the route's own distances. The fixes: impossible trail distances, pins standing on the wrong ground, Middle Peak (Gunsight) and Rimrock Ridge placed on their summits, Mount Cameron's summit pin, Andrews Pass, and 39 trailhead pins moved onto the published trailhead. 809 summit pins were checked against GNIS, and all 356 trailhead coordinates against WTA/USFS/NPS. Notes: `docs/guards/waypoints-and-tracks.md`, data `research-data/wa-waypoint-audit-2026-10-30/`.
- **Partner requirements ask for the grade the route is** (owner note: Overexposure, a 5.8, read "Strong 5.10-5.11"): `npm run audit:partner-grade` ran on all 470 routes that carry partner requirements. It now counts a route's own pitch grades as its crux and reads a bare "5.11" as 5.11a-d. 20 rows were researched and rewritten to the standard in `docs/codebase/enrichment-prose.md` (lead the crux solidly, at most one step of margin). 0 ABOVE remain; 1 accepted BELOW (Ingalls, an easier 5.4 line). See `docs/guards/route-prose.md`.
- **A route with no season of its own shows its area's season in the header** (owner note: audit optimal season): the strip falls back to the area's researched `season_months`, then the nearest ancestor's (up to four levels: crag, then crag system). Seasons are researched per crag system in `audits/season/` (2026-10-30: 283 areas reach 80,041 of 315,870 routes; below that, sources stop naming months). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **A slash range shows and sorts as its top grade too** (owner note: "routes that have 2 grades", third pass): a live census of all 315,821 routes found 13 still printing two grades; 10 were slash ranges ("V1/2", "5.9/10", "5.11/5.12"), which also sorted and filtered at the LOWER grade. `lib/grade.js` and the client filter `routeBandIdx` now read a slashed number as a range and take its top, with the same *Rated V1/2; shown at the harder of the two grades, V2.* notice; the stored `grade_num` was re-derived by `scripts/oneoff/fix-grade-num-to-highest-grade.mjs`. The other 3 are free-or-aid alternatives ("5.8 A2 or 5.10") and keep both.
- **Route maps get FastestKnownTime tracks beyond Washington, including routes with NO pins** (owner note on route tracks; the owner has FKT's permission, no credit shown; only the full trailhead -> summit -> trailhead line, never one-way; a drawn line is fine if it is accurate): FKT routes in 26 more states and provinces were downloaded (about 2,150 tracks) and matched to the 6,199 alpine, mountaineering and scrambling routes by each PEAK's summit coordinate, so routes with no pins could be matched: 440 round trips reached 310 peaks. Research was done PER PEAK: a reviewer assigned each track to the ONE route whose line it follows (FKT page plus an opened independent source; a loop up the route and down its usual descent counts), or to none; link-up tracks were filtered out. 27 routes assigned; 26 written, each with a Summit pin at the peak coordinate (the track passing within 150 m confirms it) and, on 10, a Trailhead pin at the track's start where a trailhead pass found that trailhead's location within 400 m. Examples: Dragontail East Route, Quandary East Ridge, Half Dome Cables, Blitzen Ridge, Sir Donald NW Arete, Mount Superior South Ridge. Pinned routes outside Washington: 21 pairs researched, 8 accepted, 6 written (Castle Dome, Mount Russell East Arete, Humphreys, Longs Peak Keyhole, Diamond Peak East Ridge, Grand Teton Owen-Spalding). No FKT time is stored. Notes: `research-data/gpx-tracks-fkt-peaks-2026-10-29/`.
- **A dialog that focuses its own field keeps that focus**: *New custom list* opens with the cursor in Name, not on the ✕. `lib/dialogA11y.js` had always moved focus to the first control. Closing returns focus to *+ Create custom list*. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Home no longer greets with the demo climber's name for a frame after sign-in**: the greeting waits for this account's own profile read (`pinsFor===uid`) before reading the profile's name, which starts as the seed climber's until a reset effect empties it.
- **The alpine Conditions tab is components** (PR67, the last of the extraction series, which stops here by decision): each section of `lib/AlpineConditionsCard.jsx` (forecast box, day by day, hour by hour, avalanche, snow, streams, season, alerts…) moved to `src/components/`, and the wording they share to `lib/alpineConditionsShared.js`; the card is 4 KB (84 before). Nothing on screen changed.
- **The two star numbers have two names**: a route's guidebook 0–4 stars read **Reputation** in Technical stats / Route facts (they were "Star rating", which read as climbers' votes on routes nobody had voted on); climbers' votes read *Climber votes* and *no climber votes yet*. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **The area browser is components** (PR66 of the same series): an area's page and the route finder, the last two pieces of `lib/DbAreaBrowser.jsx`, moved to `src/components/`; the file is 14 KB (198 KB before PR65). Nothing on screen changed.
- **The Climbs tab's area browser starts coming apart** (PR65 of the same series): 13 of `lib/DbAreaBrowser.jsx`'s components — the climb row, Pick a state, Near me, the area tree, search results, the peak summary, classics, nearby peaks, partners here and others — moved to `src/components/`, and its shared helpers to five `lib/` modules. Nothing on screen changed.
- **The enrichment panels are components** (PR64 of the same series): the route page's peak facts, seasonal guidance, crowds and partner-requirements panels moved out of `EnrichmentPanels.jsx` into `src/components/`, and the season-window helpers to `lib/seasonWindow.js`. Nothing on screen changed.
- **App's render is now components** (PR63 of the same series): the last overlays App drew inline — another climber's profile, All friends, the crew safety screen, Blocked climbers, Report a climber, the data request and delete-account sheets, About, Settings › Legal and › Notifications, the objectives list and the group membership-questions editor — moved to `src/components/`. `ClimbMatch.jsx` is ~625 KB (934 KB before PR61) and now holds App's state, reads and handlers plus one element per screen. Nothing on screen changed.
- **App's overlays come out too** (PR62 of the same series): Settings and its Privacy and Account & data pages, the privacy summary, onboarding, the admin review queues, Manage areas, the log-a-climb and vouch sheets, the create-event form, and an open group's Discussion, About, Members, Events and Media tabs are now components in `src/components/`; App's group helpers moved to `lib/groupRules.js`. `ClimbMatch.jsx` is down to ~660 KB. Nothing on screen changed.
- **The App component starts coming apart into components** (owner note: break the big files into single-job components, PR61 of the series): App's eleven screen bodies moved out of `ClimbMatch.jsx` into `src/components/` — `TodayTab`, `RoutesTab`, `DiscoverTab`, `GroupsTab`, `LogbookTab`, `MeTab`, the Crew tab's `CrewListView`, `CrewFriendsView` and `CrewRequestsView`, and the two chats `DirectChatScreen` and `CrewChatScreen` — each with a strict TypeScript props interface, and App's 28 lazy screens moved to `lib/lazyScreens.js` so both share them. `ClimbMatch.jsx` went from 934 KB to 756 KB; nothing on screen changed (each body is the same code as before, apart from types and seven lint-forced `if(f)f(x)` rewrites). Guards that read App's text now read it with the moved bodies stitched back in. Progress and the next step: `docs/refactor-progress.md`.
- **Washington route maps get FastestKnownTime tracks, route by route — Washington DONE** (owner note on route tracks; the owner has FKT's permission, no credit shown): all 236 FKT Washington routes were crawled (225 tracks) and matched by shape to the 773 WA routes with a trailhead and a summit pin: 188 pairs on 150 routes. Each pair was researched against the FKT page and an independent source the reviewer opened, to confirm the SAME LINE and not only the same peak: 86 researched (24 accepted, 56 rejected, 6 unclear), 91 rejected by the earlier review. Most rejections were another line on the same peak or a multi-peak link-up. 12 complete round trips written over partial tracks: Bonanza Mary Green, Eldorado East Ridge, Glacier Peak Cool Glacier/Gerdine, Goode NE Buttress, Shuksan Fisher Chimneys, Mount Pugh Stujack, Mount Si Haystack, Mount Daniel SE, Pilchuck, Shuksan Sulphide, Stuart Cascadian Couloir, Vesper standard route; the other accepts already drew a complete track. Only the shape is stored, no FKT time. Notes: `research-data/gpx-tracks-fkt-wa-2026-10-29/`.
- **Classic climbs lists are best first for real** (owner notes on classic climbs, 2026-10-29): most classics carry no guidebook or climber stars yet, so the lists fell back to NAME order under "best first" (Alabama's top 100 read Big Poppa, Bitless, Boogey...). 0393 adds `routes.classic_score` (a vote-weighted star average `flag-classics.mjs` writes on every classic; a sort key that renders nowhere), and `area_classics`, `state_classics` and the wall's `areaClassics` order by it. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
