- **A closed road counts in the distance, and text is no longer cut off with "…"** (owner note on Three Fingers South Peak via Lookout, 2026-10-29): where a road is closed below the trailhead the route's distance and gain now start where you can park, and TECHNICAL STATS says how much of it is the closed road (`road.walkMi` / `road.walkGainFt`, `lib/outing.js` `roadWalkMi`; editable in the road section). Written on 44 routes across 7 roads, among them the three Three Fingers routes (FR 41, +8 mi each way, +1,400 ft). Every one of the 151 routes with an in-force closure, on 34 roads, now has a recorded verdict, and Glacier Creek Road (FR 39), which reopened 2026-08-20, no longer reads as closed on its routes. The researched *Why go this way* reasons from #2893 are now in the database too. New `npm run audit:closure-distance` lists the candidates; the verdicts and the research procedure are in `audits/closure-distance/`. Across the app, 186 style objects that cut text off with an ellipsis now wrap, and the line clamps without a *See more* and two character cuts (the editor's current value, the section-jump chips) are gone.
- **Route map pins checked against the federal gazetteer** (owner note: "the route tracks are still messed up", resumed): every pin named for a pass, col, gap, notch, saddle or lake (388) was compared with GNIS; four that sat 1-2.5 km off their feature were moved (Castle Pass on Blizzard Peak, Cathedral Pass, Nada Lake on McClellan Peak, Source Lake on The Tooth), and Mount Fairchild's two Appleton Pass pins, a different trail from the High Divide way its text describes, became one Deer Lake pin. Bear Mountain's line built from its pins is already not drawn. Notes: `research-data/gpx-map-pass-2026-10-29.md`.
- **The route page starts coming apart into components** (owner note: break the big files into single-job components, PR52 of the series): 89 pieces of `RouteDetail.jsx` (PR52-60; PR58 moved the large panels — topos and the topo editor, route breakdown, the base check-in, the Reports consensus, the safety matrix, the trip plan, the planner calculator, tech stats, the forecast and the conditions score — PR59 the edit sheet, and PR60 the body of every sub-tab — Overview, Plan, Reports, Photos, Partners, Safety — with the share sheet and the full-screen photo, so the page itself now only holds its state and picks which body to draw) — among them the edit pencil, the missing-field note, the tag chips, rack beta, pitch gear, the float plan card, climate & season, the photo row, the topo drawing, its key and thumbnail (their shared drawing helpers now in `lib/topoMarks.jsx`), the enlarged topo viewer, the access dropdown, the crag rack and location cards, pairs well with, the trip-leg cards and trip profile, the crag fact sections, the wall and boulder neighbours, road conditions, the overnight permit, the weather glyph (the helpers they share with the page now in `lib/`), *What's changed* and the Reports consensus row — now each live in `src/components/` with typed props. Nothing on screen changes except one fix: the walk-in minutes and two parts of the gear list were drawn with a palette colour that does not exist, and now use the muted text colour. Hand-off: [docs/refactor-progress.md](docs/refactor-progress.md).
- **Finish setting up asks three more things** (owner note: "What else could we add to finish setting up that is important for the app and users?"): Home's *Finish setting up* card now also asks for your **home city** (opens the profile editor on it, outlined), to **connect with a climbing partner** (opens Find partners; a request you sent counts) and to **join a group** (opens Groups › Discover). The two social rows show only to a signed-in account once its read has answered. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Classic climbs on every area page, and Classics of your state** (owner notes on classic climbs, 2026-10-29): every area above a wall — a crag of walls, a region, a state — now lists up to ten *Classic climbs here* from everything under it (0390 `area_classics`), each naming its wall. Logbook › Challenges' *Regional Classics* card, seed-only until now, becomes *Classics of <your home state>* for a signed-in climber (0390 `state_classics`, at most 100 a state), and those climbs carry a *State classic* chip. The classic flags and the state lists are written state by state by `scripts/pipeline/flag-classics.mjs`. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Route text no longer repeats the pages it was researched from** (owner note: "Are we making sure we aren't copying the text of our app from other sources?", follow-up to #2757): new `npm run audit:copied-prose -- --live` (#2976) checked the live text of every route our research cited a page for: 1,469 routes, 7,730 pages. It found 1,657 strings on 690 routes that repeated 8+ words of a cited page. Those were all written before the gate. Every one was reworded in our own words, with the same facts and numbers. The rewrites are checked in (`audits/copied-prose-live/`), and the writes are paced and gated on database health. **The writes are partial:** 114 columns landed on 2026-10-29 before the database stopped answering. `node scripts/oneoff/copied-prose-live/apply-batches.mjs b01 … b24` resumes them, and it is idempotent. The legal, Help and tour copy was searched separately, and none was found copied.
- **The résumé headline has its own Edit** (owner note: an edit button near each section, follow-up to #2786): on your own résumé a set headline shows a small Edit beside it that opens edit mode with the cursor in the headline box. The header Edit opens the profile editor, which does not hold the headline. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Taps show at once: optimistic updates for the writes that made a climber wait** (owner note: "Optimistic ui updates"): a census of all 218 write call sites (`scripts/oneoff/measure-pessimistic-writes.mjs`) found the quick taps that only moved once the server answered. Now drawn on the tap and put back if the write fails: posting, replying to, editing, deleting and moderator-removing a comment (a just-sent one reads *Sending…* with no menu until it has an id), event RSVPs (the event and the invite list), declining an event invite, accepting or declining a crew invite, accepting or declining a crew join request, withdrawing a vouch, a group post's reaction / pin / edit on a real group, *List me here* at a crag (button and count), removing a crag listing, deleting a saved search, a topo caption or photo description, withdrawing a crew email invite and removing a pairing suggestion. Two existing optimistic taps did NOT roll back: a failed *Save to My Objectives* left the star lit, and a failed list edit (rename, public switch, add/remove a climb) stayed on screen because the refetch returned the same array and the copy effect never re-ran; both restore now. Kept waiting on purpose (the server decides the outcome, or it is a form): joining a group (may need approval), accepting a group invite, approving all requests, submissions to review, uploads, reports, guide forms, policy acceptance and account deletion. The shared helper is `patchQueries()` in `lib/queryClientRef.js`.
- **Classic climbs here, on every crag page** (owner note: a list of classic climbs like Mountain Project's): a leaf area's page leads its climbs with *Classic climbs here*: up to eight climbs flagged classic, rated 3+ guidebook stars, or rated 3.5+ by our climbers, best first. Like Mountain Project's area classics it is chosen by quality ratings, but only from our own, not copied from their list. An area with none still shows *Top rated here*. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Saved climbs no longer read as "Nothing here yet" while they load** (owner note on sharing and deleting objectives, follow-up to #2760): a signed-in climber's objectives come back as ids first and the climbs' details second; on a slow database Logbook › Objectives said *Nothing here yet — open a climb and tap Save* in between, Home's tile read *0 routes* and Recent condition reports said *Save a climb to your objectives*. The list now says *Loading N climbs…* (or *Couldn't load N of the climbs on this list* when the read fails), the tile counts the saved ids, and removing one objective no longer blanks the rest while the climb lookup refetches (`useRoutesByIds` keeps its rows). Also verified with a mocked signed-in session that the ✕, *Remove N climbs* and their confirms send the `objectives` DELETE and the climb stays gone on reload. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Auth state comes from one store** (owner note: data flow / global store, follow-up to #2422 and #2811): `useSession()` now lives in `src/AuthContext.tsx` and reads the app-wide `AuthProvider`, so App, the route page's topo and photo panels and the three guide screens share one Supabase auth listener instead of six. ARCHITECTURE.md now records the two contexts that exist (`AuthContext` app-wide, `ConfidenceCtx` around the route page) alongside `ClimberCtx`. No visible change.
- **Home's *Finish setting up* asks for Your conditions** (owner note: "For your conditions ... And also finish setting up"): a climber who has set nothing in Your conditions (no temperature, wind, humidity, sun, smoke, daylight or rain choice) gets a *Set your conditions* row that opens the Your conditions panel (`condsOpen`); it leaves the list as soon as anything is set, and waits while the account copy is still being read. See `docs/features/conditions.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Friend-activity buttons are coloured everywhere** (owner note: "Make the button on friends recent activity on the right pop out more. All grey doesn't look good", follow-up to #2756): Home's *Recent friend activity* cards and All friends' activity list now use the same amber Kudos / blue Message / green Vouch as Crew › Friends, a tint before and the solid colour once given.
- **Avalanche month ratings are editable, and an edit to a route's nested block no longer wipes the rest of it** (owner note: "Emergency and rescue isn't editable. Same with avalanche or forecast", follow-up to #2766): *Add or fix info* › Seasonal hazards gains *Avalanche danger by month*, twelve pickers (Low, Moderate, Considerable, High, Extreme, or no forecast that month) with the rating on file shown in each; the AVALANCHE FORECAST card's pencil opens it. Approving an edit to Seasonal hazards, Climate, Crowds, Partner requirements, Seasonal guidance, Emergency or Getting there used to REPLACE the stored block with the few keys the climber filled; it now merges into it (`mergeObjEdit`, `lib/objKeys.js`), as road/access/timing already did.
- **Saved is one list per section, not a stack of boxes** (owner note: "Saved areas under logbook needs to be more of a list rather than just separate boxes", follow-up to #2750): in Logbook › Saved the saved areas, peaks and routes are rows divided by a thin line inside their section's card, where each row used to be its own bordered box. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Dropdowns are easy to tap, app-wide** (owner note: "On route beta, it's difficult to click the dropdown to view more info, audit the whole app"): every dropdown toggle in the app — 70 of them, ROUTE BREAKDOWN's rows first — is drawn at least 44px tall (47 were 21-40px), 19 dropdowns that never said they were one now carry `aria-expanded` (Calendar's *Plan the day*, the area tree's expanders, a crew member's chip, a list's state groups, the hourly forecast), and ROUTE BREAKDOWN's do-nothing *Tap for detail* chip is a plain caption. New build guard `check:dropdown-targets`; hand-run probe `scripts/oneoff/probe-dropdowns.mjs` taps every dropdown at its centre in Chrome. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **White text on blue boxes, the rest of them** (owner note: "I don't like the blue text on blue boxes, change text to white", follow-up to #2752): #2752 missed 71 style objects whose blue fill and blue text sit behind a condition (the selected route tab, Find partners / Join a crew, Logbook and Groups sort chips, the units switch, friend buttons and more) and every case where the blue box is on a WRAPPER and the blue text on a child (the Ranks "You're #N" card and your own row, the partner match % and Shared objectives box, Compatibility with you, Your home turf, the start-a-crew place card, the log form's intro, the group form's options). `Pill` and the log form's `chipSt` now draw blue-on-blue white through `blueOnBlue()` in `lib/contrast.js`, as do the season calendar's months, the sun-vote buttons, the contributions Confirming badge and the risk-tolerance options. New browser walk `check:blue-on-blue` (render-guards.yml) reads the rendered colours on every tab, route tab and overlay, so a blue label on a blue box cannot come back.
- **Every popup is proven to be a page** (owner note: popups fill the whole screen, nothing behind them scrolls, a clear ✕ and Back; follow-up to #2728): new browser walk `check:overlay-fullscreen` opens all 68 openable popups at phone width, samples 21 points on each for the app showing through, and wheels 2,000px inside each to prove the app behind stays put. 66 pass; the ⋯ menu and the delete-account confirm keep their small shape by design. Runs in CI beside `check:overlay-scroll` and `check:pinned-dismiss`, which also passed on all 68.
- **Requests never cut text off with "…"** (owner note: "Requests shouldn't have 3 dots on crew tab"): on Crew › Requests a crew join request's name, *You asked to join …'s crew* and the names under Waiting on others were clipped to one line ending in "…" — invisible with the demo's short names, but a real climber's full name beside Accept / Withdraw ran out of room. They wrap now, as the crew name beside them already did; the screen has no ⋯ menu anywhere.
- **Thin descent and approach, follow-up** (owner note: "Descent is still thin on many routes. Also thicken up approach for thin routes", follow-up to #2772): six thin duplicate WA routes and two area entries filed as routes were deleted (nothing pointed at them), and two Rainier routes filed under Glacier Peak were moved. A browser-read retry of the skipped WA rows found sources for only 1 in 35, so the rest stay contribute prompts. Out of state, about 1,700 routes now carry a researched descent and approach: all 17 marquee peaks, the next tier (Slesse, the Bugaboos, the Longs cirque, Huntington and Tuckerman and others), every primary-mountaineering route a source describes, 893 ice and mixed routes across 25 states and provinces, and 217 trad routes on peaks (the Bugaboos, the High Sierra, the Tetons, the Rockies and others). After the owner reopened the item, 1,509 more alpine-tagged routes gained a researched approach (and a descent wherever a source states one, 608 of them): 44 aid, 845 trad on cliffs, 558 sport and 62 top-rope. A one-pitch route's descent is left blank unless a source states it. A read-only census of what stays thin outside Washington (almost all of it the untagged bulk crag import) is in the README. See `audits/thin-descent-approach/README.md`.
- **Requests: event invites show on their own, only while they still need you; every incoming card says when it was sent** (owner note: "Let's explore requests, what else can we do for it?", follow-up to #2720/#2933): the Event invites section sat inside the group-requests block, so an event invite with nothing else beside it never rendered, and it listed past events and ones already answered Going. It now reads the same list as Home's Alerts, counts in the Menu and Crew badges, and prints the date in your date format. Crew invites, crew join requests, group invites, group join requests and event invites each say *Sent 2d ago*, as friend requests already did. → [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md)
- **No Fire map on a world, country or state page in the seed path either** (owner note: "Fire map shouldn't be on the first climbs screen", follow-up to #2726): the seed-path Fire map card in `ClimbMatch.jsx` now starts at the range, the same rule `AreaPage` follows on the DB path.
- **InciWeb opens the climb's own state** (owner note: external links should open the page, not a search, follow-up to #2711): the *Active fires — InciWeb* row under Weather & mountain forecasts now opens InciWeb's incident table filtered to the route's state (e.g. Washington's active fires), not the national home page. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Profile photos re-audited; a new account's first Save works** (owner note, "profile photos went away", follow-up to #2492): every stored avatar and strip photo (8, 7 accounts) loads for a stranger. Edit profile now suggests a username when there is none (signup sets none, and Save stayed disabled), and a taken username no longer drops the rest of the edit. `lib/EditProfileScreen.jsx`, `saveEdit`.
- **Speed profile example names its unit**: editing SPEED PROFILE, the box's example reads *e.g. 1000 ft/hr of gain* (*e.g. 300 m/hr of gain* in metric) instead of a bare *e.g. 1000*, so the number cannot be read as feet climbed.
- **A climb shared with a friend is proven to arrive and open** (owner's sharing note, follow-up to #2700): `check:message-delivery` now has a second real account share a climb into the first's Messages; the chat must show *Open the climb ›* and tapping it must open that climb's page in place. Passed 15/15 against the live database. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Crag protection lives in RACK** (owner note: "remove protection and just move that info to the rack section. Consolidate and remove duplicates", follow-up to #2705): on sport, trad and aid routes the *Bolts* count and the *Protection* rating (G/PG-13/R/X) moved out of GEAR & SAFETY into the top of the RACK card directly above it, each still fillable when missing. → [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **The desktop rail's Alerts card is Home's Alerts list** (owner note: "integrate it all into one button and view", follow-up to #2734): the rail counted unread notifications only, so a desktop Home showed *Alerts · 3 new* beside a card reading *Alerts*. Both now read one list, `_alerts`, built once in `App`: requests, invites, unread chats and things to confirm first, then notifications. See [app-shell-and-layout](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **An uploaded topo can be zoomed, panned and paged through** (owner note: "Will uploaded tools [topos] be scrollable?"): the enlarged topo was a still image capped at 58vh, so a tall wall shrank until its belays could not be read. It now zooms to 6x (pinch, ctrl/trackpad wheel, double-tap, − / + / Fit, or + − 0 keys) and pans (drag, wheel or arrow keys once zoomed), with the line and markers kept at their size (`TopoZoomPhoto`). At 1x a swipe or wheel still scrolls the sheet. ‹ › and *Topo 2 of 3* step through every topo on the route without closing it. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Ice, mixed and aid grades show wherever grades do** (owner note: "When you click on top grades, there's no grades for ice or mixed", follow-up to #2685): onboarding asked for them, but partner rows, the crew invite list, the résumé and the share card printed only Sport / Trad / Boulder, and an ice climber with only a WI grade was still told to *Add your climbing grades*. All now read `profileGradeRows` / `profileDiscGrades`; partner rows read `climb_grades` (`usePartnerClimbGrades` for RPC rows). See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Route map: ascent and descent drawn apart; hand-drawn lines and off-route pins removed** (owner note, 2026-10-25 GPX item): a stored "track" of fewer than 10 points (74 routes, points ~1.4 km apart) is no longer drawn as a recording. A recorded track is split at its summit pin (`splitAtSummit`, `lib/track.js`) — the way up a wide blue line, the way down a thin orange one over it, with *Ascent* / *Descent* in the legend, or *Descent not recorded in this track* for a recording that ends on top (278 of the 425 drawn tracks). 168 waypoints on 119 routes were removed: 73 that sat off the route's own recorded track by more than the per-type tolerance, and 95 drive-in campground pins 3 km or more off the route on routes with no track (three rounds, rollbacks `scripts/rollback-offroute-waypoints-2026-10-29.json`, `-round2-`, `-round3-`; every drive-in campground among them is still listed under CAMPING & BIVY). The list of peak routes with no complete trailhead-summit-trailhead track is `research-data/routes-without-complete-gpx-2026-10-29.csv`. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md) Kings Peak / Anderson Pass and Twin Peaks via Broads Fork gained a way in, researched with Road Trip Ryan and a second page each.
- **Area search no longer times out on common words** (owner note, follow-up to #2683): the Climbs tab's Areas search (route finder and an area page's *Search <area>*) said *Couldn't search areas* for words like "rock" or "the" in California and Colorado, because `areas_in_subtree` normalised each hit's name six to eight times while ranking and ran past the 3 s statement timeout. 0389 normalises it once: same results, 6–20× faster. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **"V4-5", "WI3-4", "M5-6", "A2-3" show one grade too** (owner note: "I still see routes that have 2 grades", follow-up to #2670): every range on the boulder, ice, mixed, aid and YDS scales (~15,700 rows) now shows its top grade, the one search and filters already use, in the header, list rows, chips, Crux grade tile and an area's rock-difficulty span. The route page adds *Rated V4-5; shown at the harder of the two grades, V5.* "5.10a/b" stays as it is. The 20 WA scrambles whose headline sat below the top of their stored range were researched; West Twin Needle went from Class 2 to Class 4, and the other 19 keep their grade because the harder moves are optional. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Your week on a crew card saves to your profile** (owner note: "Does availability on profile get fed into the app for other instances when it's relevant?", follow-up to #2610): tapping your row of a crew card's *Step 1 · Your typical week* grid only changed that screen and reset on reload, so crewmates never saw it. It now saves to your profile's availability, which crewmates, partner search and the match % read. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **The log buttons at the foot of a route page have room** (owner note): *Quick log* / *Full log* sat 12px under ROUTE STATS' footnote and 30px above *More on*, so they read as part of the stats card. Now 30px both sides. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Numbers say what they measure, round 2** (owner note, follow-up to #2649): a way in's editor heads its distance / gain / hours boxes *Trailhead to the base, one way*, and its card prints the distance *one way* like the route's own figure; the log form's Approach / Climb / Descent time each name where the leg starts and ends; `uDistMi` keeps two decimals under 1 km in metric. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **ROUTE BREAKDOWN boxes: more in each, and opening one no longer hides it** (owner note, resumes #2656): tapping a box in a short breakdown pushed the section past its 620px fold, which then folded over the box just opened; `Capped` now stays open after a tap inside. An open pitch drops empty Length / Bolts tiles instead of showing dashes. Research pass on the 61 routes whose boxes were mostly one-liners: 19 boxes on 8 routes rewritten with terrain, route-finding and hazards (copy-checked, verified on re-read); the rest had no readable source. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Notifications: archive or clear one without swiping** (owner note, follow-up to #2663): each bell row has a ⋯ that opens Archive and Clear under it, and an archived row has Clear beside Restore, so mouse, keyboard and screen-reader users can act on a single notification. See docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md.
- **Edit a route field: Start from this text** (owner note, follow-up to #2635): the *On the app now* box on a text or long-text field has a button that copies the stored text into the box, so a climber can fix one word of an approach or descent instead of retyping it; a copy sent back unchanged is not a suggestion. See `docs/features/route-quality-control.md`.
- **Finish setting up: the last two rows say what to do too** (owner note, follow-up to #2612): *Save your first objective* used to drop you on the Climbs tab with nothing said; Climbs now opens under a card naming the task, why, and "open a climb and tap Save", and that Save button is outlined on the route page. *Verify your email* now lands on an outlined panel with a line saying what the ✓ means, scrolled into view even while Profile is still loading.
- **A peak list counts a peak you STOOD ON** (owner note on badges, follow-up to #2636): on the Bulgers, 14ers, ADK 46 and the other peak lists, an alpine, mountaineering, scrambling, ice or mixed log counts only when it records the summit (a Summit tick or *Also reached the summit*), as the alpine-summit badges already did, so a list card and its finisher badge agree; a crag climb on a list peak still counts when sent. *Hide done* on a peak list now hides the peaks you have done. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Seed census, round 2** (owner note, 2026-10-25 whole-app audit): a signed-in climber's Share-a-climb sheet no longer offers seed climbers as recipients; Home's Recent condition reports and the notification-opened report read the real area name for a database route instead of a blank one.
- **Home greeting appears once, already named** (owner note, 4th pass on the name flash): on a device that has not yet remembered your name, the greeting used to show your sign-up name ("Nathan") until your profile loaded, then your profile name ("Nate"). It now stays invisible until the profile arrives. → [profile-and-settings](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md)
- **check:field-renders no longer scans every route 61 times per run** (0387 + 0388, owner, 2026-10-28; database only, no screen changes): the guard samples 8 rows per enriched column (`<col>=not.is.null&order=id.asc&limit=8`) on every PR push; each sample walked `routes_pkey` past ~280k empty rows (~1.8 GB of buffer reads), the likeliest load behind the 2026-10-28 stall on the Micro instance. 0387 adds a partial index `routes (id) where <col> is not null` for each of its 61 columns (~100k entries total), built CONCURRENTLY on production; 0388 drops `user_reports_open_idx`, an exact copy of `user_reports_status_idx`.
- **Another climber's résumé: the friend button works like the profile's, and ← Back from a chat returns to the profile** (owner note, follow-up to #2598/#2605/#2668, 2026-10-28): the résumé's *✓ Connected* button closed the résumé and did nothing; it now reads *✓ Friend* and is disabled, with the profile's other labels. A chat opened with *Message* from a profile or résumé goes ← Back to that profile and résumé instead of the bare tab.
- **BETA and PRO TIPS: compact ✎ Edit** (owner note, follow-up to #2592): their full-size *Suggest a fix* buttons are now the small header *✎ Edit* KNOWN HAZARDS and every other route card use; same Suggest-a-fix form on Beta. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Same-named sibling areas refused, and #2835's two ice copies folded (0385)** (owner note, North Cascades duplicates): *The Smoke Bluffs Ice* (Squamish) is gone, its 3 climbs on the crags they climb (Wall Area, Bughouse Heights); Durango's ice *Pine River* is now *Pine River Area › Pine River Trail*. `refuse_duplicate_area` now refuses a child named like its sibling even with no coordinate — the gap both copies, and the Cascade River bucket before them, came through. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **A gear count stays a count** (owner note, 2026-10-28: "number of alpine draws can't be text right? How does that feed into the app? Same with rack"; 0384): the rack is one counted list, `rack_items` rows ({item, qty, sizes}), from the form to the RACK card. Add a climb's quickdraw/screw/cam/nut steppers submit rows (`rackItems`, written by `approve_new_route` since 0384) instead of text lines; the rack box is *Rack notes* for what a count cannot say, and the *Alpine draws* chip beside the Alpine draws number box is gone. The edit sheet's rack steppers start from the stored counts, keep the rows they cannot state as removable chips, and submit rows plus text (not "Alpine draws: Qty ×8"); its Alpine draws stepper shows only where # Alpine draws has no box. A corrected quickdraw count now reaches a crag's RACK card (it lost to rack_items on 938 sport routes), and off-crag RACK cards, the PDF sheet and the review queue read the counted rows too (11 alpine/mixed/ice routes whose only rack was rows printed the generic rack). → [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Route finder Filters: Discipline is the first row again** (owner note): the Distance away block added in 0369 sat above it, so on a phone the discipline chips were back below the fold. Discipline is first, as on Mountain Project's Route Finder. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Route jump pills name the section, nothing else** (owner note, follow-up to #2564, 2026-10-28): on Safety the fire fold's pill read "Active fire nearby4 fires" and the float-plan fold added a second pill, "FLOAT PLANCommitting objectiv…"; `SectionJumps` now skips a heading that wraps another and reads a `data-jump-label` title when a fold marks one. Re-measured: every pill on every tab of Disappointment Cleaver and Outer Space lands 71px down, below the 59px bar.
- **Crews tab: your crew comes first** (owner note, follow-up to #2531, 2026-10-28): *Climb again with a past crew* moved from above your crews to below them, beside *Past crews*; a crew card shows its first two join requests, then *Show N more requests*, with one help line under the heading instead of one per request. → [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md)
- **Security rules check the signed-in user once per query, not once per row** (0383, owner, 2026-10-28; database only, no screen changes): after the database stalled (40 s reads) and was restarted, the 232 `public` RLS policies Supabase's performance advisor flagged (`auth_rls_initplan`) now call `(select auth.uid())` / `auth.jwt()` / `auth.role()`, so Postgres evaluates the call once per statement. Who can read or write what is unchanged — a before/after diff of all 271 policies matched exactly once the wrap is removed. Production was applied one table per transaction (a single transaction deadlocked against live traffic) and 0383 recorded; the advisor's 60 `multiple_permissive_policies` warnings remain.
- **A climber profile's tab row: the scrollbar has its own lane** (owner note, follow-up to #2549): iOS Safari can ignore the CSS that hides the row's sideways scrollbar, so it was drawn over the selected tab's blue underline. The row now keeps 6px below the underline for it and 14px past the last tab, which keeps the profile's up-and-down scrollbar off the Objectives underline (`FullProfile`). → [profile-and-settings](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md)
- **Open in navigation app opens AT the climb** (owner note): Gaia GPS's *Open app →* opens the installed app at the route (it used to go to Gaia's download page); CalTopo, Google Maps and Apple Maps all land on the route's map area; AllTrails/USGS (home-page-only links) dropped (`lib/navLinks.js`). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Forecast at key points: less text under each day** (owner note, 2026-10-28): the NWS and MET comparison rows print only on a day that service differs by 6° or more, temperatures only; the hourly "At/During" explainer and the "Cross-check several forecasts" caption are gone. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Share a crew by link** (0381, owner, Gora audit 2026-10-28): a crew card has *Copy share link*; the link opens Join a crew with the crew pinned on top, showing where, when, how full and first names only, with *Ask to join* (or *Sign up to join*). The organiser can turn the link off, and it stops working after the climb date. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Requests shows event invites** (owner's yes, 2026-10-28): Crew › Requests has an *Event invites* section with Going and Decline. Before, an event invite reached only the bell. `ClimbMatch.jsx`, `lib/db.js`.
- **Résumé: a past mountaineering climb no longer offers Lead or Follow** (owner note, follow-up to #2491): *Add a past climb* now offers Mountaineering the same Summit / Completed / Solo as Scrambling, matching the owner's walk-up rule and the logbook's own ticks (`RESUME_STYLES`). → [profile-and-settings](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md)
- **Lint backlog cleared to zero** (owner note, strict ESLint + TypeScript; tooling, no screen changes): the 385 findings held in `eslint-suppressions.json` since #2487 (248 unused variables, 137 hook dependency arrays) are fixed and the file is gone; `npm run lint` fails if it comes back, and fails any `eslint-disable` that does not name its rule and a reason. Dependency arrays that were omitted on purpose keep their array and say why on the line; no effect or fetch fires at a different time.
- **Free this weekend leads Find partners; crew cards say spots left** (owner, Gora audit 2026-10-28): your own *Free this weekend* switch now sits at the top of *Climbers on ClimbMatch*, climbers who are free this weekend are listed first, and Join-a-crew cards read "3 of 4 · 1 spot left". See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **A take-down leaves every open screen at once** (owner note, the Supabase Realtime item, follow-up to #2507): RLS kept the UPDATE that hides a removed comment or group post from other readers, so it stayed on their screen until a refetch. 0380 broadcasts the bare id on the public `moderation` topic when `moderation` leaves 'visible', and `lib/live.js` treats it as a DELETE (docs/features/route-page.md, groups.md).
- **Conditions: dew needs a clear calm night, crags say when models split on rain, alpine routes with no height read the ground's** (follow-up to the conditions audit, 2026-10-29): the dew cap no longer fires on sunny desert mornings; a crag day the forecast models disagree on rain says so; the 4,903 alpine routes with no stated height use the forecast's ground height, so their freezing-level flags can fire. See [conditions](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Emergency & rescue: 579 counties** (eighth pass): the last nine held county pages read in Chrome; Alameda, Hood River, Los Alamos, Navajo and Pawnee added, Bucks, Nelson VA, Saguache and Washington AR refused (no labelled line on an official page).
- **Emergency & rescue: 574 counties** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, seventh pass): the 23 county pages earlier passes could not load, read in Chrome (11 added: Haines, Mercer NJ, Mora, Solano, Fauquier, Lucas, Chisago, Dare, Broward, Cambria, Clatsop), and a fresh search of the ~50 busiest uncovered counties (4 added: Montrose CO, Sanpete UT, Llano TX, Gallatin MT). 72.7% of routes now sit in a county with a line; the rest publish no labelled line (refusals in `docs/codebase/rescue-dispatch-numbers.md`).
- **Emergency & rescue: 559 counties** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, sixth pass): 9 counties whose pages had blocked scripted reads, read in a browser (Blue Earth, Hays, Moffat, Lemhi, Stearns, Dolores, Champaign, Portage, Prince George's), and 58 from the 1-9 route tail. Every uncovered county holding 10 or more routes has now been researched at least once; 71.3% of routes sit in a county with a line, and the rest are counties that publish no labelled line (911 guidance plus the Edit pencil).
- **Emergency & rescue: 492 counties; Hueco Tanks and Spearfish covered** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, fifth pass): El Paso County TX (Hueco Tanks) shows the sheriff Communications Section line, and Lawrence County SD (Spearfish Canyon) its PSAP line. Both were re-read on the county pages. 39 more counties come from the 10-24 route tail. 71.2% of routes now sit in a county with a line.
- **Emergency & rescue: 451 counties; Red River Gorge and Grafton NH covered** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, fourth pass): Lee County KY (Red River Gorge) now shows its 911 centre line, and Grafton County NH (Franconia, Cannon, Rumney) its sheriff Communications Center line, both re-read on the county pages. 41 more counties, from the 25-59 route tail. 70.3% of routes now sit in a county with a line.
- **Emergency & rescue: 410 counties** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, third pass): 13 counties whose pages had refused scripted reads were read in a browser: Buncombe, Chatham, Bedford and Clarke VA, Carson City, Lehigh, McLennan, Williamson, Milwaukee, La Crosse, Marathon, Passaic and New Castle. 68.0% of routes now sit in a county with a line.
- **Emergency & rescue: 397 counties** (owner note, Castleton follow-up, second pass): 43 more counties, from the 103 uncovered ones with 60+ routes that no earlier pass had reached and from blocked pages re-read in a browser. Among them are Carroll NH (North Conway), Contra Costa, Napa, San Benito, Baltimore County, Dane, Yellowstone and Sweetwater. 67.5% of routes now sit in a county with a line (from 64.7%). The refusals are in `docs/codebase/rescue-dispatch-numbers.md`.
- **Emergency & rescue: 354 counties, the busiest uncovered ones first** (owner note, Castleton follow-up): a census put every route in its census county, and found 61.6% of routes sat in a county with a line. 21 of the counties with the most uncovered routes were then read on their own agencies' pages: Lancaster and Berks PA, Rockland NY, San Luis Obispo and Amador CA, Anchorage and Juneau, Custer SD, Jefferson MT, Bannock ID, Hancock ME, Strafford NH and others. The census, and the counties refused (office lines only, or blocked pages), are in `docs/codebase/rescue-dispatch-numbers.md`.
- **Emergency & rescue: 333 counties now, from Colorado's and Arkansas's state 911 lists and a browser re-read** (owner note, Castleton follow-up): 34 Colorado counties come from the state PUC's list of each 911 centre's non-emergency line (Chaffee, Gunnison, Summit, Eagle, Pitkin, San Miguel, La Plata, San Juan and more). 57 Arkansas counties come from the 911 Board's "PSAP Non-Emergency 24/7" sheet. 20 counties whose sites refused scripts were read in a browser: Teton WY, Clark NV (Red Rock), San Diego, Kern, Tuolumne, Coconino, Yavapai, Taos, Stevens WA, Blaine and Kootenai ID, among others. 43 more came from county pages (San Juan County, Utah, for Indian Creek, through the state Price dispatch centre) in Maine, Maryland, Montana, Minnesota, the Carolinas, Texas and elsewhere. The Oregon, Pennsylvania and Montana state lists were read and refused, and the reasons are in `docs/codebase/rescue-dispatch-numbers.md`.
- **Emergency & rescue has a verified local dispatch line in 179 counties** (owner note, "Castleton needs SAR for near Moab", follow-up to #2485): the Safety card's verified non-emergency dispatch list (`lib/sarByCounty.js`) grew from 3 Utah counties to 179 counties in 27 states (all of West Virginia from the state's own list), each one a county whose sheriff, county or dispatch centre publishes a line for that purpose, each read on the agency's own page (evidence: `docs/codebase/rescue-dispatch-numbers.md`). Washington County, UT's (435) 634-5730 is back: the sheriff's own page lists it as non-emergency dispatch. A stored county written as "Okanogan County, WA" now finds its line and no longer prints "..., WA, Washington". See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **The desktop website is checked for sideways scrolling** (owner note, desktop layout): `check:overflow -- --width 1280` walks every tab, the route page and every openable overlay at desktop width, and `render-guards.yml` runs it beside the 390px phone walk. The rest of the desktop programme (shell, per-screen layouts, web-only features) was already merged; public URLs stay held. → [app-shell-and-layout](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
- **Ranks' top 100 is exact at any size** (owner note, the Ranks audit): `leaderboard()` returned 500 climbers ordered by climbs logged, and each board was ranked from those, so past 500 a leader on Vouched or Send points could be missed. Since 0379 the server ranks every board itself and returns each climber in any board's top 100, and your row's `stats.standing` carries your true *#N of M* even when you sit past #100. See `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **Real database examples to test with** (owner note 2026-10-28, "swap the seed examples with real database examples"): `scripts/manual-test-accounts.mjs` gains `link <email>`/`unlink <email>`, which put the four real example accounts into a real account's own screens (a friend, a request, messages, a crew to agree a day in, a crew invite, groups, an event invite, a vouch, a log to confirm), all authored by the example accounts. Upcoming crews and events are dated from the day it runs, and writes are paced. The seed examples stay for guests only. See `docs/codebase/example-accounts.md`.
- **Smoke on the Fire map** (owner note 2026-10-28, "Updated smoke overlay on the map?"): a *Smoke* layer paints the national surface-smoke forecast in the EPA air-quality colours (nothing under 9 µg/m³), and ‹ › step it 3 h at a time through the next two days, as Gaia GPS's and CalTopo's smoke-forecast layers do. A failed read says smoke was not loaded rather than showing a clear map. → [maps](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md)
- **Rain amounts, not just a chance, and a wet morning explained** (owner note 2026-10-28): the alpine card's DAY BY DAY gains a Rain cell (amount, wet hours and window, peak chance beneath) and its showers flag states the amount and window beside the %; the crag score says "Rock likely wet until 11 AM" when a dry afternoon makes a day Good above red morning tiles, and each tile's spoken label gives its dryness. Rain chances now read "hourly chance up to 42%" (NWS's higher figure covers 12 hours), and the alpine "models disagree" flag covers rain when the models split by 0.5" or more. See [conditions](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Small links and buttons are easier to tap** (owner note 2026-10-28, "Some clicks links are small"): a walk of 100 screens found 1,051 controls with a side under 44px (Apple's minimum), 62 of them under 24px. A touch that lands on nothing now goes to the nearest small control within 44px (`lib/tapSlop.js`, guarded by `check:tap-slop`); a nearer bigger control, a mouse, a keyboard and maps keep their own taps. Dropdowns are now 44px tall; the waypoint map picker's layer buttons, the fire map's layer chips and the Reports condition rows grew to 34-36px. 588 of 964 small controls now reach 44px (none did). See [app shell](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **Long text opens instead of stopping** (owner note 2026-10-28, "text would go on but it stops"): a walk of every tab, route sub-tab and overlay (`scripts/oneoff/measure-clipped-text.mjs`) plus a read of all ~210 ellipsis and line-clamp sites. Nearly all of them sit in a row that opens the full thing, or on a collapsed section's one-line summary. The ones that were dead ends now open or wrap. Group posts in *For you* and group blurbs on Discover cards get *See more*. The area page's ABOUT gets *Read more* / *Show less* past 600 characters. The area suggest-an-edit *Now:* line gets *Show all*. The route name and area line in the route header wrap instead of clamping. The alpine DAY BY DAY freezing level, parking names, the Settings email, email invites, pending-request names and the map's crag title also wrap.
- **The crag's parking pin can be added and moved** (owner note, 2026-10-28): the shared pin every route at a crag drives to (`areas.parking_*`) is now a field in the area page's Suggest an edit (0378, `parking_pin`), with *Add the parking pin* / *Move the pin* in GETTING THERE, held for the owner like every area edit. The owner's queue now shows the real current value for every area field (it said "nothing" for five of them). See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **An arrow points to the base of the climb** (owner note 2026-10-28): under *How far am I from the base?*, *Point the way with your compass* turns an arrow with the phone toward the start and names the turn (*To your left*, *Straight ahead*). Phone compasses read magnetic north, so the heading is corrected to true north with the World Magnetic Model (`lib/declination.js`, ~15° in the Cascades), checked against NOAA's 100 test values by `check:declination`. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Each approach card describes only its own way in** (owner note 2026-10-28, data): 92 routes whose cards use alternate/fallback wording were read; 23 narrate another way in inside a card, and that text moves to the other way in's own card, or to a new card on a route with no switcher (`scripts/oneoff/approach-cards-one-way-each.mjs`, plan and before-file beside it). All 23 written and re-read by 2026-10-30; the audit's CARD_TWO test finds none of them. The Trip-plan road-note move finished on all 104 routes, with four leftover notes rewritten (`move-cal-road-notes-tidy.mjs`).
- **Crews have a Rides section, and profiles show the hardest climbs actually logged** (owner's Shredder audit, 2026-10-28): on a crew card each confirmed member can say *I'm driving from North Seattle · 3 seats free* or *Needs a ride from Bellevue*; only the crew's confirmed members see it (0377 `crew_rides`). A climber's profile adds *Hardest logged* per discipline, from the logs you can see, under the grades they typed. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md` and `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Season chart verdicts are pinned** (2026-10-28): `check:conditions-score` now holds eight measured months, so a Canadian July or August cannot slip back to "Off season" for sport, trad or bouldering, and a desert midsummer stays off. See `docs/guards/honesty-claims.md`.
- **Mount Logan's two ways in, long terrain text, the month-by-month link, road notes out of Trip plan** (owner note 2026-10-28): Banded Glacier's default approach card described both Thunder Creek and Easy Pass while the page shows one way in at a time; the row is now Easy Pass throughout (the most-used way, which its road, plan and stats already described) with Thunder Creek a switchable way in carrying its own trailhead, pins, camps and the recorded track (`scripts/oneoff/logan-banded-two-ways-in.mjs`, before-file beside it). `audit:multi-approach` gains `CARD_TWO`: a card whose own text narrates another way in, never hidden by a verdict. An itinerary `cal` note's road and closure sentences move into the road block GETTING THERE shows (`scripts/oneoff/move-cal-road-notes.mjs`); the rest stays in Trip plan. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Why go this way** (owner note 2026-10-28): a route with several ways in badges the most used one in the picker as well as on its card, and each other way in can say in one line when a party takes it instead (`whyThisWay`), researched route by route. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Climbers are asked where they belayed** (2026-10-28): on a route you have logged, ROUTE BREAKDOWN says how many pitches still have no belay station and offers *Add belay stations*, opening the pitch editor at the first. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Your counted catches stop re-asking the database** (2026-10-28): `useMyTrustCounts` keyed its query on the climb-logs query's `dataUpdatedAt`, so every refetch of your logs, even one returning the same rows, fired a new `my_trust_counts` call. That was 31,713 calls in three days, the database's largest single cost during the afternoon it went unhealthy. It now keys on the user alone and is refreshed by every climb-log write through `logReaders`.
- **Full trip time takes the section header; guests see "Sign in to add a comment" on a pitch** (2026-10-28): the last Plan-tab title in its own style now matches Trip plan and the rest, and a guest's pitch comment no longer pretends to post (it was kept in that tab only). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Camps are pins on the route map** (owner note 2026-10-25): `npm run audit:camp-pins` checks every camp CAMPING & BIVY lists for a pin, a coordinate, and a place on the recorded track; `scripts/oneoff/add-camp-pins.mjs` pinned the camps it could prove from official NPS/USFS camp points, other routes' pins, OSM, USGS GNIS and research, each checked against the ground and the track. A stored line made of pins is no longer drawn as a recorded track once pins are added off it (`lineIsBuiltFromPins`, 37 routes). See `docs/guards/camping.md` and `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **ROPEWORK says three things** (owner's note, 2026-10-25): rope length, whether the pitches link, and simul-climbing, each *Not stated* when absent; the rope note is no longer printed in full and the crag Overview's *Rope notes* fact is now *Pitches link* and *Simul-climbing*. Nothing in the database changed. See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `lib/ropework.js`.
- **Belay stations researched** (owner note 2026-10-25, data): 415 pitches on 160 routes now say where the belay is (*Belay station* in each pitch's Pitch gear card), every sentence researched online, checked against its source by a second pass, and refused if it copies 8+ words of the source. Method, batches and rollbacks: `audits/belay-stations/`. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Directions to the trailhead box: spacing, and every button checked** (owner note 2026-10-25): the Plan tab's TRAILHEAD card put Google Maps, Apple Maps and the coordinates on one line, so on a 390px phone the coordinate shrank to "48.85…". The coordinates now have their own full-width row, with the two maps apps as equal 44px buttons beneath it, laid out like the crag and area cards. All three directions boxes share 12px × 14px padding, a 12px radius and 44px buttons. A click-every-button walk of the app (each bottom tab, the Partners modes and every route sub-tab, guest, 390px) found two broken controls. The current crumb of Ranks › By area was a button that did nothing; it is now plain text marked as the current location. ACCESS & REGULATIONS' dropdown header was a button with the confidence chip's Flag button nested inside it (invalid HTML, React warned on every Plan tab); the toggle now sits behind the row and Flag is its own control. Every other flagged control was checked by hand and works (file pickers, copy-to-clipboard, scroll-to, the 3D view, active tabs).
- **Getting there: routes with nothing at all, crag batch** (owner note 2026-10-25, data): 136 ice and drytooling routes on 25 crags (Banks Lake, Wayne's World, Exit 38, Alpental, Leavenworth, Mt. Baker, Spokane and others) now show where to park, the drive and the walk to the base. Each crag was researched once and checked a second time online, and the same checked text was written on its routes. 179 of the 328 routes that had nothing remain. Notes in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **Getting there: routes with nothing at all, peak batch** (owner note 2026-10-25, data): of the 328 WA alpine routes with neither a trailhead nor approach text, the 94 on peaks were researched; **13 now have a trailhead, a drive and an approach**, each confirmed by a second check that opened the pages (Rainier ×3, Lane Peak, Bryant Peak, Glacier Peak, Table Mountain ×2, Eldorado, Mesahchie, Mount Adams, Mount Stuart, Nooksack Tower). Most of the rest have no readable page about that route's own start. 5 routes filed on the wrong peak are noted for owner review. Notes in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **Getting there: accuracy check of existing directions, part 3 — every drive checked** (owner note 2026-10-25): batch 8 plus a re-check of the shallow first batches; 40 corrections over 83 routes. All 379 distinct driving directions have now been checked online with a second check on every change: 131 corrections over 299 routes in all. Blanca Lake now says it cannot be reached by car (Index-Galena and Beckler River roads both closed since December 2025), and the Suiattle River Road, FR 38 and Beckler River Road closures are dated on every drive that uses them. Data only; notes in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **Belay stations researched** (owner note 2026-10-25, data): 449 pitches on 162 routes now say where the belay is (*Belay station* in each pitch's Pitch gear card), every sentence researched online, checked against its source by a second pass, and refused if it copies 8+ words of the source. Method, batches and rollbacks: `audits/belay-stations/`. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Getting there: accuracy check of existing directions, part 2** (owner note 2026-10-25): batches 4–7 of the 379 distinct driving directions checked online with a second, page-fetching check; 49 corrections over 86 routes. Mowich Lake now says the road is closed at the Fairfax Bridge (SR-165) instead of describing a drive; Phelps Creek, Smithbrook and Skyline Divide carry their dated road closures; wrong turns, exits and a fee claim fixed. Data only; notes in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **Getting there: accuracy check of existing directions, part 1** (owner note 2026-10-25): 146 of the 379 distinct driving directions checked online, each correction confirmed by a second check that opened the source page; 42 corrected or filled in over 130 routes (Cascade Pass, Boston Basin, Blue Lake, Cutthroat Lake, Dingford Creek, Hannegan Pass at 5.3 miles up FR-32, Esmeralda Basin, Heliotrope Ridge and others). Data only; method in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **Planner: a route's stored approach or descent is never dropped** (owner to-do, 2026-10-28): when a route stores an approach or descent but no summit leg, the Planner used to ignore it and walk the leg from distance and gain, short on 20 of 69 such WA routes (0 hr on 6). It is now a floor under the walk model, so 27 WA routes read longer and none shorter. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Getting there: driving directions, batch 1** (owner note 2026-10-25): 64 WA alpine routes that had a trailhead card and approach prose but no drive now have researched directions to the trailhead (513 → 577 of 1,066), and the 7 Mount Robson routes' walk text ("Via the Berg Lake Trail…") is replaced with the drive to the Berg Lake Trailhead, so `check:trailhead-direction-shape --live` passes again. Data only; scripts and research in `scripts/oneoff/getting-there-directions/`, notes in `docs/guards/roads-and-access.md`.
- **On objectives leads to the climbers** (owner note, 2026-10-28): the ROUTE STATS *On objectives* tile is a button (*See who*) that opens the route's Partners tab, where an alpine route lists the climbers who saved it. Partners now says how many saved it in all, *you among them* when you did, and how many are not shown (unlisted, or blocked either way). A crag route's tile reads *Partners at this crag*, since crag partners are found by area (0300). A signed-out climber is asked to sign in. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **One section-card padding on the route page** (owner note 2026-10-25, box audit): the crag route page's section cards (CLIMATE & SEASON, the hazard and access boxes) were 13px × 15px while the alpine ones were 12px × 14px; all now use 12px × 14px. Tappable rows and tiles keep their own padding. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **High point filled where a route's own Summit pin gives it** (owner to-do, 2026-10-28): `scripts/pipeline/fill-high-point-from-summit.mjs` fills TECHNICAL STATS' *High point* from a route's Summit waypoint only after measuring it against the stored values (99.7% within 50 ft); the parent peak's elevation measured 62% and is not used. 6 routes filled; gain and distance are not derived from stored tracks, which disagree with the stored figures too often. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Climb length filled on alpine and mountaineering routes** (owner note, 2026-10-25): TECHNICAL STATS' *Climb length* was blank on 5,136 of 5,669 alpine and mountaineering routes; 3,752 now carry it, from the Mountain Project exports already on disk (`scripts/pipeline/fill-length-from-mp.mjs`, blanks only). Gain, distance and high point were researched and could not be sourced route by route. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Belay station per pitch** (owner note 2026-10-25): each pitch has a belay-station box (*Belay station*, typed in the pitch editor) above its anchor. Existing pitches have none yet; the research to fill them is not done. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Résumé: a Unique climbs count** (owner note, 2026-10-28): the stats row shows *Unique* beside *Climbs*, counting each distinct route (name and area) once. `Resume.tsx`.
- **Résumé: conditioning and hide-a-climb** (owner note, 2026-10-25, 0375): Conditioning is now a stored, editable 400-character note on your résumé (it was shown but nothing could write it); each logged climb in Experience can be hidden from the résumé in Edit (the logbook and Sends by grade keep it), with a Show-again list. Share and Export PDF include Conditioning.
- **A scramble shows no pitch count** (owner note, 2026-10-25): TECHNICAL STATS, the Climbs list row, Compare and the printed route sheet no longer print a pitch count on a scrambling route, since a scramble is never roped; the climbing sections on Plan are unchanged. → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Difficulty breakdown per discipline (owner note, 2026-10-25)**: sport, trad and bouldering each get their own radar. Sport: Power, Technical, Endurance, Fingery, Mental. Trad: Protection, Technical, Sustained, Strenuous, Commitment. Bouldering: Power, Technical, Fingery, Height, Landing. Other disciplines keep the original five. Migration 0374 widens the stored axis list (old reads keep their meaning). → `docs/features/route-page.md`
- **Log form audit** (owner to-do, 2026-10-25): each discipline offers only its own tick words (`tickTypesFor`: alpine/ice/mixed gain Lead, Follow, Hung, Fell; top rope and aid drop Onsight/Flash/Redpoint); the snow tags *Trail broken/packed* and the *Coverage* group say what they mean; section headers in the log form and trip report are bright 12.5px and each starts under a rule. Summit plus a style on one climb is built: *Also reached the summit* sets `climb_logs.summited` (migration 0373), and every summit reader, Peaks included, reads it through `summitedLog()` (see `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`, section 8). See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md` and `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **Pitch detail you can use** (owner note 2026-10-25, part): a pitch's anchor and gear each get a full-width box with an *Edit anchor & gear* button (the anchor no longer sits in a third-width tile); a pitch takes its own photos with *Add a photo of this pitch* (a route photo tagged with the pitch, `value.pitch`); a pitch with more than three comments shows the newest three and *Show N earlier comments*, and a long comment clamps at three lines with *Read more*. Belay-station research and the header audit are not done. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Résumé edit and share (owner note, 2026-10-25)**: *Edit résumé* is a full-width button at the top of the sheet; Sends by grade and Experience say they update as you log; a hand-added climb can be removed in Edit; *Share résumé* offers Text, Email, Copy link and More (`ShareVia`). Conditioning has no editor yet. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Ice, mixed and dry-tooling areas dissolve into their place; "Wrong place?" claims** (owner note, 2026-10-25): a discipline bucket (*Adirondack Ice & Mixed*, *Vail Ice*, *Dry Tooling & Ice Climbing at Exit 38*) is not an area: its crags move under the place above it and the empty bucket is deleted, with the same rule for every state. Place names that carry "ice" (*Ice Park*, *Ice Box*, *Ice Pond*) are kept. 77 of the 81 planned buckets are gone; the other 4 stay as held buckets (their crags move, the bucket is kept while it still holds something). 11 operations are HELD (a same-named place already exists, or a crag is still under them), and 350 discipline-named leaves holding 1,637 routes still need a per-route place (`scripts/data/discipline-areas-remaining.json`). Every area page has **Wrong place? Tell us where … belongs**, which files an `area_placement` contribution (0372) for the owner's *Wrong place claims* queue; nothing on the page changes until the owner moves the area and marks the claim. Frenchman Coulee was already one area (0276).
- **Crew tab lists open as pages** (owner 2026-11-19): *Start a crew with a friend* is a button that opens a page listing every friend (search, sort), and *Start crew* then lets you pick the climb; no friends are drawn on the tab. *Past crews* is a button too, and its page gained search, sort and *All · Climbed · Not logged · Archived* filters (Archived was a dropdown). *Quick view* became the *All crews* page. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Seven more lists open as pages** (owner 2026-11-19): a group's *All N members* and *All N past events*, *Friends' activity*, a climb's *All N trip reports* and *All N crews*, the bell's *Archived*, Messages' *Archived chats*, and each Challenges list. Each was a *Show all* dropdown; each page has a search box, a sort or filter chips where the list can use them (`ListPage`). See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).

- **Elevation profile under the route map, linked to it** (owner note, 2026-10-25): the route page's profile now sits directly under the map, shows height against distance with the climb and descent, and a dot moves along the recorded track as you scrub it. The old static profile in the Overview (after the photos) is gone; it had no link to the map and called max-minus-min "gain". → [route-page](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **The five top tabs lose their boxed header** (owner note, 2026-10-25): Climbs, Partners, Crew, Logbook and Ranks no longer draw the big title box that took a third of a phone screen. The title stays as a screen-reader heading, the one-line caption stays as plain text, and Ranks keeps its "?". `ScreenHero` takes `quiet` for this. → [app-shell-and-layout](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
- **Trip profile: every leg carries its time, and the numbers match across sections** (owner's note, 2026-10-25): the GETTING THERE legs (*Trailhead to summit* / *Summit to trailhead*) show time, gain and distance, and the DESCENT section opens with its own leg strip (distance, drop, time). Both read one `planTimes()` call at the Full trip time card's pace, pack, party and departure, so Full trip time, the legs and the descent cannot disagree. **TRIP PROFILE** (same note, 2026-10-25) adds a card above Full trip time with one timed row per leg between the route's own waypoints, measured on the recorded track and split from those same totals, so every leg adds up to GETTING THERE and the DESCENT strip. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Approach windows read in full, at the top of the card** (owner note, 2026-10-25): each APPROACH card puts its season/window in a full-width box above the name, wrapping and never cut (it was a truncated pill beside the name, with the sentence repeated below). Route names on the approach list, prev/next buttons and field values wrap instead of an ellipsis.
- **The Crews tab's climb picker lists every list** (owner to-do, 2026-10-25): *+ Create a crew* and *Climb again with a past crew* → *Invite for…* offer every climb on My Objectives and on each custom list, grouped under the list's name. A climb with an active crew stays listed, greyed out as *crew formed*, instead of vanishing. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Every date box says what it is** (owner to-do, 2026-10-25): the certification expiry box is captioned *Expires (optional)* (it had no visible name, so an empty box meant nothing); the list *Target day* picker shows its words beside the icon instead of a hover-only tooltip; the partner-area *Free from* box is captioned *From*. The other date inputs already carried visible labels (*Date climbed*, *Date of catch*, *Session day*, *Expires* on guide credentials, *Climb date*).
- **A wall's climb list filters and groups** (owner note, 2026-10-25): a leaf wall or crag has a **Filters** button over its list — discipline (only the disciplines on that wall) and a star minimum (2+ / 3+; unrated climbs stay in) — applied to the rows already loaded. The sort gains **By discipline**, one heading per discipline. `AreaPage` in `lib/DbAreaBrowser.jsx`.
- **A five-class route is never Mountaineering** (owner note, 2026-10-25): 83 live alpine routes with a 5.x grade still listed "mountaineering" in their discipline list, so they showed the Mountaineering chip. The list is now filtered as scrambling already was, the 83 rows are stripped (paced, re-read: 0 left), and `audit:mountaineering-rule` fails on the list too. See `docs/guards/database-and-history.md`.
- **Rich text buttons are big enough to tap** (owner to-do, 2026-10-25): the B, I, bullet and numbered-list buttons in every rich text box (the log's *How it went* and the comment boxes) are 44px, the platform minimum, up from 32px. The `compact` size is gone.
- **How you get there: one way in at a time, its own hazards** (owner's note, 2026-10-25): the card is headed HOW YOU GET THERE with a blue rail and shows only the picked way in; the "Pick yours" intro and the Plan-tab time note are gone. KNOWN HAZARDS follows the pick: each of the 128 routes with more than one way in carries a per-way-in hazard list (`audit:approach-hazards`: 0 missing, 0 identical).
- **Planner time: rest counted, and the GETTING THERE legs keep their times** (owner to-do, 2026-10-26; owner decision 2026-10-28): Full trip time adds 10 min of rest per hour of modelled moving time (`REST_PER_MOVING_HR`, `lib/planTimes.js`). The 2026-10-26 rule that took the times off *Trailhead to summit* / *Summit to trailhead* is superseded: each leg shows its time again (#2828, #2842), read from the same `planTimes()` estimate as Full trip time, so the two cannot disagree. The owner confirmed on 2026-10-28 that the times stay. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **One auth listener for the app** (owner to-do, 2026-10-25): `src/AuthContext.tsx` wraps the whole app in `main.jsx`. `useAuth()` gives any screen the signed-in `user` (or null), a `loading` flag until the stored session is read, and `login` / `logout` (the existing `lib/auth.js` calls). `useSession()` moved there too (2026-10-29) and reads the same listener, so its six callers (App, two route-page panels, the three guide screens) no longer open a subscription each; outside a provider (the react-dom/server guards) it still listens for itself. No visible change.

Newest first. Keep about 15 lines; older entries live in the PRs and the commit log.

- **Partner checks is no longer a safety skill**: Edit profile's *Safety skills* and the vouch form no longer offer it, and a profile or vouch that saved it no longer shows it. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Every route edit waits for the owner again** (0382, owner 2026-10-28, withdrawing 0325): an empty field no longer fills on the first edit and two agreeing climbers no longer change one; the edit sheet's Agree button and other climbers' waiting suggestions are gone, and its toasts and help text say ClimbMatch checks every edit. Removals keep their red mark and required reason (0351).
- **RLS reviewed live; every write policy asks who is calling, and views are read-only** (owner's to-do; 0376): all 84 public tables have row level security on, and every permissive insert/update/delete policy binds `auth.uid()` (directly or through a group/admin helper), which `check:rls` rule 4 holds. The three views (`crew_listings`, `catalog_directory`, `catalog_routes`) had kept Supabase's default write grants, and a view skips RLS, so the anon key could update or delete any crew or area through them. 0376 makes them SELECT-only (the catalog views also respect the caller's RLS now), and rule 5 fails the build on a view that clients can write.
- **A crash costs one section, not the app**: the tab content, the route page and the wide-layout page view each sit in a `SectionErrorBoundary` (`lib/SectionErrorBoundary.jsx`), so a render error shows "Something went wrong loading this section" with Try Again while the header and bottom navigation keep working. Switching tab or route clears it. `AppErrorBoundary` in `main.jsx` remains the last resort for the whole app.
- **7,656 crag walls now have a facing, and the Aspect & sun card reads it**: `areas.aspect` was filled from Mountain Project's area pages (only where a page states the facing or the sun, only where it was empty, never overwriting a held value), taking the areas that name one from about 530 to 7,656. The conditions score already used it (`route.aspect`, else the crag's `areas.aspect`), but the *Aspect & sun* card read only the route's own, so it said "No aspect recorded" under a score that counted sun; it now reads both. `check:conditions-score` holds the two together. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **My itinerary is a section header** (owner's note, 2026-10-25: "why does trip plan header look different"): on the route page's Plan tab, *My itinerary* was a card title (12px, short bar) under Trip plan; it is now the same section header (`SL`) as Trip plan, ROUTE BREAKDOWN and RAPPELS. CAMPING & BIVY keeps its ⌂ legend glyph on purpose (docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Polish round 5** (2026-10-25): disabled buttons now look disabled (dimmed, `not-allowed`, no press-shrink; a button that styles its own disabled look keeps it); Settings says *Sign out* like the Menu; 16 counts that can be 1 say "1 climb"; rating circles, comment options, Ranks ?, month nav and the add-route stepper got 44px hit areas; one text-field style `INPUT(C)` in lib/buttonStyles.js; `"#fff"` → `C.onSolid` and new `C.onBright` for dark ink on green/amber; route hero, group-post photos and guide avatars fall back on a failed load; sentence case on Safety sub-tabs and float-plan fields. → [app shell](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
- **Wall order is in for the whole catalog**: every sport/trad/top-rope wall in 60 states and provinces now lists its climbs left to right (24,683 walls), and 1,112 climbs Mountain Project had that we lacked were added in place with their facts and rewritten descriptions. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.- **Climbs › My objectives names the peak**: each row now reads where the climb is — the peak or crag and the range or area holding it — before its type, as the Logbook's objectives rows already did; it showed only the route name ("North Buttress"). The *From your objectives* suggestions use the same line. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Saved searches follow the account, and Saved has an Offline chip**: the Route finder's *Save search* kept the search only while the finder stayed open — it never reached Logbook › Saved, the Menu's count, or the database (0 rows). It now writes `saved_searches` (named for its words, filters and area; *✓ Saved* when the current search already is), and tapping one in Logbook › Saved or the finder's list reopens the finder on that area with those filters. Logbook › Saved gains an **Offline** chip (states downloaded on this device, pinned-but-not-here, saved route maps, *Manage offline areas →*), reached from the Menu's new *Offline library* tile. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Climbs › My objectives names the peak**: each row now reads where the climb is — the peak or crag and the range or area holding it — before its type, as the Logbook's objectives rows already did; it showed only the route name ("North Buttress"). The *From your objectives* suggestions use the same line. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **The route map draws only real GPS tracks** (owner, 2026-10-25): the line on a route's map is a recorded track (a recording, a trip-report download, an approved submission) or nothing. No line is drawn through the waypoints any more, a stored "track" that was only the pins joined up counts as no track (the map shows the pins, Download GPX gives the waypoints only), and a short track no longer gets a dashed stub out to the trailhead or summit pin. A track must also reach the summit (owner, 2026-10-28): one that stops more than 300 m short of a summit pin counts as no track. Routes with no summit pin keep theirs. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Résumé: an Edit on each section** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): on your own Climbing Résumé the header and the grades strip each have an Edit that opens Edit profile at that part, and *Courses & Training* and *Experience* each have one that opens that section's add form, as LinkedIn puts a pencil on each profile section. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Crag Season chart is less picky** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): a month's Prime / Good / Off rating now looks at its whole climbing day (the cool morning up to the afternoon high) instead of the afternoon high alone, and lets short summer showers through (under 16 wet days adding up to under 4 in.). July and August are now prime for sport and trad at Squamish, Lake Louise and Bon Echo; desert crags still read off in midsummer. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Swipe is discoverable, and float plans swipe too** (owner's note, 2026-10-25): the notifications Activity list, Messages and expanded Past crews now print a line saying which way to swipe, and a float plan row swipes left to delete (the Delete button stays). See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Route header names its place in the place's own case** (owner's note 2026-10-24): the line above a climb's name reads `📍 Mount Index`, as the area page titles it, instead of `📍 MOUNT INDEX`.
- **Home greeting never names the demo climber** (owner's note 2026-10-24): with a backend, a frame with no account greets "Good morning" with no name. A tab that once used *View as guest* and later signed in fell back to guest Home whenever an expired token's refresh failed on open, and greeted with the seed climber's name ("Nathan") until the session landed ("Nate").
- **Alerts are the account's, from the database** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): a guest's Home Alerts and bell no longer show the seed demo's alerts, requests, unread messages or crews — the dropdown says *Sign in to get alerts*; signed in, only alerts derived from the account's rows show (plus *Finish setting up*), so the canned *Climbed recently?* nag and the session-only echoes of your own actions are gone. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Hour by hour: no how-to-read captions**: the crag hour-by-hour drops its "At 2 PM / During 1–2 PM / Drag along a chart…" box and the alpine one its "Key times for this day… Tap an hour, or step with ‹ ›" line (the wind caption the owner quoted had already gone in #2425). See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Alpine Conditions: Day by day, and a day step on every section** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): alpine, mountaineering, scrambling, ice and mixed routes get a *Day by day* list like crag routes have (flags, high / low, freezing level, gusts, new snow and the start for each of the seven days; tap one to open it), and the flags card and Sun and shade now carry the same ‹ › day step as the other sections, so the day never has to be picked from the small tiles at the top. `lib/AlpineConditionsCard.jsx` (`DayByDay`), `check:alpine-conditions`.
- **Day by day reads as a table** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): on the crag Conditions tab each day is a full-width row with its date, ruled off from the next by a visible border, and the selected day has a green edge. The same visible rule now divides the days of *My itinerary*, a trip report's itinerary and a crew card's plans; they had used `C.borderLight`, about 1.1:1 against the card behind it.
- **Conditions score: tracks under the hour tiles, with a key** (owner's note, 2026-11-14: "this part looks weird"): the clock, the sun mark and the rain mark used to share one cell per hour, and the clock prints only every third hour, so the marks zig-zagged and a row of grey squares (shade) had no key nearby. Now the clock, a yellow *sun on the wall* bar and blue *rain* dots are separate aligned tracks, shade is no bar, and one key line names the best-3 bar, sun and rain (only those drawn). → [conditions](docs/features/conditions.md)
- **Conditions score: "Pick an hour", equal tiles** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): the crag hour strip was always the overall score at each daylight hour, but "Score by hour" read as a separate score; it is now *Pick an hour* and the big number says "Overall score at 3 PM". Tiles share one clock axis across the week, so they are one size (an evening's last hour no longer fills the card) and the best 3 hours are an underline, not a taller tile. → [conditions](docs/features/conditions.md)
- **Hour by hour charts no longer feel sticky left and right** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): a plain sideways drag on a Conditions chart scrubs straight away (it used to need a 350 ms hold, so a quick drag did nothing), and the cursor follows the finger instead of jumping hour to hour 120 ms behind it. The boxes above still read whole hours; a mostly-vertical swipe still scrolls the page. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **ROUTE STATS: climbers in the past 12 months** (owner note, 2026-10-24, 0371): the *Sends* and *Logged climbs* tiles now say how many of their climbers did it in the trailing 12 months, beside the lifetime count. The note's other asks were already true — DIFFICULTY BREAKDOWN names its raters and each axis's "N climbers rated", the header stars "N votes", trip reports "N reports from M climbers · K in the last 45 days" (#2403, #2645).
- **Polish round 3** (owner, 2026-10-25): **no emoji anywhere in the app** — every icon an `ActionIcon` or none, reactions are icon + word chips, route tags / risk levels / challenge lists carry icon names, `Lbl` drops an emoji it has no icon for; new in-build guard `check:no-emoji`. The last typed row arrows and ▸/▾ toggles became `Chevron`; visible Title Case went sentence case ("Full trip time", "Quick match", "Open messages", "Digital float plan"…); outlier solid buttons took `BTN_SM`/`BTN_LG` geometry. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Polish round 4** (owner, 2026-10-25): ~55 bare "Loading…" lines became `LoadingRows` placeholder rows; ~27 empty lists became one `EmptyState` (icon + the same sentence); card corners standardised at 12; new palette tokens `C.onSolid` (white text on solid fills) and `C.scrim`/`C.scrimStrong` (pop-up backdrops). Words unchanged. → [app shell](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
