- **Polish round 2** (owner, 2026-10-24): the route page's first jump pill no longer reads "ROUTE FACTSHigh" (`CardHead` gives the heading role to its title, not its confidence chip); one quiet outlined Edit button with a pencil on every route, area and Profile section (`editBtnStyle` in `lib/areaChrome.jsx`); area rows show grey counts with commas and a chevron, and the area page's buttons are Route finder over one row of View map · My objectives · All areas; area search names each route's crag; the rating cell leaves the route header until someone votes; the résumé card wears the header glow; headers are sentence case; Settings rows carry icons; the last ▸/▾ open/close glyphs are `Chevron`, and trailing row chevrons are grey; Crews' *Climb again with a past crew* is a section heading.
- **Your conditions: the daylight window is one two-handle range with hour marks under it** (owner's note 2026-10-24): the two separate "Start no earlier than" / "Finish by" sliders, which had no times under them, are now one `RangeSlider` like temperature and wind, with the scale marked every third hour (12a, 3a, 6a … 9p, 12a). `RangeSlider` gained optional `ticks`/`tickFmt`/`ends` props; nothing else changes and the stored `window` is the same.
- **Past crews: Archive and Clear are saved to your account** (0370, owner's note 2026-10-24): archiving a past crew (it moves to *Archived*, where *Restore* brings it back) or clearing it (swipe or *Clear*, with *Undo*) used to last only until a reload. Each choice is now one `crew_past_state` row that only you can read, so it holds across reloads and devices and the rest of the crew never sees it. The toast waits for the save; a refused save puts the crew back. Example crews (signed out) still say "on this device until you reload".
- **Custom lists: the dropdown chevron stays top-right when a list is opened** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): it had slid back to the middle of the header once an opened description made it taller — #2396's fix was lost when the ▸/▾ glyph became `<Chevron/>`.
- **Thin DESCENT and APPROACH text thickened on Washington alpine routes** (owner's note, 2026-10-24; data only, no code): every WA alpine/mountaineering/scrambling route whose descent read under 150 characters, or whose approach did (with no approach variants), was researched. 54 routes gained text (48 descents, 22 approaches) through `enrich:apply`, every entry source-checked for copied runs and verified on re-read. Thin WA descents went 214 → 166 and thin approaches 154 → 132. The rest were left blank rather than invented: no reachable source names the line, or the row is a duplicate or misfiled. That list, the batch files and the rollbacks are in `audits/thin-descent-approach/`.
- **Profile is back on the bottom bar** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): the right-most tab, after Ranks, as Strava places its "You" tab and AllTrails its Profile tab; the Menu's "You" card still opens it. Its attention badge stays on the Menu avatar, where the queues it counts live.
- **Editing pitches, anchors and the rack** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): the pitch-by-pitch editor lists every pitch the page shows, in its order, with a row for each pitch the route lists but nobody has described; ROUTE BREAKDOWN says when anchors change by pitch (gear, tree, bolted…); the RACK pencil no longer copies quickdraws, bolts and rope into the rack text, which printed them twice. → [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md)
- **Safety tab: SEASONAL HAZARDS and AVALANCHE FORECAST have an Edit pencil; Finish setting up asks for an emergency contact** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): both cards open *Add or fix info* at the Seasonal hazards field (the avalanche card's zone line is that field's *Avalanche forecast zone* row); an empty SEASONAL HAZARDS stays as an *Add seasonal hazards* placeholder off the crag disciplines. EMERGENCY & RESCUE already had its pencil (#2485). Home's *Finish setting up* now also lists *Add an emergency contact*, which opens the profile editor on that field. See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Waypoint colours on the route map and its legend are easy to tell apart** (owner, 2026-10-24): six of the twelve waypoint types had colours too close to tell apart (Campsite vs Approach, Trailhead vs Base, Summit vs Crag…). Topout, Base, Crag, Pass, Approach and Landmark now differ in lightness as well as hue, so the closest pair is 19.3 apart in CIEDE2000, up from 8.2. A pin's glyph is now drawn in whichever of white or near-black reads best on its fill. `check:wp-styles` fails any pair closer than 18.
- **Route map in 3D, and climbers' tracks** (owner's note, 2026-10-24): a *3D* button on the route map tilts the line, pins and tracks over the terrain; under the map, CLIMBERS' TRACKS lists the GPS recordings attached to reports, sorted Latest or Fastest, filtered by season, and any of them can be drawn on the map in its own colour. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Route finder: distance away, elevation gain, round trip, peak elevation, prominence** (0369, owner 2026-10-24): the Filters sheet starts with *Distance away* (your location or a town/zip, a radius, *Nearest first*), and adds gain and round-trip ranges and peak elevation / prominence minimums, read exactly as the route page reads them. See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Someone logged one of your objectives: the bell says so** (owner, 2026-10-24): when another climber logs a climb (or reports conditions) on a route in your objectives within the last 30 days, the bell and Home's Alerts show it. A report you can read names the climber and opens it; one they kept to friends or to themselves arrives as *Someone climbed …* with no name and opens the route's Reports, where its conditions already count (0368, the Mountain Project private tick). Settings › Notifications has a new *Your objectives* switch; the trip-report visibility setting says who is named. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **A popup's ✕ and ← Back stay on screen while it scrolls** (owner, 2026-10-24): every popup's header row is pinned to the top as its content scrolls under it (`pinDismiss` in `lib/dialogA11y.js`, one rule for all ~60). Also fixed: the sign-in sheet's ✕ hid under the app header, and Log a climb could scroll off whole. `check:pinned-dismiss` walks every popup. See [app-shell-and-layout.md](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **Adding a climb vs logging one, reviewed** (0367): whoever adds a climb or an area now gets a notification when it is approved (opening its page, where they can log it) or turned down (with the reason); *Your contributions* shows added climbs as *Route added* and opens them once live; notification links to a climb open even when it is not loaded; the add-a-climb success screen no longer claims a pending proposal is visible to others. See [route quality control](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Objectives: Select, share and remove several at once; a shared link survives the sign-in screen** (owner, 2026-10-24): Logbook › Objectives and every custom list have a *Select* button: tick climbs, then *Share N climbs* (the list share sheet, each climb with its own link) or *Remove N climbs* (asks first). The objectives ✕, Remove and *Add climbs* now write the `objectives` table; before this a removed objective came back on reload. The sort reads *Date added, newest first* and each objective shows *Added <date>*. A `?route=`/`?list=`/`?report=` link opened while signed out is kept on the device until the visitor is in, so *View as guest*, a new account, or a Google/Apple/email redirect still opens it. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Researched text is checked against its sources**: `enrich:apply` fetches every page in a batch's `review.sources` and refuses a string that repeats 8+ words of one; `audit:copied-prose` runs the same check on a batch. 74 pitch and rappel notes on 21 routes from the 2026-10-23 pitch wave (#2655) repeated their sources and were reworded, facts unchanged. See `docs/codebase/enrichment-prose.md`.
- **Friends’ recent activity buttons in colour** (owner, 2026-10-24): on Crew › Friends, Kudos is amber, Message blue and Vouch green (a tint until given, solid once given) instead of three grey outlines.
- **Profile: Edit profile and Settings sit under your bio**: they were two small boxes alone in the top-right corner of the profile card, above your photo. They are now a full-width row of two equal buttons under your name and bio, where Instagram and Facebook put the action row, styled like the Share profile / View public profile row below. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Partner cards read in white** (owner, 2026-10-24): on Find partners, a card's facts — level, location, distance, pace, *Usually free*, the bio, the catches/climbs/vouches chips and the *Full Profile & Vouches* button — are white (`C.text`) instead of grey; on a real climber's row the @handle/distance, the match % (now bold) and the pace are white. Only loading and "not enough info" notes stay grey.
- **Every box-shaped button is at least 40px tall**: one `index.html` rule lifts the 25-31px chips and row buttons (skills, belay, disciplines, Accept / Decline / Vouch, Fill in the missing...) to 40px. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Bigger selector boxes**: Ranks' Overall / Near me / Friends / By area row is four full-width 46px boxes; the crag Grade/Stars/A–Z sort and All/Trad/Sport/Multipitch filter, the Groups sort, a group's topic filter and the list status filter grew to a 40–44px touch target with larger type. See `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **White text on blue boxes, app-wide**: every blue-tinted button, chip, badge and selected toggle (`C.blueBg` / `C.blueChip` / `C.blueDim` or a blue wash) used to print its label in the same blue; those labels are now white (`#fff`) — 189 styles across 36 files, including the shared `btnChip` and `onB`. The box keeps its blue fill and edge, so a selected chip still reads as selected. Blue text on a dark (non-blue) background is unchanged.
- **Create a group: sections stand apart**: on the Create a group / Group settings sheet each section now opens 24px below the one above, while its label stays 8px from its field (it was 13 vs 8, so the form read as one run of fields); the About box no longer leaves a stray gap under it (`gfHead` in `ClimbMatch.jsx`).
- **Saved areas are a list**: Logbook › Saved draws saved areas and peaks as rows (name, type and parent area, › to open, ✕ to unsave) like the Saved routes rows, not wrapping pills. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Settings scrolls from anywhere**: on *Your data & account*, a swipe that started on the temperature or wind slider used to be swallowed — and could drag the range (45°–68° became 18°–68° just by scrolling). The sliders now take only a sideways drag; an up-down swipe scrolls the page, as a native slider does (`lib/RangeSlider.jsx`, pinned in `check:conditions-score`).
- **Crew card: Not ready yet sits under the members**: an opened crew card shows *Crew Ready* / *Not ready yet* directly beneath its members list, where it used to sit at the foot of the card below the gear list (`lib/CrewCard.jsx`).
- **Forecast links open at the climb**: every outside-forecast link on Conditions (NWS, Mountain-Forecast, AirNow) now opens at the same point the in-app forecast reads — the crag on a crag route, the summit pin on alpine — where the NWS pair used to read the area coordinate. A Canadian climb gets Environment Canada's point forecast instead of NWS (which only redirected to a national home page); OpenSnow, which cannot open at a location, is gone. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **A variation shows the whole line**: Boving Roofs now links to the Southwest Rib it is climbed on; its Overview lists all 8 pitches to the summit in order (rib 1–3, its own 3, rib 6–7), and the Southwest Rib lists it under *Variations of this route* (`viaPitches`, lib/approaches.js). Both pages now say the South Arete's 3 rappels land at the foot of the South Arete, a walk from the start. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Route header: the season reads as a short window, one spelling**: the strip under a climb's name (`5,991 ft · 16p · Jul-Sep`) no longer ends in a cut-off sentence or shows a window the route says to avoid; 14 routes' `season` rewritten to the window they describe, and every window is spelled one way (`Mid Jul-Sep`, not `Mid-July to September`). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Area map: what you tap is listed under it**: tapping a blue circle (or a single pin) on the Climbs map lists those areas, with their climb counts, below the map — in full screen too; a circle still zooms. Full screen's top row now clears the phone's notch and has *← Back*, and its exit is an opaque ✕ on the map where ⤢ was. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **The sign-in screen's look across the app** (owner, 2026-10-24): every screen header has the sign-in's blue top glow and a bigger title, and its line under the title is cut to one short line; Home's banner shows the mark beside ClimbMatch on the sign-in background, and the Menu opens under the mark and wordmark. Also: the "coming soon" Apple/Google buttons are dark outlines instead of grey slabs; the four top-right icons are one plain dark style; ▸/▾ markers are one chevron and the Logbook rows lose their blue boxes; Jump back in's three "find" actions are a short row of their own; Recent friend activity is a heading like the others; Crews' "Invite for…" pickers are quiet; Logbook's sort and filters fold behind *Sort & filter*; Partners' Match % note is a plain line; and the Climbs tab on a phone lists the countries, then the regions, as cards under the two pickers. → [app-shell-and-layout](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)
- **A 5.x route is never called a scramble**: Monte Cristo's route is now *North Col* and shows Alpine, not "Scrambling 5.6". Route badges and the discipline filters lead with the route's own primary (`rDiscs`), a scramble with a 5.x grade is alpine (`catOf`, trigger 0366), and 123 peak routes whose discipline list contradicted their primary were repaired (`audit:mountaineering-rule` now checks both).
- **Adding a route photo shows one box**: on the Photos tab, *+ Add photo* opens a sheet whose only box is *Choose from camera or library*; the *Describe this photo (optional)* text box that appeared under each picked photo is gone, and picked photos show as a row of thumbnails above *Add N photos*.
- **Notifications: nothing covers a climber's photo**: the small crew/shield badge pinned on the corner of the avatar in the bell's rows is gone. The sentence beside the photo already says what happened. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Saved headings sit above their boxes**: on Logbook › Saved, *My itineraries* and the other four section headings now sit above their box, like the Objectives tab's headings, instead of inside it (owner note 2026-10-24).
- **Partners: Only climbers of my gender**: a switch in partner search, offered once you have set your gender, shows only climbers who stated the same one; anyone who has not is left out, and a failed check shows nobody rather than everyone. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Your own topo can be fixed or taken down**: in the enlarged topo, a line you drew offers *Edit your line* (the editor opens with it, `updateTopoLine`) and *Delete line*; a photo you posted offers *Delete this photo* unless other climbers have drawn on it (`deleteTopoPhoto`, row first, file after). Closing the editor with unsaved points — Cancel, ✕ or a tap on the backdrop — now asks *Discard this drawing?* instead of throwing the work away. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **New custom list is two short steps**: name it (and an optional description), then add climbs, instead of one long sheet that stacked the form on top of the whole country → state → crag browser under three heading styles. *Create without climbs* makes an empty list; the list is still saved in one write.
- **Route stars say how many voted, and only light when they save**: the five stars over the route photo now carry the vote count beside them ("N votes"), the same count as the ★ cell under it ("★ N votes", was "★ N rated"). Signed out, tapping a star used to light it while saving nothing, so the ★ cell never changed; it now asks you to sign in. Signed in, the ★ cell and both counts update when the rating saves. No minimum-votes filter (Mountain Project has none). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Dropdowns stand apart from the box above**: ACCESS & REGULATIONS (Plan tab, or a crag's Overview), ACTIVE FIRE and FLOAT PLAN (Safety) sit 24px from their neighbours (`FOLD_GAP`, twice the 12px card gap) instead of hugging the card above.
- **Forecast caveats trimmed**: the crag Conditions card no longer ends with *"A forecast for the crag, not a promise about the rock…"*, and the alpine hour-by-hour footer drops *"Forecasts from different services can differ by more at a summit"* (its measured station-error sentence stays). The long *Elevation-aware…* caption under the forecast went in #2425. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Home has ONE Alerts button**: *Unfinished business* is gone; its requests, invites, unread chats, crew days and climbs to confirm now sit at the top of the Alerts dropdown, above the notifications, with nothing listed twice. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Every popup is a page on a phone**: the ~20 popups that were still a rounded card floating over the dimmed app (Settings, Feedback, Privacy, About us, a climber's profile, Log a climb, a trip report, Friends, Notifications and the rest) now fill the screen like the bottom sheets already did, scroll inside themselves, and keep their ✕ / ← Back at the top; only the delete-account confirm and the photo viewers keep their own shape ([app shell](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md)).
- **No Fire map on a country or state page**: the red *Fire map* button is no longer drawn on the header of a country or state page — the first screen after picking a country and state on the Climbs tab; it starts at the range, as the Save button does, and still opens from every range, peak, crag and wall page and a route's Safety tab (`lib/DbAreaBrowser.jsx`, `AreaPage`).
- **Feedback and Rate app actually work** (0365): Feedback sends your note to the ClimbMatch team (it only copied it before); Rate app records your stars and an optional comment (it recorded nothing and thanked you anyway). The owner reads both in Menu › Review › *Feedback & ratings*, with the average rating. When ClimbMatch gets an App Store or Google Play listing, setting `VITE_APP_STORE_ID` / `VITE_PLAY_STORE_ID` adds a *Rate us on …* button for that device, shown to everyone, never only to happy raters (store rules). About us shows the real version and Send feedback / Rate buttons. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Safety tab reordered**: the forecast opens it on every route, then active fires (now a dropdown), known hazards, cell coverage, emergency & rescue, and the float plan last (also a dropdown) — see `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Safety reports, Appeals and Reported photos queues removed**: the three admin tiles in Menu › Review, the screens they opened and Home's admin 24-hour safety banner are gone (owner: not needed). Climbers can still report, block and appeal; those rows land in `user_reports` / `content_reports` / `moderation_appeals` and are read in the Supabase dashboard. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Requests reads top to bottom**: everything waiting on you — including group invites and group join requests, which used to sit under *Waiting on others* — now comes before the requests you sent; friend requests are shown open (no *Tap to review* fold) with when each was sent; every Accept is the same green button. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Every ← Back and ✕ has a blue edge**: the close ✕, the ← Back (in popups and in-page, over photos too) and the remove ✕ on tags, days, photos and list rows now carry the same blue ring as the résumé's Edit button, in place of the faint white one. One token in `lib/popupChrome.js`; the 18px ✕ inside a chip keeps its white ring.
- **No "Continue as a demo climber" on sign-in**: signing out of any build with a backend (not only the live site) lands on the real sign-in screen with *View as guest*; the old demo screen, now reached only by a build with no Supabase, offers *View as guest* too instead of a sample account. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Kautz Glacier (Rainier): its two ice pitches, its map and its camps are right**: ROUTE BREAKDOWN counted the Rock Step and only the UPPER ice step as roped pitches, and drew the lower ice step as a walk. Now the two pitches are the two ice steps (AI2, then the AI3 crux), and the Rock Step, which is rappelled, is a leg. Two pins had no coordinate and the summit was 130 m off. The map now carries 11 pins at recorded GPS positions checked against USGS ground height (Nisqually crossing, Wilson Gully, Lower and Upper Castle, Camp Hazard, Rock Step, the base and top of the ice chute, Columbia Crest), with one-way trail distances and a recorded ascent track. CAMPING & BIVY listed Camp Hazard before the Castle camps, at "7 mi". It now lists the camps on this line in walking order: Wilson Moraine, Lower Castle, Upper Castle, Camp Hazard, Rock Step bivy. The Comet Falls way in lists only the camps above where it joins (data only — `scripts/oneoff/kautz-glacier-pins-pitches-camps-2026-10-24.mjs`).
- **Mount Robson's route maps match the other alpine maps**: its seven routes stored no waypoints, so ROUTE TRACK drew one hollow peak marker. Each now has the Berg Lake Trailhead, the campgrounds it goes through (Kinney Lake, Whitehorn, Berg Lake for the north-side routes; the Ralph Forster Hut, uncoordinated, for the South Face) and the summit, plus `approach_logistics` for the Directions button; the area is filed as a peak, not a crag, at 12,972 ft (data only — `scripts/oneoff/robson-waypoints-2026-10-24.mjs`).
- **Climate & season moved to the Conditions tab**: the CLIMATE & SEASON box (the typical weather, spring to winter, and the forecast zone) and its *No season guidance* note left the Plan tab (and a crag route's Overview) for the Conditions tab, after the forecast, beside ASPECT & SUN and SEASONAL GUIDANCE. A discipline with no Conditions tab (aid) keeps it where it was. `check:bare` holds it: once, on Conditions.
- **Each rappel is a dropdown**: under RAPPELS a station shows its number, length and anchor (and a red hazard count when it has any); its station, hazards, pull and notes open with *▸ More*, the way a camp opens under CAMPING & BIVY. The first opens expanded (`RappelRow`).
- **Home: Finish setting up no longer flashes on a set-up account**: a climber with nothing left to set up saw the card pop up for a moment on each load, asking them to verify an email they had verified (or save a first objective they had saved), then vanish. The card waited for those reads to land but checked the state the app copies from them a render later; it now checks the rows themselves.
- **Forecast links open the peak's own page**: *Mountain-Forecast.com* under Weather & mountain forecasts opened a Google search; it now lands straight on the nearest peak's forecast (Mount Rainier → Mount Rainier), and *Fire & smoke — AirNow* opens the smoke map centred on the climb. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Comments and group feeds are live** (0326): a comment, reply, edit, delete or reaction another climber makes on a route, area or group post, and a post, edit, pin, delete or reaction in your groups, now appears on your screen within about a second, with no reload. `useComments` and `useGroupFeed` became `useLiveQuery` (`lib/live.js`, Supabase Realtime under the same RLS). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Moderation queues and Blocked climbers moved to the Menu**: the admin Review queues (safety reports, appeals, reported photos, guide applications, route proposals, route edits, flagged details, contributor standing, accessibility reports) left Profile's *Review* sub-tab for a *Review* tile section on the Menu, each tile with its waiting count and opening that one queue full-screen; *Blocked climbers* is always a Menu row. The speed card's unit reads on one line, *ft/hr gain · Trail / Approach*. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Camping & Bivy lists camps in the order you reach them**: the Planner's sites were grouped by use (where most parties sleep / other options / not yet sorted), so a high camp could print above the valley campground you sleep at first. They are now one list from the trailhead outward (trail distance, else height; unplaceable sites last, and the intro says so), each tagged with its use, and each says how far on and how much higher it is than the camp before. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Trip plan day cards: the pack weight sits on the same row as the other stats**: a day with gain, loss, hours, miles and pack weight wrapped the pack pill onto a line of its own on a phone (the row needed ~320px of ~294). The five pills are now tighter and share the row's width, one line down to a 360px phone.
- **Alerts remember what you read, and Unfinished business lists what waits on you**: read and archived alerts are kept on the account (`notification_state`, 0346), so a reload no longer brings back every kudos, catch and group post as new and Clear all sticks; a tap on one Home alert marks it read. *Climbs to confirm* (a partner tagged you on their log) is now an alert and a line of Unfinished business, as are a catch a partner filed that you have to confirm and an invitation to an upcoming event you have not said you are going to; the all-caught-up card counts them. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Finish setting up opens on the field it asks for**: tapping *Add your climbing grades*, *Write a short bio*, *Set your availability*, *Add your home zip code* or *Add a profile photo* on Home used to open the whole profile editor at the top (availability went to the profile page), with nothing saying what to do. Each now opens the editor scrolled to that one section, outlined, under a card that names the task, why it matters and what to tap.
- **Base of the climb: one consensus, and it moves the route's Base pin** (0363): the on-site check-ins are clustered by the database (`route_base_spots`: the best-supported check-in seeds a 25 m spot, its centre is the members' median), so the map and the card read the same answer. A spot is *confirmed* when at least 3 climbers stood in it and they are most of those who checked in; a confirmed spot becomes the route's own Base pin (placed, or moved when within 200 m of the pin on file; further than that it is held for review), logged in `route_base_pin_changes` with the pin as it was. An unconfirmed spot no longer outranks the recorded pin. The unverified *Start of climb* form (Suggest a fix, and the log form's *Report start location*) and its START OF CLIMB card are gone: a map tap from home was a second, competing consensus for the same fact. The check-in now recognises all 11 spellings of a Base waypoint.
- **Crag map: the orange crag pin is easy to see**: the crag's own pin (and any derived trailhead) was a dashed ring around a 13% tint that disappeared into rock and forest; it is now an opaque dark disc with a bold dashed ring and a white halo, slightly larger. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Fire map: readable weather alerts, full screen, legend, satellite heat**: *Full text →* on a Red Flag Warning / Fire Weather Watch opened api.weather.gov's raw JSON (a 404 once expired); it now opens the alert's text in the app with a link to weather.gov's page for the area. A ⤢ map-only view, the area map's 📍 Me button, a legend under the map (contained = grey), and a Heat layer of satellite detections for where a fire is burning now — the dot is only where it started (`lib/FireMap.jsx`, `lib/fire.js`).
- **Profile header: disciplines get their own row**: your discipline pills (Sport, Trad, Scrambling…) sit in one full-width row under your photo and name, above the bio and photos, instead of wrapping into ragged rows beside the photo. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **A topo photo stays on its own route (0364)**: a topo shows only on the route page it was posted from (`topos.route_id`, `useRouteTopos`), not on every route of the same peak or crag. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Sharing a climb or a list: friends first, then text, email or a link**: both share sheets list your friends, and *Send* puts a chat in their Messages whose link opens the climb or list in place (it used to send the name only). *Send another way* offers Text, Email, Copy link and More…; the link opens for anyone, and the sign-in screen tells a visitor a climb was shared. Save as PDF is on phones too and opens in a new tab with *← Back to ClimbMatch*. Links start from `appLink`, which takes `VITE_PUBLIC_URL` once the website has its own address. See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Find partners and crews by your saved areas**: Find partners has a *Saved areas* mode — one tap searches every crag, canyon or range you saved (or one, from its chip) for the climbers listed there; Join a crew's *My climbs & areas* now also shows sessions at areas you saved. Your saved list stays private: only climbers who listed themselves are shown. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Topos: labelled variations, finer dots, clearer markers, photo captions (0362)**: a variation is a dashed yellow line with a tag (V, V1, V2…) on the photo and its own caption in the key (`topo_lines.variant_labels`); the editor's line dots are 7px with a 28px grab area; new *Bolt* (×) and *Crux* (⚡) markers, and *Hazard* is a warning triangle; whoever posts a topo photo can write a caption with no length limit (`topos.caption`), which everyone reads under the enlarged topo and under its thumbnail. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **No PROTECTION section; RACK says it once**: the Plan tab on alpine, mountaineering, ice, mixed and scrambling routes no longer shows a PROTECTION card that repeated the RACK card's cams and rack in one line. The bolt count it alone carried is now a RACK line ("8 bolts — bring 8+ quickdraws"). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Step to the next problem on the same boulder**: a boulder problem's page shows **ON THIS BOULDER** where a wall climb shows NEXT DOOR: *← Previous* / *Next →* open the other problems and variations on that boulder, with "Problem n of N on <boulder>", in Mountain Project's order where stored. An area named as a group (a sector, a field) reads *In this area* instead. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Home's Alerts swipe too**: on a phone, each row of Home's Alerts list now swipes right to archive and left to delete, as the bell's Notifications panel already did; it still shows the newest 8, with *View all N alerts* opening the full, scrolling panel and its *Archive all*. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Aspect & sun and Seasonal guidance moved to the Conditions tab**: on every route that has a Conditions tab (sport, trad, top-rope, bouldering, alpine, mountaineering, scrambling, ice, mixed) they sit right under the score or flags card, above the forecasts; they left Overview (crags) and Plan (alpine). Crowds stays where it was. See [route page](docs/features/route-page.md) and [conditions](docs/features/conditions.md).
- **Route page: the sub-tab bar shows that it scrolls**: while Partners or Photos is hidden past the edge of a phone, that edge fades and carries a › button (‹ at the other end once scrolled), so a climber can find the hidden tabs.
- **Popups fill the screen on a phone**: the 27 popups that slid up from the bottom (forms, lists, share screens, Quick log, Filter routes, vouches, group and event forms) now open full screen with the ✕ at the top; a post's or comment's ⋯ menu stays at the bottom. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Option boxes stay on one row**: Edit profile's four *Experience level* boxes (Beginner · Intermediate · Advanced · Expert) used to wrap one box onto a second line on a phone; they now share the row like a segmented control. The same fix holds the four *When you're free to climb* boxes (the Menu card, the Profile tab, a climber's profile; a long label breaks onto two lines inside its box) and add-a-climb's four *Time commitment* boxes on one row from 320px up. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Loading screen, a branded sign-in screen and a stepped first-run setup**: a branded splash while the app loads, the sign-in screen full screen under the app mark and wordmark, and setup asks for a photo (Google photo offered), home area (*Use my current location*), climbing, gender (optional, 0359) and alerts, one step at a time. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md` and `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **The area map's full screen is a real full screen, with a Me button and zoom out of the layer row's way**: ⤢ used to stretch the map in the page (under the bottom bar, the ✕ jumping, the height animated); it now opens over the whole app like the route map, keeps the same map, and closes with ✕ or Escape. **📍 Me** sits on the map (it was a full-width button that full screen hid), and + / − moved bottom-left. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Log a belay catch fits the screen, finds any climber, and counts only when both confirm** (0361): the sheet is portalled and capped at the visible screen with *Log catch* pinned at its foot (on a phone it ran under the browser bars, so you could not pick who you caught); it finds any climber with a profile, not only friends and past crews; and a catch now counts on the Belay board only once BOTH climbers have recorded it, so nobody can inflate another's record. Profile's At a glance, Availability and Certifications & Skills each get a ✎ Edit that opens the editor at that part. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Climbers can add areas; the area search stays put (0360)**: under an area's list and under the route finder's results, *Don’t see a climb or area here?* offers Add a climb and Add an area. A new area is a `new_area` proposal the owner approves in Profile › Review; the sheet lists existing places with that name first. The area page's Areas | Routes search pins under the top bar while you scroll its list. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **The app opens on the sign-in screen, with View as guest**: a visitor without a session meets sign-in first; *View as guest* goes straight into the app. *Create account* asks for the email and password twice, and *Sign in with Apple* and *Continue with Google* are on the screen (they say "coming soon" until each provider is switched on in Supabase). See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Onboarding asks your ice and mixed grade**: tapping Ice, Mixed or Aid under *What do you do?* now adds a grade picker for it beside Sport, Trad and Boulder, saved to the profile like the editor's. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Free to climb: mornings and evenings, weekdays and weekends**: the availability chips on the Menu card, the Profile tab and the profile editor offered Weekends / Weekday AM / Weekday PM / Flexible; they now offer Weekday mornings, Weekday evenings, Weekend mornings and Weekend evenings, saved to the week grid, and a climber's profile shows the same four. Partner search uses the same words. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **New build guard `check:no-module-scope-core-reads`** (#2682): after #2678 took the live site down (a component core imports back read `SKILLS` at module scope, so the bundle threw before React mounted; hotfix #2679), the build now replays ESM evaluation order and fails on any module-scope read of an export that does not exist yet. Notes: `docs/guards/static-code.md`.
- **Past crews: popups open on top of the list**: *Climb with them again* (at an area, or a new climb) opened the Start a crew sheet or the new crew BEHIND the full Past crews list, so it seemed to do nothing until Back; the list now closes first. The Stay in touch *+ Add* sheet is portalled to the page body so it always sits above the list. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`. Found by the same sweep of every overlay: in a climber's résumé, the *All disciplines ▾* grade-pyramid menu could not be closed by tapping outside it (its tap-catcher lay under the résumé); it now closes on any outside tap.
- **Route map: one card per tap**: tapping a waypoint where pins overlap used to stack every nearby pin's full card in one popup, which read as two overlapping boxes. The card now shows the tapped pin, with an *Also here* row of buttons that switch the same card to a neighbouring pin. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **A partner's numbers agree with what they open**: the example partner card's *N climbs* chip counts the climbs its résumé lists, and a catch record with 29 catches no longer says "No catches logged yet". See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **A pinned state's page loads as fast as an unpinned one**: opening Washington (from Saved areas or the Climbs tab) on a device that had it downloaded read all 14,270 Washington climbs out of browser storage to build Suggested climbs, while the database answers in ~0.4 s. The Route finder's list and count over more than 2,000 climbs now ask the database first, with the download as the no-signal fallback; a crag inside a pinned state reads only its own climbs from the device instead of the whole state's. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Downloading a state works again when the nightly file is missing or old**: the live download read routes through a join on the state's path that hit the database's 3 s timeout on every attempt (Delaware's 76 routes and Washington's 14,270 alike), so a pin failed with "Download failed" whenever the nightly file was over 3 days old. It now reads routes by area id (Washington in ~100 s, all 14,270). The nightly builder reads the same way, and one state it cannot read is left out of that night's files instead of failing all 61 (the 10-15 and 10-22 nights failed that way). Downloads persist across sessions in IndexedDB, unchanged.
- **A "Class 3-4" route shows ONE grade, its hardest, plus a notice**: headlines, list rows, the Crux grade tile and the Composite Grade chips no longer print a class range; they show the top ("Class 4", the grade search and filters already use) and the route page adds *Graded Class 4 for its hardest moves; easier sections down to Class 3* (133 routes carry one). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Accepting the Terms closes the Terms page**: after *Read Terms* (or *Read Privacy*) on the policy-update notice, *I accept* used to leave that document open on screen. Accepting now closes it and the notice at once and puts you back where you were. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Desktop: the rail's Alerts card starts collapsed**: it shows only *Alerts · N new* until you click it; then it lists the unread alerts and *See all alerts ›*. Home's Alerts row on a phone was already closed by default. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Notifications: swipe right to archive, swipe left to delete; ← Back top left**: a notification swiped right goes under Archived (restorable); swiped left it is deleted, gone from Archived too, and a stored notice is deleted from the database. Archived rows swipe left to delete. ← Back replaces the ✕ at the top left, and *Clear all* is now *Archive all* with the right edge to itself. Restore now really brings back a stored notice (it cleared only the session list, so the notice stayed archived). See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Approach distance is precise about WHICH distance**: add-a-climb asked a bare "Distance", so nobody knew whether to type one way or the round trip (the page doubles it). It now asks *Approach, one way* (or *Total distance* for a loop / point to point) with a hint, and SuggestFix, Compare and the way-in editor say the same; a crag climb's Approach box asks for the distance and minutes ("0.4 mi, 15 min") instead of "roughly how long". The route header strip showed the one-way figure as "Dist" above a tile reading twice that as "Round trip"; it now shows the tile's number and label. Metric distances printed unrounded ("7.564 km"); now one decimal, two under 1.
- **Logging a climb asks where you camped (0358)**: on a route you might sleep on, the log form's *Getting there* asks *Nights out* (day trip, 1–3, or a number) and *Where did you camp?*, picked from the route's own CAMPING & BIVY list in the order slept in, or typed when it is somewhere else. The trip report shows it, and it feeds back: each camp on the Planner says how many trip reports slept there and when last, and camps reports name that the list lacks are offered to the next climber to add. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md` and `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Manage areas always offers Canada**: the United States · Canada choice and all 13 provinces and territories are listed from the moment the screen opens, offline included; Canada used to appear only after the catalog read landed (8-28s), so the screen read as US-only. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Route page: BETA leads to the climb, and every ROUTE BREAKDOWN box opens**: the BETA card ends in a *Follow the climb section by section* button that opens Plan at CLIMBING ROUTE, or at ROUTE BREAKDOWN when there is none. A travel leg now opens like a pitch, with an edit button, the tiles that hold a value and comments. *Edit this pitch* now lands on the right row on a route that mixes pitches and travel legs. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Ranks: tap a number to see the climbs behind it (0357)**: on every board counted from logs, a ranking row's value opens *How N points add up* — each counted climb with its grade, tick, date, backing (×1 / ×0.75 / ×0.5) and what it scored, summed by `rank_breakdown()` with the same SQL as `leaderboard()`. Another climber's logs you can't read are counted on one "kept private" line, never named. See `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **Pitch-by-pitch and rappel detail on 25 more climbs** (data only): researched ROUTE BREAKDOWN tables for 17 multipitch classics that had none (Epinephrine, Kor-Ingalls, Cat in the Hat, Irene's Arete, Outer Space…), richer pitch notes on 8 more, and station-by-station rappel tables on 5. Batches, held routes and stored-row findings: `audits/pitch-rappel-detail/2026-10-23-findings.md`.
- **Avatars start loading with the screen, not as they scroll in**: avatars were `loading="lazy"`, and inside the app's scroll pane a lazy image is fetched only once it is on screen, so every avatar appeared after the text around it. They now load eagerly (a thumbnail is 3–9 KB), and the silhouette shows in the circle until the photo arrives. Docs: [profile-and-settings](docs/features/profile-and-settings.md)
- **No emoji in the Calendar**: the car, sun, climber and ℹ️ emoji are gone from the Calendar screen; the notes and the empty state use the app's line icons instead. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Editing a route: camping and bivy are easy to find**: the edit sheet's group now reads *Approach, descent & camping*, the Camping & bivy row lists the camps on file by name instead of "✓ on file", and every box in a camp is captioned (Kind, Elevation, Used for, Water, Capacity, Permit, Notes). A metric climber editing a camp no longer saves its height 3.28× too high. Crag lines that show no camping section no longer offer the field.
- **Your résumé is saved, and other climbers see it (0342)**: the headline, courses and past climbs you add by hand were lost on reload and reached nobody; they are now stored in `climber_resumes` and shown on your résumé to anyone while it is public. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Requests: one place for everything pending; the friend button stands out**: a partner card's *+ Add friend* is filled blue and *✓ Accept friend* filled green (both were a dark chip that blended in), and a sent request reads *Requested · tap to undo* in amber and is taken back by tapping it again. Crew › Requests now also lists climbers asking onto your crews and a **Waiting on others** section of every request you sent (friends, crews, groups), each with Withdraw. The bell leads with a Requests section that includes crew invites and crew join requests, with *See all requests ›*. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **ROUTE STATS on every route page** (0353): page views (one per visitor a day, total and last 30 days), sends and logged climbs with how many climbers, climbers with it on their objectives, the star breakdown, and sends by month, at the foot of Overview. Private logs count without a name. Docs: [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Add a climb: search finds any area, and an existing one is not filed as new**: the area step opens on a search of the whole catalog (no country or state first), with each hit's trail, so Jack Mountain, six levels deep, is one search away. The *Not in the catalog?* box suggests existing matches before treating text as a new crag, and opening the form from an area page no longer files that area as a new crag. Every field has a line of context, shared with the edit sheet (`lib/fieldHints.js`). See `docs/features/route-quality-control.md`.
- **Camping & Bivy: one camp listed once, and help choosing**: a camp recorded both as a researched site and a map pin under two spellings (Wing Lake / Wing Lake camp, Upper Cathedral Lake basin / Upper Cathedral Lake — 22 routes) now lists once, with the pin's trail distance. Each camp can show which night of the trip plan sleeps there and the walk in at the fitness and pack set in the planner.
- **Route page: *Got beta?* sits at the bottom of the tab**: the *Got beta? / Know this route?* box now closes Overview, Plan and Safety, just above Top contributors and the Discussion, instead of sitting partway down Overview. It does not appear on Reports, Conditions, Partners or Photos.
- **Add a climb asks what each discipline's page shows** (0352): approach, location on the wall, bolts, anchor, fixed gear, rock quality, seepage, rope length, split rock/ice/alpine grades and steepest angle, per discipline as the route page's edit sheet takes them; the quickdraw/cam/nut/screw steppers now reach the RACK card (they reached no screen); `check:add-route-fields` §3b keeps the two forms level.
- **Removing a waypoint (or a pitch, rappel station, camp or hazard) always waits for the owner** (0351): two agreeing climbers can no longer strip a route; the edit sheet asks why, and the owner's queue marks the removal in red with the climber's reason. See `docs/features/route-quality-control.md`.
- **Route-page section headings carry the map legend's icon**: CAMPING & BIVY shows the purple ⌂ campsite icon, APPROACH the ⇢ approach icon and GETTING THERE the green ◈ trailhead icon — the same glyph and colour the route map's legend and pins use (`SL`'s `wp` prop, from `WP_STYLE`).
- **Climbing route before the pitches**: on a route's Plan tab CLIMBING ROUTE (the climb section by section) now comes before ROUTE BREAKDOWN (every pitch), joined by a line saying how many pitches follow; it also shows on the 46 alpine routes where it was hidden. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Three more places filed twice, and the duplicate guard's gaps closed (0341)**: Arizona's *Courthouse Butte & Bell Rock*, Pennsylvania's *Devils Den, Gettysburg* (holding Plum Run) and Icicle Creek's *The Fist/Rob's Corral* were second copies beside the real areas; their climbs moved in and the copies are gone. The database's list-name refusal now also matches a spacing variant ("El Dorado" = Eldorado), and `check:area-duplicates` also asks daily about names that list nearby places without brackets. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Badges on profiles, for real milestones only**: a profile opens with its trophy case (the badges that climber earned, top tier of each, newest first; tap one for what it was for and when). Badges are re-cut to mean something: 50+ crag sends, 10+ alpine summits (a logged Summit only), 10+ ice routes, 100+ pitches, 6+ month streaks, 50+ climbs in a year, 10 onsights, 5 disciplines, a first ascent, a developed route, and a finisher badge per curated list. Logging a climb that earns one shows *Badge earned*. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Gear editing: two rope lengths, gear by month, no generic chips**: *Rope length* in Suggest a fix takes up to two lengths ("60m or 70m"); *Other gear* no longer offers insulating layers & shell or navigation/GPS (add-a-climb's hiking list drops Navigation too); a picked gear item can name the months it is needed, e.g. "Crampons (May–Jul)". See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Crew "who is free" grids read real crewmates' availability**: the crew card's typical week and the Safety tab's heatmap showed every real member as never free (their profile was fetched without `availability`/`avail_week`), so they said no slot worked for the crew. Both now read what each member saved on their profile.
- **Menu › Requests: the count is a corner badge**: the tile reads plain *Requests*, and what is waiting on you shows as a red badge in its top-right corner, as on the header's objectives, notifications, calendar and messages icons.
- **Editing a route field shows the whole of what is there**: opening a field in *Add or fix info* now shows *On the app now* with the full stored text (approach, descent, description, beta…), one pitch, rappel or pin per line. It used to show the first 44 characters. See [route-quality-control.md](docs/features/route-quality-control.md).
- **Accessibility audit fixes** (0354): every screen now passes axe-core's WCAG 2.2 A/AA rules, signed in and signed out, and `check:axe` keeps it that way (render-guards.yml); tag text lifted to 4.5:1 contrast, 24px tap targets, every dialog named (`check:dialog-dismiss` section 2), no controls nested in controls, one `<h1>` per screen, main landmarks on the full-screen views. A visitor who is not signed in can report an accessibility problem from the sign-in screen; the owner sees reports in Profile › Review and the badge; the topo line can be drawn from the keyboard. Manual screen-reader checklist and log: `docs/accessibility-testing.md`. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Accessibility problems can actually be reported**: Settings › Legal & help › Accessibility now ends in a *Report an accessibility problem* form that saves to `accessibility_reports` (0343). The statement used to send people to the Feedback button, which sends nothing. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Save a route map offline, again**: *⤓ Save map offline* under a route's map saves the map from the trailhead to the climb (USGS tiles) plus the page itself, so both open with no signal; *Manage areas › Saved route maps* lists them to open or remove. It had been unreachable since the trip pack was removed (#2368). See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Groups Step 6** One palette on the event page and the group invite card: neutral date tile in the climber’s date format, no 📍, one accent on RSVP and capacity (red stays only on Cancel event). “Who’s going” names show to the event’s host only; everyone else sees the count.

- **Forecasts swing less, and are never saved offline**: every Open-Meteo forecast (Safety tab panel, Conditions cards, Best day) now leads with the NWS National Blend of Models instead of GFS alone past day 2 (measured: a 10 F+ change between runs in 0.7% of hours vs 12%). The dead per-route forecast snapshot is removed. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Desktop: nothing stretches across the page**: the page column is 640px (was 720), and action buttons — *Log a past climb*, *Edit profile*, *Climbed it? Log the climb*, *Add trip report*… — are as wide as their label instead of the whole column; list rows still run edge to edge, phones are unchanged. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Float plans ⇄ crews**: a crew's place, partners, meet-up and day now fill a crew float plan's empty fields; a plan started from a route's Safety tab (or with no crew) can be moved onto a crew; and a saved crew plan can be sent to the crew chat so everyone in the crew has it. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Log a belay catch: more ways to find the climb, a bigger ✕**: the climb search shows each route's crag and grade (30 results, not 8), finds a peak or crag and opens its climbs, browses by area, and suggests climbs done with that partner, your recent climbs and recently viewed ones. The ✕ (now 48px app-wide) stays pinned while the sheet scrolls. DB routes round-trip (search, save, Recent catches).
- **Desktop: chats in the right rail**: on wide screens the rail lists your direct messages and crew chats, newest first with unread counts, under Coming up and Alerts; one click opens the conversation. Desktop website only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop: draw an approach**: a route page's *✎ Draw approach* traces the way in on a map, shows its length and terrain gain and loss, and downloads it as GPX (marked hand-drawn); on a crag it can be suggested as the trail through the existing review. Desktop website only. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Desktop: print a route, download your logbook**: a route page has *⎙ Print* — a one-sheet printout of approach, pitches, descent, rack, hazards and waypoints — and My Ascents has *⤓ Download as CSV*. Desktop website only. See `docs/features/route-page.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Desktop: the Calendar is a month**: above its lists, a month grid of your crews' agreed climbing days and group events, ‹ › to move and *Today* to come back; each entry opens the crew or the event. Desktop website only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Manage areas lists every state again**: while the state list was still loading (seconds on a slow connection) it fell back to the demo data, so 46 of 50 states read "No climbs in the catalog", greyed out and untappable, and Washington claimed "1 climb". Rows now say *Loading the catalog…* until the real counts land, and a failed read says so. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Profile: Vouches you’ve received**: your own Profile tab now shows what partners said about you (the two newest in full, *See all* for the rest) above the vouches you gave. Before, it showed only a count, and the vouches themselves were reachable only through View public profile. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Certifications and skills, catalogued**: Edit profile suggests 66 certifications as you type (and lists them under *Browse certifications*), across medicine, avalanche, instruction, guiding, rescue, gym and club courses; skills add a Technique group and 10 more (42 in seven groups), shared with the vouch form. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Vouching from a profile opens the vouch sheet over it; larger ✕ everywhere; more vouchable skills**: the vouch sheet drew at z 220, under the profile, the résumé and most overlays, so tapping Vouch closed the profile and whatever was still open showed first. It now draws above the profile, which stays open beneath it. Every popup ✕ and ← Back is 48px (was 44) with a larger glyph (`lib/popupChrome.js`); a profile's ✕ stays pinned while it scrolls, and the résumé's header now actually sticks. The vouch and profile skills list grew from 17 to 32 (rescue, gear, snow and ice, soft catches and more).
- **Friend activity: the profile is its own tap**: on Home's Recent friend activity and Crew › Friends' recent activity, a friend's avatar + name now opens their profile, and the climb line beneath opens the climb; before, the whole row opened the climb.
- **A shared trip report opens the REPORT**: its link (`?report=<id>`) now opens that report over its climb instead of only the climb; who can open it follows the report's visibility, and the share sheet says so. A not-shared or deleted report says so on arrival. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Itineraries reach the Logbook (checked)**: a plan saved on a climb's Plan tab shows in Logbook › Saved › Itineraries and survives a reload, verified with a real account. A plan whose climb is still loading, or failed to load, now says so instead of leaving a blank card.
- **A climber's profile says HOW YOU MATCH**: under COMPATIBILITY WITH YOU, one line per thing the match % scores (grade, mountain objectives, disciplines, listed areas, availability, pace, verified), heaviest first, each marked as counting, partly, not, or not said by both. Built from the same rows the score sums (`compatParts`/`compatWhy`). The profile now scores with the same objectives the partner row does, so the two no longer show different %s. See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Recent condition reports show every report, not just the newest**: each objective's card now gives the newest report in full (author, date, outcome, labelled conditions, all tags, hazards first) and lists the other reports from the last 90 days, each tappable, plus *All trip reports on this climb*, which opens the route's Reports tab. The *Show more* button now counts climbs, which is what it adds. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Crew itinerary: your own plan, and Remove**: *Import from Plan* offers *Your plan* (the one saved on the climb's Plan tab) first, then the climb's suggested plans, plus *Build your own*. The crew card's builder starts from your plan and *Share with the crew* saves it as your plan and shares it in one step (it used to keep a draft on screen until somebody pressed Adopt). The crew's itinerary box has *Remove* beside *Change*. The red *Safety plan* button on a backcountry crew is blue like the card's other secondary buttons.

- **ACCESS & REGULATIONS is a dropdown**: on the route page the card is closed by default to one row (heading + what it holds, e.g. "Permit · Fees · Land manager"); tap to open. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Another climber's résumé is no longer empty**: the example climbers' *View climbing résumé* (and their card's *N climbs* chip) opened a résumé reading "0 climbs"; they now list notable climbs from the seed routes in their disciplines. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **View public profile shows your pace and belay devices**: `meLive` reads `mySpeedFtHr` and the profile's safety fields directly; your own Objectives tab says others see it and no longer marks every row "You both want this!"; the Certifications & Skills card opens Edit profile.
- **KNOWN HAZARDS: a compact ✎ Edit, not a big Suggest a fix**: the Safety tab's hazards card used a full-size red button beside its heading; it now uses the small ✎ Edit every other route-page card header has (same form, same action).
- **Search your friends list**: *See all friends* now always opens with a *Search friends* box (name or @handle) — it used to appear only past four friends — with a clear ✕; while a query is typed the activity feed steps aside so the matches sit under the box. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Profile photo from the avatar; readable dropdowns; the zip says where it puts you**: with no photo, the Profile avatar carries a + and adds one directly. Every `<select>` draws the app's blue chevron (the rule moved to `index.html`; screens App returns early, like Edit profile, drew a black native arrow). Empty rating stars are grey, not border-dark. Edit profile explains the home city is a label and checks a typed zip live (recognised? which state? matches your state?).
- **Area header: a larger Fire map button, centred on the right**: the red *Fire map* pill on an area's header box was a small chip in the bottom-right corner beside the climb count; it is now a 44px-tall button in its own right-hand column, vertically centred against the area's facts (`lib/DbAreaBrowser.jsx`, `AreaPage`).
- **Safety: *Suggest a fix* on KNOWN HAZARDS no longer crashes the route page**: the button passed its click event to the form as the hazard; the fixed-gear chips passed a bare string to a multi field. Both now seed an array (or nothing). See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Profile photos load from cache; no ID verification (0339)**: photos are stored with a one-year cache instead of one hour, so the Profile's avatar and photo strip no longer wait to be resized again on each visit, and a new upload is pre-sized. `verification_records` can no longer hold an identity-document record; email is the only verification.
- **North Cascades: one Cascade River tree, and a guard against bucket copies (0340)**: Mountain Project's *Cascade River Drainage (Eldorado, Forbidden, Johannesburg, Sahale, Torment, etc.)* and its *Boston Basin Peaks (…)* stood beside North Cascades Core, which already holds every one of those peaks. Both are gone; their one climb's boulder field (Hidden Lake Lookout) moved into North Cascades Core. The database now refuses an area NAMED by a list of two or more places the state already files, and `check:area-duplicates` sweeps for one daily. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Logs and trip reports earn contribution points (0338)**: a logged climb 1 (once per route), a trip report 2 (once per route per month), counted only when shared with Everyone, on Ranks › Contributions, TOP CONTRIBUTORS and the ★ badge. See `docs/features/ranks.md`.
- **Menu: Your crews, and shortcuts first**: a *Crews · N* tile joins *Yours* (Groups takes the chat icon so the two differ), and a *Your crews* section lists up to five active crews with their unread counts, each opening that crew. *Your crews* and *Your groups* now sit above the tile groups, as Facebook's Menu leads with your shortcuts.

- **Three more peak lists: Colorado Centennials, Adirondack 46, Sierra Peaks Section (0344, 0345)**: the summits the catalog lacked are added with a standard route (227 peaks), list peaks held only as climbing walls get a peak-bagger's route, and a climb on a peak's face sub-area now ticks the peak. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Climbing lists on Home; a clean Fifty Classics list (0337)**: an expanded list in Logbook › Challenges has *Show on Home*; Home's *Your lists* shows the picked lists' progress (every list still counts every log). The Fifty Classics list follows the book: numbered, the route over its peak and region, no "null" grade. See [logbook-crews-home.md](docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md).
- **Logged climb vs trip report, reviewed**: both are one `climb_logs` row, and the Logbook doc now maps what each kind feeds. A *Conditions only* report no longer pairs two climbs as "Logged the same day" (0336). Your *Log conditions on 5 routes* challenge now counts Conditions-only reports, and it no longer shows another climber a 0 it cannot measure. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Groups Step 5** Events: one event row everywhere (date tile, when · where, going), a tap opens the event; a group’s Events tab shows Upcoming then Past (3, “See all”); the hub’s Events tab puts “You’re going” first. “Form a crew” left the group page.

- **Desktop: full-screen views are pages** (1024px+): Calendar, Messages, Edit profile, a group, an event, Settings' pages, Best day, Terms and the rest open in the page column with the top bar, sidebar and rail still live, and the sidebar leaves them; the bell opens Notifications as a drop-down; Home's Alerts list is phone-only. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Desktop: the Menu fills the screen, site links live in the footer, no Best day in the rail**: ☰ Menu is now a full-window sheet on desktop (it was a 440px drawer), its content centred; About, Help, Feedback, Privacy, Terms and © left the bottom of the left sidebar, since the page footer already holds them; the right rail is Coming up and Alerts only. See [app-shell-and-layout.md](docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md).
- **Route finder: several types, unrated climbs kept, grouped by wall, pick an area first, save from the list**: tick Sport and Trad together; a star minimum no longer hides climbs nobody has rated (it emptied every search); climbs list wall by wall, left to right, under each wall's name; on a whole state you pick an area first (or type a name); the search bar stays at the top; a ☆ on each row saves the climb to My Objectives, your lists or its wall (0334). See [maps-and-area-browser.md](docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md).
- **Route page: the approach picker sits under the sub-tabs**: *How you get there* moved from above Overview · Plan · Reports… to the top of each tab's content, with more room around it. A climb with more than one way in now reads *Choose your approach · N ways in* over full-width radio rows (one marked *Showing*); *Other finishes from this route* is its own card, on Overview only.
- **Trailhead to summit / Summit to trailhead beside GETTING THERE**: the Plan tab now shows the two legs (time, gain, distance) right under the trailhead's drive directions and coordinates, from the same estimate as Full Trip Time. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **The desktop website shell** (1024px+): a sticky top bar (☰ Menu far left, wordmark, a search for every climb and place, alerts, avatar), a sidebar with Groups, Profile and *Your crews*, a right rail at 1280px+ (Coming up, Alerts, Best day — the future ad column), a site footer, the Menu as a left drawer, bottom sheets as centred windows, hover and per-page tab titles. The phone tab bar is now soft and see-through. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Route jump links land below the top bar**: tapping a section pill at the top of a route page used to put that heading under the sticky app bar, so you had to scroll up to read it; it now lands just below the bar. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **The trust score is gone from the database too (0335)**: `compute_trust_score`, `trust_scores_view`, and the trust columns of `partner_signals()` and `leaderboard()` are dropped. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Crews tab: + Create a crew leads the tab, for everyone**: it sat below every crew card and vanished for a climber with no saved climbs; it now opens the tab and offers an area, your saved climbs, or *A climb — find it in Climbs…*. A past crew no longer counts as a climb's crew: its climb can be crewed again, and invites, *Open crew* and the objectives badge reach only an active crew (`activeCrews`).
- **Wall order for a whole state, with the missing climbs added**: `order-from-mp.mjs --state <slug>` orders every MP rock wall left to right, adds the 5.x sport/trad/TR climbs a wall lacks (never one the duplicate checks or trigger 0216 flag), and `--facts` plus `extract-route-details.mjs --adds` give each added climb its length, pitches, GPS, stars and rewritten description, protection, location and first ascent. New York done: 406 walls, 25 climbs added. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **The trust score is gone** (owner decision): no badge, number, ring, Profile trust card, Min trust filter, Most trusted sort, Trusted board or Trust 20+ group policy. Conditions consensus, start pins and topo lines no longer weight reports by reporter trust. Vouches, belay catches and ✓ Verified stay. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Crag routes: no essentials field, BETA always shown**: sport/trad/toprope/aid/bouldering editors stop offering *Route-specific essentials* (it rendered nowhere on a crag page), and an empty crag BETA keeps its heading with *Add beta*. See [route-page.md](docs/features/route-page.md).
- **Route finder Filters work on a phone**: the sheet is now a full-height screen — the ✕ and the Discipline row sit below the notch instead of under the browser bar, Clear all / **Show N routes** are pinned at the bottom with the live count, and the page behind no longer scrolls under it. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Who reads a trip report is a Settings choice only, and every report feeds the conditions**: the log form no longer asks; Settings › Privacy & safety decides, and a new report takes the stored setting server-side. A report kept to friends, a crew or yourself now adds its conditions (date, tags, outcome, times, snow/trail answers) to the route's consensus for everyone, with no name or words (0329, the Mountain Project private-tick model). Help and the Privacy Policy say so. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Another climber's profile: the tab strip hides its scrollbar** (`cm-hscroll`, as your own Profile's tabs already did), so the sideways scrollbar no longer draws over the active tab's blue underline (`FullProfile` in `ClimbMatchCore.jsx`).
- **Menu: Saved & lists, and Edit profile beside View profile**: a *Saved & lists* group (Saved areas, Saved searches · N, Everything saved, Objectives & your lists, Challenges, Climbing lists, which opens the curated lists straight away) replaces the single *Your lists* tile; *Plan & log* adds *Your ascents*; the profile card has *View profile* and *Edit profile* buttons side by side and no longer prints a trust score. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Settings is four pages**: the sheet lists Privacy & safety · Notifications · Your data & account · Legal & help, each opening full screen with ← Back, as Facebook's settings do; every control moved as it was. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Home's "Nothing needs you right now" card**: the all-caught-up card no longer tells a climber who already has objectives to "save one to get started"; they get a plain all-clear, and only a climber with no objectives sees the *Explore climbs* button, which now opens the area list rather than the last route left open.
- **Sport, trad and top-rope show one grade**: the TECHNICAL STATS "Crux grade" tile no longer repeats the Composite Grade panel on sport, trad and top-rope routes; it shows only when a stored crux grade differs from the route's grade. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **One contribution score, rolled up the area tree (0333)**: `contribution_points()` is the one ledger that area Top contributors, the profile badge and Ranks › Contributions all read — points for live work only (new climb 10, photo/topo 5, edit/topo line 2, report 1), each kind once per place, summed over an area and every place inside it. Before, the three disagreed: pending/rejected new climbs counted on area boards, reports counted nothing on Ranks, topos counted nowhere, and Ranks By-area showed worldwide totals. See `docs/features/ranks.md`
- **Wall order from Mountain Project, and the climbs next door**: `scripts/pipeline/order-from-mp.mjs` fills `routes.sort_order` for one area from its MP page (matches, adds MP's missing sport/trad/TR routes in place, anchors ours MP lacks); first run on The Uberfall – left. A crag route shows **NEXT DOOR** (left and right climbs) under LOCATION, and the Route finder sorts **Left → right / Right → left** across every wall (0332). See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **No demo flash on load**: the live site no longer shows the demo climber's Home ("Good morning, Nathan") for a split second before your own; it waits for the stored session first, and a first load on a new device greets you by the name you signed up with. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Alpine Conditions: switch day from any section**: the day strip's sticky pin never held (the document scrolls, not `#appscroll`), so each day section (alerts, hour by hour, snowfall, air) now has its own ‹ › in its heading, stepping the same day for all of them. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **One NEXT DOOR, never on a boulder problem**: the header's *Routes next door* strip (it stepped through a list sorted by name) is gone; NEXT DOOR sits under BASE OF THE CLIMB. The list at the foot of a crag route reads *More at <crag>*, and bouldering counts *problems*, not routes on a peak.
- **WHERE IT STARTS** (where the line starts on its wall, `routes.location`) moved from above Getting there to directly above BASE OF THE CLIMB on crag routes, and shows in full.
- **A crag's climb list can be sorted**: Left → right (default), Right → left, A → Z, Z → A. The two wall orders appear only where `routes.sort_order` is stored (1 route of 314k today; the order will be filled from Mountain Project's area pages, which hold more routes than OpenBeta), and climbs with no position are listed last, by name, with a line saying so. See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Sign out works offline**: when the logout request cannot reach the server, the app still signs this device out instead of saying "Signed out" over a session that came back on reload. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Hour by hour charts scroll freely on a phone**: a swipe across a Conditions chart scrolls the page and no longer changes the boxes above it; tap an hour, or touch and hold then slide, to read it. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Topo editor: tap to mark, drag to look around**: holding and moving a finger on the topo photo pans it (pinch or − / + zooms) instead of drawing; only a tap places a point or pin. The route line and its dots are red; variations are yellow lines on the *Draw line* tab (`topo_lines.variants`, 0330) rather than a pin; a *Grade* marker prints a pitch's grade beside it. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Partners and Crews breathe**: the filter panels on Find partners and Join a crew start closed behind the Filters button, sections and cards get more room, and every crew card on the Crews tab starts collapsed to a summary with View plan. See `docs/features/partners.md` and `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Crew gear has quantities**: each item on a crew's gear list can say how many the crew wants, and each climber bringing it how many they bring (several can share one item; it counts as covered when the numbers add up). The card no longer suggests gear. Migration 0328. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Alpine start never mid-morning on a glacier; the day picker stays in view**: on glacier and alpine-ice routes the Conditions start is never later than one that has the party back off the snow by noon (Easton on a cold day read "leave camp by 7:30 AM"; now about 1:30 AM, with the forecast's later limit and the route's usual start beside it). The day strip sticks to the top while you scroll the hour-by-hour and other sections. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Draw the trail to the crag**: on sport/trad/bouldering routes *Add the trail* opens on a map where a climber taps the path from the parking pin (P) to the crag (C); it goes to review like a recorded GPX and then shows as the walk-in line on the CRAG MAP. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **"Quality" says what it means**: a route's guidebook stars read **Star rating** (now **Reputation**), not "Quality", and the rock report-tag group reads **Rock quality**. See `docs/features/route-page.md`.
- **Partners › By route: Recently viewed is shorter**: it lists the 3 newest climbs, with a "Show N more" toggle for the rest (up to 5). See `docs/features/partners.md`.
- **Groups Discover: explore by STATE, search finds states and About text**: one chip per state or province (home first, then where you listed partner areas), combinable with the search box; "Washington" now finds "Seattle, WA". New groups default to moderator-hosted events. See `docs/features/groups.md`.
- **Rock type reads plainly**: the Rock tile, the Conditions card and the fix form no longer add "(mapped)" to a crag's rock type taken from the bedrock map. See `docs/features/conditions.md`.
- **Climb lists show the pitch count**: every climb row in the Climbs area browser reads e.g. "Trad · 5 pitches" beside its discipline (none on boulders or climbs with no count). See `docs/features/maps-and-area-browser.md`.
- **Crag map is headed CRAG MAP**: on sport/trad/bouldering/aid/top-rope routes the map section (was TRAIL TO THE CRAG, before that ROUTE TRACK) is a location map, where the crag is and where to park, and says it is not a track of the climb. A shared walk-in line is still drawn and described when one exists.
- **Desktop layout, phase 1**: at 1024px and wider the bottom tab bar becomes a fixed left sidebar and the column widens from 520 to 720px, the two centred together. Phones are unchanged. See `docs/features/app-shell-and-layout.md`.
- **Route page foot**: on crag routes, sun, season and crowds now sit above *More on <crag>*, so that list ends the Overview right above Top contributors and the Discussion.
- **Profile photo sticks**: "Change photo" saves the avatar as soon as it uploads, so Cancel or a greyed Save (no username yet, every new account) no longer loses it; the editor says why Save is greyed. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Menu lists your groups**: under *Yours*, a *Your groups* section shows up to five of the groups you are in, newest post first, with each one's new-post count. A tap opens that group, and *See all ›* appears past five. See `docs/features/profile-and-settings.md`.
- **Crag route Overview: LOCATION, GEAR & SAFETY and CONDITIONS are their own sections**, moved out of the top ROUTE FACTS card — Location above Getting there, the other two under the Rack; each missing fact has its own + Add.
- **Route page: PAIRS WELL WITH takes a full section header**: the rule and 30px gap every other route-page section has, so it no longer sits tight under the "Got beta?" box.
- **Quick log**: the route page offers *✓ Quick log* beside *Full log* — pick a style or outcome, the date, an optional rating, save. The rest can be added later from the logbook. A crew quick log no longer writes two `climb_logs` rows. See `docs/features/logbook-crews-home.md`.
- **Route edits: an empty field fills at once, two climbers agreeing change a filled one** (0325): the database approves the edit itself; the edit sheet shows other climbers' waiting suggestions with an Agree button; anything else still waits for the owner. Area edits are still held.
- Route page: the *Brought different gear? Suggest a gear update* box sits directly under the RACK card (alpine GEAR section, before ROPEWORK and GEAR & ESSENTIALS; crag Overview, under its RACK card) instead of at the end of the gear section or the page.
- **Best day on Home again + Menu tiles**: Home shows the Best day to climb tile right under Jump back in (it also stays in the Menu). The Menu's Plan & log adds **Downloads** (scrolls Home to the pinned/downloaded states card, `#pinned-downloads`) and **Hire a guide** (Partners in guides mode).
- Route page: the duplicate *+ Add start location* button on Safety is gone; start of climb is filed from Add or fix info and the log form, shown on Overview.
- **Logbook › Saved filter** Chips All · Routes · Peaks · Areas · Itineraries · Searches show one kind at a time, as Facebook’s Saved does; All is the default.
